# SCRUM-486 deal accounting certification oracle — partial evidence

## SCRUM-929-SUPPLIER-BALANCES

Read-only report and alert projection. `convex/supplierBalances.scrum929.test.ts` exercises registered queries and the alert worker with real documents. The initial red contract commit is `db0dec582`; the full SHA is recorded in each scenario. Harness evidence is not cloud, concurrency, browser or release certification.

| Cell | Literal expected result | Bound check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Statement parity, dispute, recovery | JOD payable 5,500,001 minor; receivable 3,200,000; net -2,300,001; disputed payable 5,500,001 | `each supplier/currency equals the statement to the minor unit, including dispute and recovery` |
| Two currencies, closed rows | JOD 9,500,000 payable and USD 1,234 receivable stay in separate rows; CANCELLED and RECOVERED excluded | `two currencies remain separate; cancelled claims and same-day settled recoveries disappear` |
| Stored identity | Renamed supplier retains linked 9,500,000 minor payable; unlinked 500 JOD excluded as in statement | `renamed suppliers retain id attribution; unlinked legacy rows are outside statement scope` |
| Completeness | 101 payable rows, six suppliers in a five-supplier page, or exhausted byte headroom report partial; incomplete supplier figures absent | The three family-bound, registry-pagination and byte-headroom scenario records |
| Amman day boundary | Overdue payable changes from 0 to 9,500,000 at 21:00 UTC; gross unchanged | `Amman midnight changes only overdue aging from the explicit clock argument` |
| Permissions, tenant, plan | Unauthenticated, foreign-org, foreign-supplier and non-accounting requests refuse; cost permission required | `requires accounting plan and authentication, refuses foreign tenant and supplier`, permission variants in the same file |
| Notification confidentiality | No amount or supplier name for a finance-only recipient; text still says who owes whom | `non-cost recipient receives neither amount nor supplier name; direction remains useful` |

The records under `regression/scenarios/{money,permission}/supplier-*` with `source.runId` `scrum929-supplier-balances` bind these cells. No money is posted or implicitly offset.

## SCRUM-760-DECISION-B-BLOCKED-DOORS

Owner rulings SCRUM-795 c22479 (2026-10-08, Q2 selective containment: do not activate a new retained-credit
payout authority) and SCRUM-795 c23829 (2026-10-28), under SCRUM-760 decision B (2026-10-19).
These are expected-refusal cells for the pilot, not activation certificates.
Every refusal below is a public-mutation call in `convex/pilotBlockedDoors.scrum795.test.ts`, with the shipped
switches and no mocks. Its helper requires the exact code, matching English text and an Arabic dictionary entry,
and identical contents of every application schema table and the scheduler. That proves a coded refusal and that
nothing was committed. It does not prove where the guard sits; the position is read from the cited handler lines
(`docs/pilot/blocked-server-side-doors.md`, "How a block is built").

| Door | Pilot refusal code | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `retainedCreditRefunds.requestRetainedCreditRefund` | `CUSTOMER_CREDIT_PAYOUT_DISABLED` | `requestRetainedCreditRefund refuses the customer-credit payout without writes` |
| `retainedCreditRefunds.respondToRetainedCreditRefund`, APPROVED | `CUSTOMER_CREDIT_PAYOUT_DISABLED` | `respondToRetainedCreditRefund APPROVED refuses the customer-credit payout without writes` |
| `retainedCreditRefunds.executeRetainedCreditRefund` | `CUSTOMER_CREDIT_PAYOUT_DISABLED` | `executeRetainedCreditRefund refuses the customer-credit payout without writes` |
| `collections.registerSaleInvoiceCheque` | `SALE_INVOICE_RECEIPT_DOORS_DISABLED` | `registerSaleInvoiceCheque refuses the sale-invoice receipt without writes` |
| `collections.reserveSaleInvoiceReceipt` | `SALE_INVOICE_RECEIPT_DOORS_DISABLED` | `reserveSaleInvoiceReceipt refuses the sale-invoice receipt without writes` |
| `collections.activateSaleInvoiceReceipt` | `SALE_INVOICE_RECEIPT_DOORS_DISABLED` | `activateSaleInvoiceReceipt refuses the sale-invoice receipt without writes` |

## SCRUM-869-SUPPLIER-SETTLE

Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23881, 2026-10-29: supplier payable payment, consignment margin receipt and supplier cost-recovery receipt are in the pilot. No reversal is indispensable, so every reversal door for the three refuses server-side. Amounts and postings are unchanged by this slice. The ruling digest in `regression/rulings.json` is computed from the owner's verbatim comment text (`refreshRulings.mjs`, commit 52067abd), and every `supplier-*` record cites it.

Hand-computed JOD figures (1 JOD = 1,000 minor units). Control 2400 is credit-normal, so a payment is a net debit; control 1240 is debit-normal, so a receipt is a net credit. Every row is a public-mutation call in `convex/supplierSettlementCertification.test.ts` unless a file is named.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Payable 8,000, pay 3,000 then the remaining 5,000 | PARTIALLY_PAID then PAID; 2400 net +8,000,000; cash net -8,000,000; no unbalanced entry | `8,000 payable: 3,000 then the remaining 5,000 posts Dr 2400 / Cr cash, balanced, status PARTIALLY_PAID then PAID` |
| Payable over-application, finer than 3 decimals, second settlement | 5,000.001 after 3,000 refused ("That would pay"); 0.0005 refused; 0.001 after PAID refused; markPaid after PAID refused; database unchanged | `over-application, fractions and a second settlement are refused with nothing written` |
| Payable bank payment | missing, wrong-currency, foreign-org bank refused; JOD bank posts to the bank account, not cash | `a bank-funded payment needs an active JOD bank of this organization` |
| Payable replay | one journal, one payment, 2400 net +3,000,000 | `a replay under the same key posts once and audits once` |
| Payable permission and tenancy | view-only caller FORBIDDEN on all three doors; other organization refused; database unchanged | `MANAGE_FINANCE is required; another organization sees the payable as not found` |
| Payable closed period | subledger moves, one PENDING queued event, zero journals | `payable payment: a closed period: the subledger moves, the event is QUEUED, and the GL stays empty until the period reopens` |
| Margin 500: 200, 350, 300 | PARTIALLY_PAID remaining 300 (1240 net -200,000); 350 refused ("That would record 550 against 500 owed"); then PAID (1240 net -500,000, cash +500,000) | `500 commission: 200 then 300 posts Dr cash / Cr 1240, balanced, PARTIALLY_PAID then PAID; 350 is refused` |
| Margin bank, replay, permission, tenancy, closed period | same guards as the payable cells | the three `margin receipt:` tests |
| Recovery 200: 80, 130, 120 | 1240 net 120,000 after 80; 130 refused; RECOVERED at 0; one more minor unit refused | `200 cost: 80 then 120 recovers it; 130 is refused; the receivable ends at zero and every entry balances` |
| Recovery bank, replay, permission, tenancy | same guards | the two `recovery receipt:` tests |
| Margin receipt by CHEQUE or CARD (SCRUM-869 review F1: a cheque posts to cheques-in-hand with no `postDatedCheques` custody row, so no deposit or bounce lifecycle) | `SUPPLIER_RECEIPT_METHOD_PILOT_UNSUPPORTED`, EN and AR, refused before the idempotency claim; no table or scheduler change, no journal entry, no cheque row, a replay also refused; CASH, BANK_TRANSFER and an omitted method still record | `convex/supplierSettlementContainment.test.ts`, `consignment margin receipt method`, four tests |
| Supplier payable payment by CHEQUE or CARD (SCRUM-869 follow-up to F1: `markPaid` and `recordPartialPayment` credit the bank immediately for a cheque, with no issued-cheque record and no clearing or bounce lifecycle) | `SUPPLIER_PAYMENT_METHOD_PILOT_UNSUPPORTED`, EN and AR, refused on both doors before the idempotency claim; no table or scheduler change, no journal entry, the payable stays PENDING, a replay also refused, a viewer gets FORBIDDEN; CASH, BANK_TRANSFER and an omitted method still pay (an 8,000 payable paid in cash, bank transfer and omitted-method parts, then settled: accounts payable to suppliers nets 8,000 and cash on hand nets -2,000) | `convex/supplierSettlementContainment.test.ts`, `supplier payable payment method`, four tests |
| Cancelling a sale after a margin receipt (SCRUM-869 round 3 P2: the refusal was an uncoded English string, so an Arabic user saw English) | `SUPPLIER_RECEIVABLE_PAID_CANCEL_REFUSED` through `throwAppError`, same English text, refused before any write: claim stays PARTIALLY_PAID, sale stays COMPLETED; the Arabic dictionary entry is what an Arabic user sees (also `lib/errors.test.ts`) | `convex/supplierRefusalTexts.scrum869.test.ts`, two `margin-claim refusal` tests; scenario `supplier-margin-claim-sale-cancel-refusal-is-coded-en-ar` |
| Receipt reversal (the only reversal door) | `SUPPLIER_RECEIPT_REVERSAL_PILOT_DISABLED`, EN and AR, no table or scheduler change, a replay also refused, receipt stays LIVE, 1240 net 120,000 | `convex/supplierSettlementContainment.test.ts`, four tests |
| Dispute and payment audit, idempotent dispute, completeness flags | `ALLOCATE_PAYMENT` and `SET_SUPPLIER_DISPUTE` entries; replay is a no-op; same key with a changed payload or direction is refused; list queries report `complete` | `convex/supplierSettlementAudit.test.ts`, eleven tests |

Correction doors (SCRUM-869 S1b), bound in `convex/supplierObligationCorrections.test.ts`. Two further doors could silently undo the settlement figures above, so they refuse server-side behind the same switch file, with a coded EN/AR error before any write:

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `vehicles.correctAcquisitionCost` SUPPLIER_INVOICE_ERROR or VENDOR_CREDIT on an unpaid ON_ACCOUNT car (12,500) | `SUPPLIER_PAYABLE_CORRECTION_PILOT_DISABLED`; payable stays 12,500; 2400 net 12,500,000 credit; no table or scheduler change | `refuses to rewrite the supplier payable, coded EN/AR, and changes nothing` |
| Correction dialog context for the same car | no allowed types while unpaid; CASH_REFUND offered once paid | `the correction dialog is not offered the refused types for an unpaid ON_ACCOUNT car` |
| CASH_REFUND on a PAID ON_ACCOUNT car (ruled pilot route) | cost corrected, payable untouched | `control: a CASH_REFUND on a PAID ON_ACCOUNT car still corrects the cost and leaves the payable alone` |
| `expenses.reverseExpense` while a 200 cost recovery is open | `SUPPLIER_COST_EXPENSE_REVERSAL_PILOT_DISABLED`; 1240 net 200,000 debit unchanged | `refuses while the cost recovery is open, coded EN/AR, and changes nothing` |
| Same after an 80 receipt | same code, receipt stays LIVE | `refuses with the same code once the supplier has paid part of it` |
| Expense with no supplier obligation | still reversed | `control: an ordinary expense with no supplier obligation is still reversed` |

The expense-reversal message that advised reversing every receipt first is replaced: it now says the expense cannot be reversed while those receipts stand, because the receipt reversal door is itself closed. The expenses screen disables the reverse action for a supplier-borne expense. Not covered: OCC, browser rendering of the screen and dialog, and any real-backend run. The rehearsal cases `SUP-PAY` (payable 8,000: 3,000 then 5,000), `SUP-MARGIN` (margin 7,000: 2,000 then 5,000), `SUP-RECOVERY` (cost 200: 80 then 120) and `SUP-REFUSALS` (the three refusals above, state unchanged) in `scripts/accountingRehearsalCases.mjs` repeat these figures through public API calls and are required case ids. Their only run so far is against the in-memory model backend in `scripts/accountingRehearsalCases.test.ts`, which catches eleven injected defects; a green real-backend run remains a SCRUM-762 obligation.
## SCRUM-179-mobile-expense-categories

One shared category tuple preserves the independently captured native twelve-value domain and order for registered create/update arguments and stored expense records. Forms without amortization inputs derive the eleven non-prepaid choices; mobile reads retain PREPAID. DETAILING, TRANSPORT and FEES are offered under their existing meanings; INSPECTION, REGISTRATION, CLEANING and INSURANCE are removed without remapping. Server payroll and prepaid business guards remain authoritative.

`convex/expenseCategories.test.ts` compares actual registered/stored validator metadata with an independent native capture. `convex/expenses.test.ts` exercises the three restored ordinary categories through public pending creation, identical-key replay and removal, and measures unchanged expense, transaction, event, journal, line, outbox, command and prepaid records around prepaid/payroll refusals. The real EN/AR mobile component/SelectField controls in `apps/mobile/src/features/workspace/modules/expenses.categories.test.tsx` failed before the fix and verify offered choices, exact save category, refused-response retry identity/date/payload and success-only retirement. Their Convex SDK boundary is mocked; they do not establish native financial writes. The existing expense/prepaid lifecycle tests cover the retained backend transitions. These are local component, metadata and serial backend-harness controls, not authenticated device/platform, OCC, production-data or release certification.

## Configuration choice read budgets, SCRUM-760

Matrix rows `SCRUM760-lead-choice-read-budget` and `SCRUM760-quote-choice-read-budget` bind the two literal tests in `convex/configChoiceReadBudget.test.ts`: `lead choices retain boundary values and refuse an oversized configuration` and `quote choices retain boundary values and refuse an oversized configuration`. Each authorized reader receives the final active row among 8,192 configured rows; adding one inactive row makes the query explicitly refuse with `CONFIG_CHOICE_LIMIT`. Inactive rows count toward the read budget. These controls distinguish refusal from silent truncation; they establish harness behavior, not Convex platform I/O, concurrency or capacity. Settings write authority and write quotas do not change.

## Marketplace reply authority, SCRUM-760

Matrix row `SCRUM760-marketplace-reply` binds `convex/marketplaceResponses.test.ts`, test `a marketplace-only member can reply without gaining inventory visibility`. The same member is refused by `vehicles.list` and persists a matched request's `NOT_AVAILABLE` response under existing `marketplace:respond` authority. The actual form skips the protected catalog query when visibility is absent; a separate control preserves catalog choices for authorized members. A non-vehicle reply does not promise lead creation, and the success message now states only that the reply was sent. These are source/harness and anonymous paint controls, not authenticated platform acceptance. No reply-kind requirements, inventory permission or write authority change.

## Lead source read authority, SCRUM-760

Matrix row `SCRUM760-lead-source` binds `convex/leadSourceAuthority.test.ts`, test `Reception receives active own lead sources while settings remains refused`. Reception reads only active own-tenant IDs and labels under existing `view:leads`; the settings query still refuses. The same control excludes inactive and foreign rows, refuses foreign and unauthenticated access, and refuses after authority is revoked. The form keeps its historical free-text source contract for automations. No settings permission or write authority is granted. A separate anonymous screen replay paints a configured source selected through the actual form; authenticated role execution and real-backend verification remain separate release obligations.

## Quote status read authority, SCRUM-760

Matrix row `SCRUM760-quote-status` binds `convex/quoteStatusAuthority.test.ts`, test `an accountant can read only active tenant quote choices without settings authority`. Existing quote creation requires `view:sales`; its picker returns only active tenant IDs and labels under that same authority. The settings query still refuses the accountant, and separate controls refuse non-sales users, foreign tenants, unauthenticated users and revoked authority. No settings permission, quote economic rule or write door changes. The screen replay opens the actual quote dialog for every allowed audited role and compares the checkbox count to the public picker query. These are source and harness controls until the real-role browser execution passes; neither the new read nor passing component controls certify a sale, financial posting or production data.

## Consigned supplier payable VAT refusal, SCRUM-781

Matrix row `SCRUM781-consigned-payable-vat` binds `convex/consignedPayableVat.scrum781.test.ts`, test `scenario consigned-supplier-payable-refuses-vat-and-settles-gross: VAT 2,000 is refused, then the same payable settles gross at taxAmount 0` (the refusal and the gross settlement run in one test with the ledger asserted; other tests in the file are controls). Owner ruling SCRUM-773 c22401 (2026-10-07, option أ, reaffirmed 2026-10-28): a consigned (SOURCED) vehicle's supplier payable is settled at its gross amount and a VAT amount on it is refused. Independent expected values: a consigned payable of 18,000 JOD (sale 20,000, commission 2,000) paid with VAT 2,000 must be refused with no payable change, no `commandIdempotency` row, no accounting event, journal or line, and the same key must be refusable again (the refusal is not cached); the same payable then settles gross as Dr supplier accounts payable 18,000,000 / Cr cash 18,000,000 minor units with no VAT_RECEIVABLE and no COST_OF_VEHICLES_SOLD line, so deal profit stays 2,000. Controls in the same file: VAT omitted or 0 settles gross; an instalment-paid consigned payable still refuses VAT; a VAT amount that rounds to zero minor units is still refused; a SOURCED vehicle without a linked sale and a payable whose vehicle row is unreadable both fail closed; an owned ON_ACCOUNT payable still reclassifies VAT out of Vehicle Inventory (unsold) or Cost of Vehicles Sold (sold). `recordPartialPayment` has no VAT argument; its test is a characterization of the argument validator and not a failing-first control. This row is serial `convex-test` harness evidence only: it does not prove concurrency (the harness has no OCC), Convex platform behaviour, or production data. Historical or queued consigned settlements that already carry VAT (SCRUM-782) are not covered and are not certified by this row. The refusal is the coded error `CONSIGNED_PAYABLE_VAT_REFUSED` (English and Arabic through the error catalog), the same idempotency key retried after a refusal still refuses with zero writes, `sourcingPayables.list` and `get` return `vatReclassAllowed` from the same predicate `markPaid` enforces, and the web /sourcing pay dialog hides the VAT field for a consigned payable and submits 0 (rendered tests in English and Arabic). The mobile app is outside the pilot and is not changed: it still shows a tax field and would receive the coded refusal as a generic error.

## Queued consigned supplier VAT, SCRUM-782

Matrix row `SCRUM782-queued-consigned-payable-vat` binds `convex/consignedQueuedVat.scrum782.test.ts`, test `legacy queued consigned VAT dead-letters visibly without a VAT or COGS journal`. Owner ruling SCRUM-773 c22401, reaffirmed by SCRUM-760 c23812 ruling 1, forbids reclaimable VAT on a consigned settlement. A legacy PAID payable of 18,000 JOD carrying taxAmount 2,000 and a queued settlement of 18,000,000 minor units with taxMinor 2,000,000 must produce zero VAT_RECEIVABLE lines, zero COST_OF_VEHICLES_SOLD lines and zero new financial records. The original payable and payload remain unchanged. Each refused attempt consumes the existing retry budget; the tenth leaves an operator-visible FAILED row. An unchanged manual retry refuses again. AP reconciliation reports UNAVAILABLE / PENDING_POSTINGS, and the VAT report has zero input VAT. This is quarantine, not a repaired settlement or reconciled books.

Controls in the same file cover direct posting, permanent refusal before a missing-period hold, valid zero-VAT queue progress and duplicate replay, missing or foreign payable/vehicle references, a sale-linked payable whose vehicle now reads STOCK, and SOURCED without a linked sale. Queued OWNED ON_ACCOUNT controls retain Dr AP 18,000,000 / Cr cash 18,000,000 plus Dr VAT_RECEIVABLE 2,000,000 / Cr Vehicle Inventory (unsold) or COGS (sold) 2,000,000. Whole financial-record comparisons cover additions, removals and snapshot changes.

Persisted `PERIOD_HELD` fixtures cover eager drains and the one-time rollout migration. Automatic drains release invalid supplier VAT holds when an organization drains anyway. The cron selects only due unclaimed/dispatched rows and schedules no parked-range scan. The literal test `cron ticks with only valid period holds schedule no work or parked-range scans` measures the persisted scheduler records across three ticks with owned-VAT and zero-VAT holds; no job is added. This is harness evidence about scheduled work, not a production bandwidth measurement.

The internal `accountingOutbox:recoverPeriodHeldSupplierVat` migration pages the parked range 100 rows at a time. Its deployment-wide `supplierVatHoldMigration` singleton stores the cursor and completion marker in the same transaction as releases and scheduled claims. `rollout migration resumes beyond the first page of valid period holds` puts 101 valid holds before the invalid row, calls one page, then resumes from the stored cursor and requires one refusal attempt with no financial change. Owned-VAT and zero-VAT holds remain byte-identical. A repeated call after completion returns READY with zero reclassifications and changes neither the checkpoint nor scheduler records.

`scripts/rolloutRelease.mjs` invokes this page function after verifying deployment identity and before the empty-deployment success path, through the existing operator-key CLI wrapper. `scripts/releaseGuard.ts` runs pages serially to READY, shares the bank/Inbox rollout deadline and imposes a page cap; failed calls and malformed evidence refuse the rollout. Later deployments make only the completed-state probe. READY certifies reclassification, not completed quarantine or repaired settlement. This follows the bank rebuild's persisted-cursor MORE/READY rollout pattern (`bankBalanceBackfill:runPage`, `rebuildBankBalances`) and [Convex's migration guidance](https://stack.convex.dev/migrating-data-with-mutations). The posting-time VAT refusal precedes the only period-hold writer, so new invalid consigned VAT rows cannot enter this closed migration population. No new migration framework or recurring recovery is used.

The literal test `historical taxed settlement exact replay through direct returns the posted occurrence` calls `postAccountingEvent` directly. It first posts the taxed journal through that engine with owned evidence, restores consigned evidence, removes the period and replays the identical command. It must return the existing event and journal with `alreadyPosted: true`, changing no financial record. The outbox counterpart completes its duplicate row against the existing event before period or VAT eligibility checks. Payload, source and key collisions still refuse; reserved receipt authority is proven before either idempotency lookup. Outbox replay-proof failures record the fixed code `SUPPLIER_REPLAY_REFUSED` before any financial write. Only this read-only proof is caught; financial posting exceptions still escape and roll back. This follows [AWS's idempotent API guidance](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/making-retries-safe-with-idempotent-APIs/): acknowledge the same request and reject changed parameters. It acknowledges historical VAT without correcting it.

The disposition follows `accountingOutbox.ts`'s `custodyPostingRefusal` and `failOutboxRow`: record an expected refusal before financial writes and before temporary holds. [Convex scheduled functions](https://docs.convex.dev/scheduling/scheduled-functions) documents atomic scheduling from mutations and internal-error retries; [Convex error handling](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/error-handling/) allows typed expected-failure outcomes. The shared posting-engine guard covers direct calls as well as queue processing. The pure posting rule is not the enforcement point.

These are serial convex-test checks. Real-backend execution, OCC, authenticated EN/AR screens, independent review and production data are not proven. Already-posted consigned VAT is unchanged and needs an operator census and, where present, an approved correction. No production census or correction ran in this change.

## Supplier payables reconciliation cutoff, SCRUM-876

Matrix row `SCRUM876-supplier-payables-recon-cutoff` binds fourteen literal tests in `convex/supplierPayablesReconciliation.scrum876.test.ts` (one scenario record each, `regression/scenarios/money/ap-recon-*`): seven for the post-cutoff row, payment, credit and date guards and seven for the cancelled-payable guard added in round 2. Invariant: the AP-Suppliers subledger and the GL control balance are measured at the same cutoff, and a comparison that cannot show that is UNAVAILABLE with a reason and a close warning, never `isReconciled: true`. A native payable row is only its current state (one cumulative `amountPaid`, one `updatedAt`) and cannot be restated to an earlier date, so `computeSupplierPayablesReconciliation` returns `POST_CUTOFF_ACTIVITY` when a native row's `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `paidAt`, `disputedAt` or `cancelledAt` is after the cutoff or not a finite number, or when the canonical AP account holds a journal line dated after the cutoff that no retained opening payable accounts for (a future-dated sale credits AP on its sale date, not on the day the row is written; round 3 below). A non-finite cutoff returns `INVALID_DATE`. CANCELLED payables are read last under the same shared 4,000-row bound (a larger set is `OVER_LIMIT`, refused and not summed) and contribute no balance, but a native one that was written after the cutoff, or whose `cancelledAt` (or `updatedAt` when a legacy row has no `cancelledAt`) is after the cutoff or not a finite number, also returns `POST_CUTOFF_ACTIVITY`: it stood for its AP credit at the cutoff while its reversal is dated later, so omitting it either hides a real difference or invents one. A cancelled native row also counts as a native row for the dated-AP-line probe, because its credit can be dated after the cutoff while its reversal is dated before it. A cancelled opening-basis row is not read for a balance, as before. An opening-basis payable keeps its own dated reconstruction (its basis date and dated payments) and does not by itself trigger either guard. Period close maps both reasons to `SUPPLIER_PAYABLES_RECON_POST_CUTOFF_WARNING` (English and Arabic in the close dialog); the close is never blocked, matching the owner decision that reconciliation differences warn. Independent expected values (JOD, three decimals): two periods each hold a 19,000 liability; deleting the earlier payable while its GL credit stays used to read `isReconciled: true` with no warning at the earlier period's close, and now reads UNAVAILABLE with the warning. A later 4,000 payable that offsets a 4,000 shortfall inside the earlier period, a 5,000 payment dated after the cutoff (which used to invent a 5,000 difference), a GL credit dated after the cutoff, a row written after the cutoff for an earlier-dated posting, and NaN or infinite cutoffs are all UNAVAILABLE. Cancelled-payable expected values (JOD): a 19,000 January payable with a misposted 19,000 January debit reads a 19,000 difference while live (GL 0, subledger 19,000); cancelled in February with the February reversal it used to read `AVAILABLE`, `isReconciled: true`, no warning and `canClose: true`, and now reads UNAVAILABLE with the warning, `canClose` still true, in an open and in a closed (snapshot-read) January alike; the same payable with no misposting used to invent a 19,000 difference once cancelled and now reads UNAVAILABLE; a legacy cancelled row with no `cancelledAt` and a February `updatedAt` is UNAVAILABLE; a payable cancelled in January whose credit is dated February is UNAVAILABLE; three cancelled rows read inside a bound of three are AVAILABLE and inside a bound of two are `OVER_LIMIT`. Controls: a payable and its credit inside the period reconcile with no supplier warning, a payable created and cancelled inside the period with its reversal reconciles to zero with no warning, a row stamped exactly at the cutoff is on the cutoff, and a genuine 4,000 difference wholly inside the period is still reported as a difference. Known limits: this is a refusal, not a reconstruction, so every close of an earlier period whose payables or AP postings have moved since will warn that the comparison could not be made, even when the books are right; a hard-deleted payable that left no AP-control line dated after the cutoff is still caught only as an ordinary difference (round 3 closes the case where it did leave one); a cancelled native row with neither `cancelledAt` nor `updatedAt` cannot be placed and is treated as before the cutoff; an organisation whose cancelled and paid payables together exceed the 4,000-row bound now reads `OVER_LIMIT` instead of AVAILABLE (truthful, and a follow-up should index or bound cancelled history); `OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` still maps to the pre-existing over-limit wording at close. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence only: not the Convex runtime, OCC, read-limit enforcement or production data.

Round 3 (Seat 2 HIGH, same matrix row, fifteen further literal records `regression/scenarios/money/ap-recon-*`, eight in `convex/supplierPayablesReconciliation.scrum876.test.ts` and seven in `convex/supplierPayablesReconciliation.scrum876.opening.test.ts`). Invariant: surviving payable rows must not be the only evidence that later AP activity exists. The dated-AP-line probe used to run only while a native payable row survived, so hard-deleting the last native row switched it off. It now runs for every organisation. The GL side is always read as of the cutoff, so a later line cannot distort it; what a later line proves is that the native subledger, which cannot be restated, moved after the cutoff. While a native row survives, any later line on the canonical AP control account is `POST_CUTOFF_ACTIVITY` as before. With no native row, each later line (at most 200, one journal-entry read each, more is `OVER_LIMIT`) is attributed through its journal entry and is excused only when the entry is an opening-balance posting or its source is a retained, non-cancelled opening payable already rebuilt as of the cutoff; a sale or acquisition posting, a reversal of one, a payable that no longer exists, a manual journal or an opening payable's id under another source family stays `POST_CUTOFF_ACTIVITY`. Expected values (JOD): the Seat 2 sequence (19,000 January payable, misposted 19,000 January debit, February cancellation and reversal, then hard delete) read `AVAILABLE`, `isReconciled: true`, no warning and `canClose: true` at the previous head, and now read UNAVAILABLE with the warning and `canClose` still true, in an open and in a closed (snapshot-read) January alike; a part-paid then deleted payable and a deleted payable whose credit is dated after the cutoff are UNAVAILABLE; an omitted cutoff refuses a future-dated AP line even with no payable row at all. Controls that must not change: an empty system reconciles at zero with no warning; a 19,000 January GL balance with no payable row is `AVAILABLE` with a reported 19,000 difference; a later line on another account does not refuse; an opening-basis payable of 100 JOD (100,000 minor) paid 40,000 minor after the cutoff still reads 100,000 on both sides at the cutoff and 60,000 on both sides afterwards, also when the payment was reversed after the cutoff; and an opening balance posted after the cutoff leaves the earlier period `AVAILABLE`. Option weighed and declined: running the probe unconditionally with no attribution is one line, but it turned the first three controls and the budget record red, because it discards the opening payables' dated reconstruction (a payment of an opening payable after the cutoff would make every earlier period UNAVAILABLE for good). Retained lifecycle evidence was not used because the schema keeps none for a deleted native row and this change adds no schema. Known limits: a hard delete that leaves no AP-control line dated after the cutoff, and no other trace, is not detectable by any reader of this schema; a manual journal on the AP account dated after the cutoff now makes an organisation with no native payable row UNAVAILABLE where it was AVAILABLE (warning only); an organisation with more than 200 AP lines after the cutoff and no native row reads `OVER_LIMIT`. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence only; the opening settlement payments in the new controls are written as rows and journal entries directly (the real settlement path is `openingRemainingFamilies.test.ts`). Official sources consulted: Convex limits https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits (32,000 documents scanned, 16 MiB read, 4,096 index ranges per transaction) and Oracle Fusion Payables to General Ledger reconciliation https://docs.oracle.com/en/cloud/saas/financials/25d/ocuar/considerations-for-payables-to-general-ledger-reconciliation.html (the ending Payables balance is agreed to the General Ledger Trial Balance for the last day of the period being reconciled). Both pages were fetched on 2026-11-01; the Convex page defines the 4,096 index ranges as the number of `db.get` and `db.query` calls, which is why each attributed entry read counts and the attribution is capped at 200.

## Sourcing read authority, SCRUM-760

Matrix row `SCRUM760-sourcing-finance` binds `convex/sourcingPayables.test.ts` test `a non-finance manager cannot read sourcing financial queues`. The actual MANAGER template lacks `view:finance`: payable, pipeline and supplier-recovery queries must each return the structured `FORBIDDEN` refusal. The same fixture's finance owner reads one known payable and empty pipeline/recovery queues, proving the refusal is authorization rather than a broken transport or query. The separate `SourcingPageAuthority.test.tsx` controls exercise safe page refusal before subscribing. This row is harness evidence only; the four actual language/viewport sourcing cells in the role audit must pass on the repaired real backend before runtime acceptance. No ledger, payout or production-data certification is implied.

## Supplier concession on a SOURCED financed vehicle, SCRUM-766 PR-C

Matrix rows `pilot-supplier-concession`, `pilot-supplier-concession-direct`, `pilot-supplier-concession-commission`, `pilot-supplier-concession-refusals`, `pilot-supplier-concession-reversal`, `pilot-supplier-concession-authority` and `pilot-supplier-concession-disclosure` bind the literal tests in `convex/supplierConcession.scrum766c.test.ts` (14 regression records, ids prefixed `supplier-concession-`, `financed-direct-supplier-` or `consigned-financed-`). The owner's worked example is the independent oracle, in JOD: quote 12,500, finance company approves 11,500 at 85% financed, so the NET shortfall is 850. The supplier's contract entitlement is E = 9,500 and the vehicle's `sourceCost` never changes; the concession S lives on the finance application (`supplierGapShareMinor`) and is frozen on the sale (`consignedSupplierConcessionMinor`).

| Variant | Who covers the 850 | Effective entitlement E_eff = E - S | Commission base M = K - E_eff - contra | Commission at 10% |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| V0 | showroom 850 | 9,500 | 775 | 77.5 |
| V1 | customer cash 400, showroom 450 | 9,500 | 1,175 | 117.5 |
| V2 | customer to financier 400, showroom 450 | 9,500 | 1,175 | 117.5 |
| V3 | supplier 400, showroom 450 | 9,100 | 1,175 | 117.5 |

V3 through the dealership, literal `SALE_COMPLETED` lines (debit positive, JOD): AR finance companies +11,500; sales consideration reductions +1,225; AP suppliers -9,100; consignment commission revenue -2,400; AP finance companies -1,225; the lines sum to zero. The supplier payable row is 9,100, not 9,500. V3 direct to supplier: receivable from suppliers +2,400 (A - E_eff) in place of the AP suppliers line, same commission revenue. Reversal: cancelling after the company payment is taken back cancels the 9,100 payable and nets the ledger to nothing; a part-paid payable blocks the cancellation. Stock-car concessions stay refused server-side. A concession is also refused, with the coded error `SUPPLIER_CONCESSION_PLAN_REFUSED`, on a deal that does not close on the current (v3) sale plan with a finance company, because only that plan carries the commission basis M. Row `pilot-supplier-concession-refusals` binds two more literal tests for it: `a supplier concession on a deal that is not on the current sale plan is refused before any write` (frozen v2 stamp, v1 fingerprint, no finance company: the full database is unchanged, and the dialog's eligibility query answers false) and `a stored concession is not applied when the deal builds no current-plan sale: finalize refuses and writes nothing`. A zero supplier share is unaffected. Pending owner ruling Q3 (`SUPPLIER_CONCESSION_ALLOWS_GROSS_MANUAL`) is unchanged by this rule.

Owner rulings (SCRUM-766 c23841, 2026-10-28, on top of SCRUM-760 c23812 ruling 3): Q1 is approved, the commission basis is M = K - E_eff - contra (77.5 for V0; the previous consigned financed code gave 300); Q2-Q8 are accepted as implemented: Q2 a concession after finalization is refused; Q3 a GROSS_MANUAL shortfall may carry a concession (default allow, `SUPPLIER_CONCESSION_ALLOWS_GROSS_MANUAL = true`), UNAVAILABLE never, and LEGACY_GROSS is not refused (current behaviour, covered by a test, no ruling); Q4 the supplier's consent is not recorded; Q5 no cap beyond S < E and S <= shortfall; Q6 DIRECT is chosen after the concession is resolved; Q7 the 625 fees; Q8 a `view:sales` caller can still derive S from vehicle cost minus the sale entitlement. Still pending: Q9, no separate display line (c23841 does not mention it).  Evidence class: local `convex-test` harness only. Not covered: OCC and concurrent resolution, closed-period deferral and unposted-outbox cancel, runtime and data parity, a browser rendering of the dialog, or the trusted cloud rehearsal.

Draft `M486C5` covers an owned configured-financier sale with no deposit or contribution. Its public workflow creates a 10,000 JOD stock vehicle, obtains approval for 12,500 JOD, records the handover, expected payment and legal invoice, and finalizes with an explicit zero-fee company policy. The literal acquisition is Dr 1400 / Cr 1100 for 10,000,000 minor units. The literal sale is Dr 1210 / Cr 4100 for 12,500,000 and Dr 5100 / Cr 1400 for 10,000,000. The one collectible invoice must name the finance company as payer for the full 12,500,000; the sale-linked zero-value customer document remains PAID and noncollectible per the owner ruling in SCRUM-486 c22859. Public trial balance and income statement must show 12,500,000 vehicle revenue, 10,000,000 COGS and 2,500,000 net income. The case inventories new journals to catch balanced extra entries. Local fake-backend controls reject wrong account and journal dimensions, a customer-payer finance invoice, a positive customer claim and compensating extra journals. This is model evidence only: M486C5 remains **UNAVAILABLE** as real-backend proof until exact reviewed code passes a main-only disposable-preview rehearsal with a producer-bound verdict and preview deletion. It does not certify financier receipt, cancellation, deposit lifecycle, closed-period accounting or production data.

This is the independent expected-value design for the required ownership × deal-route × deposit × lifecycle suite. It is derived from the owner's SCRUM-486 rulings (comments 21356, 21360 and 21364), not from the application's posting helpers. The numbers below are **JOD minor units** (1 JOD = 1,000 minor units). No row is certified merely because it appears here. A cell becomes certified only when a required CI test drives its public mutations, checks the literal values at each step, and the same scenario passes on a disposable Convex preview.

Case `M486C1` extends the owned CASH 12,500 / 10,000 JOD harness row in the trusted accounting rehearsal registry. Since SCRUM-802 (owner ruling, Option A) the CASH procedure is **pay in full by deposit first**: a cash sale completes only when its full price is already held as a deposit, so the case records a 12,500 JOD CASH deposit on the quote before it completes the sale. It creates the stock vehicle through the public mutation and checks the event-linked acquisition posting (Dr 1400 inventory / Cr 1100 cash, 10,000 JOD) before the public quote, the full-price deposit and the sale. It checks the deposit receipt (Dr 1100 / Cr 2100) and its application (Dr 2100 / Cr 1200) as exact event-linked journals, the sale-linked PAID customer invoice with zero outstanding, the event-linked sale journal's four literal lines on chart codes 1200/4100/5100/1400, AR/revenue customer and salesperson dimensions, all four vehicle dimensions, and public trial-balance and income-statement row deltas (customer AR nets to zero across the sale and the application, the deposit liability falls by 12,500, revenue and COGS rise by the literal 4100 and 5100 amounts). The public inventory balance must rise by 10,000 JOD on acquisition and return to its pre-acquisition level after the sale. It searches the tenant's receivables for duplicate sale claims across customers or payer types and returns UNPROVEN if the 200-row public listing could be truncated or the case crosses its UTC accounting month. Its in-memory rehearsal tests include missing/understated acquisition posting, uncleared public inventory, absent/duplicate/wrong-customer invoice, cross-customer and other-payer duplicate, wrong AR or revenue customer, wrong vehicle or salesperson journal dimension, wrong revenue account, wrong chart code, missing income row and one-minor-unit report-drift negative controls. A reviewed main-only release rehearsal at SHA `436b7a80f066512be2a42c5e2ac44210df91793b` passed `M486C1` among 23/23 real-backend cases with a successful producer-bound required verdict and deleted preview ([run 38081573337](https://github.com/aalzriqat/Auto/actions/runs/38081573337)). That run predates the SCRUM-802 re-cut: it ran the earlier complete-then-collect flow, so it is **not** evidence for the deposit-first procedure, which the backend now enforces. That is evidence for this exact SHA and row, not the full matrix. It does not certify customer receipt settlement, cancellation, vehicle SOLD state or duplicate-sale refusal. The separate harness row patches the vehicle cost directly without a purchase posting; its 1400 result is a sale delta, not a full vehicle inventory lifecycle balance.

Case `M486C2` follows the same public acquisition and quote path, takes a cash deposit of the full 12,500 JOD price (SCRUM-802: a cash sale completes only when its full price is held as a deposit), completes an owned cash sale, and checks the deposit receipt, application and sale journals separately. It expects Dr 1100 / Cr 2100 on receipt, Dr 2100 / Cr 1200 on application, the original 12,500 JOD invoice with zero outstanding and PAID status, and unchanged 12,500 JOD revenue and 10,000 JOD COGS. At receipt, the deposit must already point to a settled, inbound, CASH canonical customer payment with 12,500 JOD unapplied; sale completion must create exactly one ACTIVE allocation of that same payment to the invoice and leave it with zero unapplied balance. Public trial balance must show cash and deposit liability each rise by 12,500 JOD while held, with AR unchanged; after application cash remains, liability clears and AR nets to zero (the sale debit and the application credit are both 12,500 JOD). Public revenue, COGS, expense and other-income totals and rows must remain unchanged while the deposit is held, and the sale must add the literal 4100 revenue and 5100 COGS row deltas. Local mutation-killing controls reject a missing, misattributed or wrong-method receipt payment, a receipt payment already allocated while held, sale allocation of a different payment, an over-allocation of the held payment, an unapplied hold, omitted deposit receipt in the public report, erroneous deposit-time revenue, missing revenue row, a missing application posting, an unreduced invoice, wrong original invoice amount, wrong balance document or outstanding amount, and wrong revenue account. A reviewed main-only release rehearsal at SHA `f6db18123efa7cf4a6f4a48058389b78a2e7e30f` passed `M486C2` among 24/24 real-backend cases with a successful producer-bound required verdict and deleted preview ([run 38089315403](https://github.com/aalzriqat/Auto/actions/runs/38089315403)). That run predates the SCRUM-802 re-cut (its deposit was 200 JOD against a 12,300 JOD balance, which the backend now refuses), so it is not evidence for the full-price deposit procedure. This certifies only this exact SHA and row, not the full matrix. This case does not certify the separate cancellation/refund lifecycle or the quote wizard's alternative completion door. The global B2 journal/outbox sweep currently executes before this row and does not certify entries M486C2 creates; its own four event-linked journals are asserted exactly.

Case `M486C3` takes a 200 JOD CASH deposit against an owned CASH quote and refunds it before any sale. The independently computed receipt is Dr 1100 / Cr 2100 for 200,000 minor units; the refund must be Dr 2100 / Cr 1100 for the same amount. Relative to the post-acquisition baseline, cash and deposit liability rise while held and every account/currency balance returns to that baseline after refund. The owned car must be RESERVED while held and AVAILABLE after refund. Customer AR, vehicle revenue, COGS and income must not change at either deposit step. The owner who took the deposit must not resolve it; a distinct authorized manager must execute the refund. The public deposit, accounting events, journals, canonical inbound payment and outbound refund payment must agree on the one 200 JOD movement, with no extra deposit event, sale invoice or deposit application. Local negative controls exercise creator refusal and attempted write, revenue misclassification, unexpected receivables, accidental allocations, wrong held and outbound payment fields, stray balanced entries, stale vehicle hold, missing income data, and incorrect incoming payer or method. A separate control requires a UTC month crossover to be UNPROVEN. These representative fake-backend mutations do not individually prove every oracle assertion. The existing generic B1 refund case is not proof of this owned quote route. A reviewed main-only release rehearsal at `6e240882817e66129739e33f2ae0e0fe87d2a0a1` passed M486C3 among 26/26 real-backend cases with a successful required exact-SHA verdict and deleted preview ([run 38123663397](https://github.com/aalzriqat/Auto/actions/runs/38123663397)). That does not prove the operator's no-code UI path.

Case `M486C4` takes a 200 JOD CASH deposit against an owned CASH quote, keeps it HELD, then has a distinct authorized manager forfeit it before sale. The receipt is Dr 1100 cash / Cr 2100 deposit liability for 200,000 minor units; the forfeiture is Dr 2100 / Cr other income for 200,000. Relative to the pre-deposit baseline, cash remains 200,000 higher, deposit liability returns to baseline, and GL other income and the legacy operational P&L each rise by 200 JOD on the forfeiture date, outside vehicle turnover. No outbound collection payment, invoice application, or sale is permitted in this row. The trusted case reads the public deposit, literal event-linked journals, trial balance, GL income statement, operational P&L, and collection-payment direction; it also confirms opening collection rows remain present. Local negative controls reject omitted operational income, unexpected outbound money, and deletion of an opening collection. Harness tests additionally cover source-linked historical receipt exclusion, unchanged original receipt journal after later forfeiture, hold-sourced forfeiture, reversal dates and 129/512/513 old-receipt availability boundaries. SCRUM-815 c22806 rules that the old receipt month must show zero revenue while the later forfeiture month shows other income; a genuinely wrong GL posting in a closed period needs a separate documented, auditable correction and is not certified by this read-side check. The main-only release rehearsal at protected main `6e240882817e66129739e33f2ae0e0fe87d2a0a1` passed M486C4 among 26/26 real-backend cases, with zero failed or unproven and the required exact-SHA verdict successful; its disposable preview was deleted ([run 38123663397](https://github.com/aalzriqat/Auto/actions/runs/38123663397)). That evidence covers this row at this SHA, not production data or the full matrix. The operator no-code path remains separate SCRUM-813 acceptance work.

The M486C4 harness also characterizes the 2,048 source-query ceiling: 200 historical receipts with nine holds each must refuse the report rather than return a partial total. This is an availability limit, not a certified real-backend capacity measurement.

## Fixed owned-vehicle example

| Fact | JOD | Minor units |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Vehicle price / approved finance-company amount | 12,500 | 12,500,000 |
| Owned vehicle cost | 10,000 | 10,000,000 |
| Customer deposit held in cash | 200 | 200,000 |
| Dealer contribution | 1,375 | 1,375,000 |
| Custody issued / spent / returned | 300 / 150 / 150 | 300,000 / 150,000 / 150,000 |
| Direct handover cost paid from bank | 100 | 100,000 |

The advance itself is not an expense. Actual handover expense is 150,000 + 100,000 = **250,000**. A cancelled deal retains this actual expense; it does not create a second cancellation loss. The configured-finance **commissionable vehicle margin** is 12,500,000 − 1,375,000 − 10,000,000 = **1,125,000** and a separately configured 10% percentage policy would compute **112,500**. This is a *calculation-path* expectation, separate from the requirement to preserve an approved manual commission verbatim. Supplying 112,500 manually and reading it back cannot certify the percentage rule. Automatic percentage commissions must not be silently enabled.

### Required literal journals (owned vehicle)

Each line is a separate debit or credit; a scenario must assert account code, currency, amount and journal/event identity, not only a net balance. Account codes identify the chart visible to the dealer. The row order below is business order, not an assertion about asynchronous posting order.

| Step | Debit | Credit | Amount |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: |
| Hold customer deposit | 1100 cash | 2100 deposit liability | 200,000 |
| Issue employee custody | 1250 custody clearing | 1100 cash | 300,000 |
| Consume custody for non-refundable service | 6890 ownership transfer expense | 1250 custody clearing | 150,000 |
| Return unused custody | 1100 cash | 1250 custody clearing | 150,000 |
| Pay direct service cost | 6890 ownership transfer expense | 1110 bank | 100,000 |
| Recognize owned vehicle sale | 1210 finance-company AR (or 1200 customer AR for CASH) | 4100 vehicle revenue | 12,500,000 |
| Release owned vehicle cost | 5100 COGS | 1400 inventory | 10,000,000 |
| Recognize financed dealer contribution | 4180 contra-revenue | 2220 finance-company AP | 1,375,000 |
| Forward deposit obligation to financier | 2100 deposit liability | 2220 finance-company AP | 200,000 |
| Forward contribution plus deposit | 2220 finance-company AP | 1110 bank | 1,575,000 |
| Receive full approved amount from financier | 1110 bank | 1210 finance-company AR | 12,500,000 |
| Accrue a 112,500 commission after the independent policy calculation | 6100 commission expense | 2300 commission payable | 112,500 |

For the configured financier's completed example, **net vehicle revenue is 11,125,000**, gross vehicle margin **1,125,000**, handover expense **250,000**, commission expense **112,500**, and net deal profit **762,500**. The 200,000 deposit is forwarded customer money, not a reduction to the 12,500,000 receivable or to revenue. The company must pay the full approved amount. Manual-financier and purchasing-lessor cases have the same debtor and gross receivable under OR-1/OR-2 when those routes are supported; a mere lender to the customer is not that debtor.

**Pilot CASH scope (SCRUM-802, owner rulings 2026-10-19): taxed cash sales, and cash sales with fees, a warranty or GAP, are not completable in the pilot, are not offered at launch, and the server refuses them.** A deposit is capped at the quote's `vehiclePrice`, while such an invoice exceeds it, so the whole invoice can never be held as a deposit. Every CASH completion door refuses it with `CASH_SALE_TAX_FEES_WARRANTY_REFUSED` (AR and EN), after tenant auth and before any write or idempotency record (switch `CASH_SALE_EXTRAS_PILOT_REFUSED`, with a mutation attack that turns the refusal cells red). The taxed owned-sale cells (`ownedSaleTaxEndToEnd`) that completed a taxed cash sale by inflating the quote's `vehiclePrice` to price plus tax are retired (reason recorded in that test's header): that setup is not reachable by a user, so they certified a path the pilot refuses; the file now proves the refusal. **Trade-in settlement of a cash sale is not offered either (owner: deposit only):** only deposits count toward pay-in-full-first (constant `TRADE_IN_COUNTS_TOWARD_CASH_PAY_FIRST`, pinned false), and a cash sale paid only by a trade-in, or by a part deposit plus a trade-in, is refused with `CASH_SALE_BALANCE_UNPAID_REFUSED`. Both are known pilot limits and post-pilot activation gates: each needs a rule or cap change, a new certification round and the owner's approval. **Pilot CASH procedure (SCRUM-802, owner ruling Option A): pay in full by deposit first.** The customer pays the whole 12,500,000 as a deposit (Dr 1100 / Cr 2100), and completion applies it to the invoice (Dr 2100 / Cr 1200), so the receivable is zero and a partly paid cash sale is refused. The partial example that follows is the full-lifecycle (post-pilot) shape and is not enabled for the pilot: for CASH with the same owned vehicle, the 200,000 held deposit applied to the invoice is Dr 2100 / Cr 1200. The remaining customer receipt is Dr cash/bank 12,300,000 / Cr 1200. Vehicle revenue is 12,500,000, COGS 10,000,000, and the customer receivable is zero after payment. Under a separately configured 10% percentage policy on the 2,500,000 vehicle margin, commission is 250,000 and final profit is 2,000,000. An approved manual commission is preserved independently. **SCRUM-455 remains open:** the cash handover-cost path cannot be certified as available until its public writer exists.

### Separate consigned-agent example

Direct settlement is only a valid route for a **SOURCED** vehicle sold as the supplier's agent. The owned example above must never be reused for it: choosing a supplier route for owned stock is refused. For a simple sourced vehicle with approved amount 12,500,000, supplier entitlement 10,000,000 and **no dealer contribution or deposit**, the dealer's agency margin is **2,500,000**. When the finance company pays the supplier directly, the dealership's literal posting is Dr **1240 supplier receivable 2,500,000** / Cr **4170 consignment commission revenue 2,500,000**. There is no dealership vehicle revenue on 4100, no 5100 COGS, no 1400 inventory release, no customer or finance-company vehicle-price AR, and no 2400 supplier payable for the gross price. The claim remains due from the supplier until collected. A sourced deal with a dealer contribution needs its own owner-rule derivation and independently reviewed journal before certification; this simple row does not cover it.

### Deposit endings and cancellations

| Path | Literal expectation |
| --- | --- |
| Held then refunded before sale | Dr 2100 / Cr 1100 for 200,000; deposit liability zero; vehicle revenue zero. |
| Held then forfeited before sale | Dr 2100 / Cr 4200 other income for 200,000; vehicle revenue zero. |
| Applied sale then cancelled | First reverse the recognized sale through its public door; deposit returns to held liability. Refund or forfeit only through a separate authorized release. |
| Paid/disbursed sale cancellation attempted | Refuse before changing any row until receipt, financier forward, disbursement and paid-commission prerequisites are unwound through their public doors. |

Every refusal must compare full before/after tenant-owned table content, including journals, events, deposit applications, receivables and snapshots. A caught exception can commit a Convex mutation; an error response alone is insufficient evidence of rollback. A foreign-ID attempt must leave both organizations unchanged.

### Intermediate states that must become literal rows

| Transition | Expected state to pin before advancing |
| --- | --- |
| Financed owned sale recognized, before any forward or receipt | 1210 AR 12,500,000; 2220 AP 1,575,000; 2100 deposit liability zero; 4180 debit 1,375,000. Neither bank movement has occurred. |
| Forward contribution and deposit | 2220 AP zero; bank decreases 1,575,000; 1210 AR still 12,500,000. A retry must not transfer again. |
| Receive 5,000,000 of the financier's approved amount | 1210 AR remains 7,500,000; bank increases 5,000,000. A repeated idempotency key must not produce a second receipt. |
| Receive the remaining 7,500,000 | 1210 AR zero; total financier receipts 12,500,000. A returned or voided receipt must restore only the affected receivable and reverse its own bank movement. |
| Abandon before sale after custody 300,000 / spend 150,000 / return 150,000 and direct spend 100,000 | 1250 custody clearing zero; expense debit 250,000; cash net decrease 150,000; bank decrease 100,000; vehicle sale revenue, COGS and commission zero. No second cancellation loss. |
| Deposit applied, then sale reversed | Sale-side 4100/5100/1400/AR and commission postings reverse; customer deposit returns to 2100 liability 200,000. Only a later authorized release may refund or forfeit it. |

The implementation must add separate literal transition rows for partial/repeated customer receipts, financier cheque return, forwarded amount void/replacement, disbursement reversal, commission adjustment/payment, closed-period queued posting and reversal, and a refused premature cancellation. Each row names the public door, expected event key/status, affected receivable/payable and untouched rows. Until those literal rows exist and execute, those transitions are **UNAVAILABLE**. A closed-period row must assert the trial balance as the consumer reads stored snapshots before and after a later reversal; current-period journal sums cannot substitute.

OR-4 also requires distinct abandonment rows for: (1) non-refundable consumed expense, (2) substantiated refundable service tracked as a recoverable rather than cash before repayment, (3) qualifying preparation retained in vehicle inventory subject to recoverability, (4) an estimate never incurred or committed cancelled without a posting, and (5) a received but unpaid service whose expense and payable survive cancellation. The 250,000 example covers only (1). For each other classification, first verify a supported public classification/correction door; an absent door is a named product gap, never a fabricated test-row PASS.

## Matrix scope and current disposition

| Route | No deposit | Held/applied | Refunded | Forfeited | Current constraint |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Owned CASH | Required | Required | Required | Required | Handover costs lack public writer (SCRUM-455, In Progress). |
| Owned, configured financier | Required | Required | Required | Required | Exercise both full receipt and pending/returned payment states. Supplier-route selection must refuse without writes. |
| Sourced agent, CASH | Required | n/a before sale | Covered (`SCRUM486-SRC-CASH-REFUND`) | Covered (`SCRUM486-SRC-CASH-FORFEIT`) | Deposit only; no sale. Pilot evidence only after owner confirms SCRUM-486 c23529. |
| Sourced agent, configured financier, through dealership | Required | Required; covered with a contribution (`SCRUM486-SRC-FIN-DEP`) | Required | Required | Agency economics use supplier payable, not owned vehicle revenue/COGS. |
| Sourced agent, configured financier, direct to supplier | Required | Required public guard/refusal if held; covered: `convex/scrum411ForwardOrdering.test.ts` "A 12,500, E 9,000, R 500, H 200, D 1,375: the hand-worked entry posts once and balances" | Required | Required | Supplier, not dealer, receives approved amount; assert 1240 supplier claim and 4170 agency revenue for the no-contribution example. |
| Owned or sourced, named manual finance company | Required | Required | Required | Required | Financier debtor/settlement gap SCRUM-27, In Progress; no fake customer AR pass. |
| LEASE newly quoted | Required refusal | Required refusal | Required refusal | Required refusal | Current backend retires the mode under SCRUM-495; assert public quote/finalize refusals and unchanged state. Historical lease rows need a separately named migration/support contract. |
| INTERNAL_INSTALLMENT newly quoted | Required refusal | Required refusal | Required refusal | Required refusal | Owner says dealer never self-finances; assert public refusal and unchanged state. Historical rows are separate. |

The suite must expand each applicable cell across quote, deposit, approval, handover cost, finalization, receipt/disbursement, settlement and allowed cancellation. At each checkpoint it must assert literal account/currency/minor-unit lines and balanced journals; party-specific AR/AP and cash/bank; sale and application state; commission base. The owned configured-finance reference row separately expects ledger P&L net vehicle revenue **11,125,000**, gross vehicle margin **1,125,000**, handover expenses **250,000**, commission expenses **112,500**, and net profit **762,500**; dashboard deal profit **762,500** and collections **12,500,000 due before receipt / zero after full receipt** are distinct consumer expectations, not values copied from the ledger query. Trial balance and balance sheet must each have their own literal account balances and zero difference. Existing SCRUM-487 tracks a likely dashboard divergence; the cell must remain red with that Jira key until corrected. Money-moving mutations also need default-role and custom-role permission denial checks. Existing SCRUM-761 permission work is a separate lane; this suite must consume its contract rather than edit it.

## Gate and evidence contract

1. Define expected rows as literal data in a file importing no production money helper. Drive setup and transitions through public mutations. Direct inserts may seed identity, organizations and opening vehicle facts only; they must not fabricate deposits, approvals, economic classifications or postings.
2. An independent reviewer attacks the literal oracle before tests are compared with production code. Deliberately mutating the contribution account (4180 to expense) or netting 200,000 from the 1210 receivable must turn a covered cell red.
3. **Pilot subset (owner ruling SCRUM-760 decision B, 2026-10-19).** For the initial pilot, every cell for a deferred workflow (customer invoice receipt application, retained credit and the customer-credit payout, and any cancellation that depends on them; the owner confirms this list) is an **expected-refusal cell**. Such cells cannot turn the required aggregate red merely because the lifecycle is deferred, and they are the evidence that the excluded workflows are blocked.
   - **A cell passes only when** the call goes through that door's own entry type, by an actor who is otherwise authorized, with valid arguments; it asserts the specific containment refusal (the `*_REFUSALS` codes in `convex/utils/customerCreditPayoutContainment.ts`, or, for a door without one, a dedicated containment code defined beside them; a generic error code does not qualify); a full inventory of all financial tables and state (journals, subledgers, deposits, payments, sales, applications, credit balances and bank-balance aggregates) shows no change, not a spot check; and removing or flipping the server-side block turns the cell red (the item 2 mutation attack). Items 4 and 5 still apply: an unmeasured cell is UNAVAILABLE, and the cell must replay on the real backend.
   - **Unwind and cost correction are enabled, not deferred (owner ruling, SCRUM-760 timeline: "unwinding a deal, correcting a car's cost, both needed for the pilot").** These two workflows are removed from the deferred list above and are **full-behaviour cells** in `convex/scrum486Certification.test.ts` (rows U-RET, U-ABANDON, U-SOURCED, CC-CASH, CC-AP, CC-RESTATE, CC-SOURCED; see the implementation ledger). The enabled scope is exactly: deal unwind (`startDealUnwind`, `recordDealUnwindForwardReturn`, `finishDealUnwind`, `abandonDealUnwind`) of a **financed** deal whose finance-company payment arrived by bank transfer or cash, finished with the zero-credit dispositions (RETAIN_CREDIT, or REFUND with no active customer credit); and `vehicles.correctAcquisitionCost` on owned (STOCK) stock that is not yet sold. Deal unwind has no CASH-deal form (a CASH deal ends through the cancellation door), and cost correction of a SOLD or SOURCED car is refused. Other operator corrections named in the SCRUM-813 lifecycle (anything beyond `correctAcquisitionCost`) stay deferred. Deposits held against an unwound deal return to HELD with a SCRUM-712 pending disposition and leave through the existing deposit refund or forfeit doors, which are not behind the payout switch.
   - **What stays an expected-refusal cell inside unwind.** The customer-credit payout exit: `finishDealUnwind` with `customerPaymentDisposition` REFUND and positive active invoice credit, or any customer payout field, must refuse `CUSTOMER_CREDIT_PAYOUT_DISABLED` (and `FORBIDDEN` first for an actor without MANAGE_FINANCE), with every table unchanged. Row U-REFUND pins this against the shipped, unmocked `CUSTOMER_CREDIT_PAYOUT_PILOT_DISABLED = true`, and pins the other half of the contract (RETAIN_CREDIT with positive credit finishes and leaves the credit in 2110). The U-REFUND positive-credit state can only be created by the pilot-dark receipt-application helpers, so that row is harness-only and **UNAVAILABLE** as real-backend proof by design.
   - **Door census.** The list of entry points to each deferred workflow must be complete: public mutations, actions, HTTP routes, webhooks, and internal functions reachable from crons or the scheduler. Each door is exercised through its own entry type: the public mutation or action by a client call, an HTTP route or webhook through its handler with a valid request, and an internal function through its real trigger (the cron or scheduled call), or, in `convex-test`, by calling it directly with the trigger's real arguments, with the real-backend replay (item 5) running the real trigger. Where a door cannot be exercised that way, the accepted evidence for its type is a call-graph or route-table census showing, for a public mutation, action or HTTP route, no public registration, and for an internal function, no live caller from any cron, scheduled call or other function (no public registration alone never excuses an internal function), verified by an independent review seat and re-checked at every release and whenever the door's callers change. A reason that is self-attested or not re-verified counts as UNAVAILABLE, never as covered. A missed door is a matrix defect.
   - **Scope changes need the owner.** Reclassifying an enabled path as an expected-refusal cell narrows the pilot and needs an owner scope ruling, never agent judgement. Treating an existing refund-type path as pilot-enabled also needs the owner's confirmation and certification before launch as AGENTS.md requires. A recorded owner answer on whether any refund or correction is indispensable for launch, even "none", is part of the pilot evidence.
   - **Activation.** When a workflow is activated, its cells become full-behaviour cells in the same change. A deferred cell that is not an expected-refusal cell is a matrix defect to fix under SCRUM-486, not a reason to enable the workflow.
   - **Status today.** The full expanded matrix is not yet consumed by the required aggregate, but the selected-path harness in `convex/scrum486Certification.test.ts` already runs in `unit-and-integration`, and it includes positive financier/supplier receipt, held-deposit release, cancellation-with-refund and forfeiture cases. Each must be confirmed by the owner as a pilot-enabled and certified path, or reclassified by an owner scope ruling as an expected-refusal cell, before it counts as pilot evidence (open scope question recorded on SCRUM-486).
4. Each known failing cell cites a validated open Jira defect and fails explicitly; a skipped or unmeasured cell is **UNAVAILABLE**, not PASS. The required CI aggregate must consume the matrix.
5. Replay the same cases through the trusted real-backend disposable-preview rehearsal, with exact-SHA evidence, parsed scenario counts and preview deletion. `convex-test` proves harness behavior only; it does not prove Convex platform concurrency or retained production-data parity.
6. Two gates stay separate (owner ruling SCRUM-760 decision B, 2026-10-19; see AGENTS.md). The **pilot launch gate** is the narrow scope: only certified operations enabled, excluded mutations blocked server-side with evidence. The full 30-day no-code receipt application, retained credit/refund and correction path chosen in SCRUM-813 is a **post-pilot activation gate**: it must be certified before those workflows are enabled, not before the narrow pilot. Passing a dealer sale cell satisfies neither gate by itself.

This document is a proposed oracle and coverage ledger. It does **not** mark SCRUM-486 or SCRUM-760 Done.

### Phase 2 trusted-preview execution boundary

The existing `trusted-accounting-rehearsal.yml` checks out its controller at the trusted workflow SHA. For a PR, it stages the exact PR merge's `convex/` backend as **data**, audits that stage and deploys it only to a disposable preview with `--preview-create`. The credentialed step then executes `scripts/accountingPreviewRehearsal.mjs` from the trusted checkout. That script does not import this PR's `convex/scrum486Certification.test.ts`. A green accounting rehearsal on this draft PR therefore proves the existing main-owned scenarios ran against its candidate backend; it measures **zero** of the new SCRUM-486 cells.

The Phase 2 implementation must put a bounded, independently reviewed certification runner and literal oracle in the trusted controller before it can count these cells. It must call the public mutations with preview-only identities, record a stable ID and owner-ruling reference for every attempted cell, compare journals/subledgers and public report consumers with literal account/currency/minor-unit expectations, and fail closed on a missing, skipped, unparseable or unexpected cell. Extend the existing accounting rehearsal case registry, evidence validator, and producer-bound release verdict under SCRUM-762/812; a second parallel registry or advisory green check cannot satisfy G2. The required release verdict must bind the scenario evidence to the exact main SHA and the preview's create/pin/seed/delete lifecycle. Candidate PR scripts must never execute with preview credentials. A PR rehearsal may validate a candidate backend using already trusted matrix code; newly added matrix code becomes trusted only after normal review and merge, followed by a fresh main-only release run.

Selected real-backend rows M486C1–M486C4 now have exact-SHA disposable-preview proof described above. The SCRUM-760 unwind and cost-correction rows have **no** real-backend replay yet and are **UNAVAILABLE** as real-backend proof until they are added to the trusted case registry (blocked on the financed chain being green there, SCRUM-759/FD1). The remaining matrix rows are harness-only. Fully elapsed periods need consumer-facing snapshot assertions, and these release runs cannot prove retained production-data parity without separate reconciliation.

## SCRUM-869-AR-1240-RECON

Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23881 (2026-10-29): the consignment margin receipt and the supplier cost-recovery receipt are in the pilot and must be certified. Design decisions SCRUM-869#c23880 (W2, W7, W13, OQ-10): the reconciliation is per currency, carries an Unlinked bucket, says UNAVAILABLE when it cannot be computed, stores no balance, and a difference is displayed and never posted. It is not a period-close gate. The ruling digest in `regression/rulings.json` is the same one the S1 slice records, computed from the owner's verbatim comment text.

Practice checked against [ACCA's FA1 examiner report, example 1](https://www.accaglobal.com/content/dam/acca/global/PDF-students/fia/examreports/FA1/FA1%20examiners%20report%20spet-19-aug20.pdf): the list of receivable balances and the GL control account are independent sides of a reconciliation, and an error can affect either side separately. This report compares them per currency and reports differences without posting adjustments. Unlinked items stay in the total. Opening control accounts are discovered from actual opening journal lines, independently of surviving recovery rows, allocations or headers. A custom ASSET account that the product classifies as supplier-recoverable and that holds GL lines or snapshots, yet is not compared, refuses the report (independent chart-class coverage; at most 30 such accounts and 100 asset accounts are read, otherwise OVER_LIMIT). That refusal is permanent for a non-supplier custom asset account with GL activity; an explicit chart marker would narrow it and is a separate schema decision. Retained headers after reset are ignored only after their journal, referenced chart accounts, journal lines and snapshots are proved absent. The GL label in English and Arabic includes declared opening supplier recovery control accounts as well as 1240.

Account facts, traced in `convex/accounting/postingRules.ts`: GL 1240 (`RECEIVABLE_FROM_SUPPLIERS`) is debit-normal. It is debited by the consigned DIRECT_TO_SUPPLIER sale (margin) and by a supplier-borne expense (cost recovery). It is credited by `SUPPLIER_RECEIVABLE_COLLECTED`, by `SUPPLIER_COST_RECOVERY_RECEIVED` and by a deposit retained against commission (seeded into `amountReceived` when the receivable opens). The subledger is the signed open balance of every non-CANCELLED receivable plus every non-REVERSED recovery. Every row is a call to `accountingReports.supplierReceivablesReconciliation` in `convex/supplierReceivablesReconciliation.scrum869.test.ts`; figures are JOD (1 JOD = 1,000 minor units) unless stated.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Opening 100 JOD on 1999, then recovery row deleted | Independent GL read 100,000; report GL 100,000, subledger 0, difference 100,000; not reconciled | `a lost opening recovery leaves a difference on its independently discovered GL account` |
| Valid 100 JOD opening with custom partner-capital counterpart | AVAILABLE; A/R GL and subledger 100,000; equity excluded | `a valid custom partner-capital counterpart does not block supplier reconciliation` |
| Opening allocations/results and recovery deleted, GL retained | Independent GL 100,000; difference 100,000 or UNAVAILABLE, never a match | `lost opening allocations and recovery cannot hide retained custom-account GL` |
| Opening supplier journal leg deleted after allocation/recovery loss | Independent snapshot GL 100,000; OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE, incomplete | `partial opening journal-line loss cannot hide a surviving snapshot balance` |
| Supplier opening leg replaced by equal cash debit, journal still balanced | Independent snapshot GL 100,000; OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE, incomplete | `balanced replacement of an opening supplier leg cannot hide its snapshot` |
| 3,000 two-line recovery receipts plus 3,000 two-line reversals, real opening and expense/receipt | AVAILABLE; unchanged GL/subledger 220,000, difference 0; harness I/O within document/range limits | `three thousand recovery receipts and their reversals remain reportable` |
| Retained non-cutover journal, referenced chart/lines/snapshots and recovery deleted | OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE; journal identity prevents false reset retirement | `a retained non-cutover journal prevents retirement after its accounts and GL evidence are gone` |
| Opening header and recovery deleted, GL retained | Independent GL 100,000; UNAVAILABLE, no totals | `lost opening header and recovery cannot hide retained custom-account GL` |
| Orphan supplierCostRecoveries-family custom GL | Independent GL 100,000; OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE | `orphan supplierCostRecoveries family GL on an unclassified custom account refuses` |
| Orphan supplierCostRecoveryReceipts-family custom GL | Independent GL 100,000; OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE | `orphan supplierCostRecoveryReceipts family GL on an unclassified custom account refuses` |
| Opening supplier journal leg deleted AND its line removed from the retained header, allocations and recovery lost, snapshot survives | Independent snapshot GL 100,000; OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE, incomplete, no currencies (a header that agrees with the live legs proves nothing) | `r5 H2(i): deleting the supplier leg AND its retained-header line cannot hide the surviving snapshot` |
| Supplier account swapped to cash in BOTH the live leg and the retained header, allocations and recovery lost, snapshot survives | Independent snapshot GL 100,000; OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE, incomplete (the chart's supplier-class asset account still holds GL evidence) | `r5 H2(ii): swapping the supplier account to cash in BOTH the live leg and the retained header cannot hide its snapshot` |
| Opening journal lost its supplier leg and every line and snapshot for it, header edited to agree | OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE (retained lines no longer balance) | `r5 H2(iii): an opening journal that lost its supplier leg is refused even when no snapshot survives` |
| Custom ASSET account with no GL activity | AVAILABLE, complete, reconciled | `r5 H2 CONTROL: a custom asset account with no GL activity does not block the comparison` |
| 30 idle custom ASSET accounts, then a 31st | 30 reconcile; 31 is UNAVAILABLE OVER_LIMIT, never a partial look | `r5 H2: exactly 30 idle custom asset accounts reconcile; a 31st is OVER_LIMIT, never a partial look` |
| More than 26,700 unrelated journal lines beside a real recovery receipt | AVAILABLE, reconciled; harness reads under 2,000 documents (the read follows the chart, not journal history) | `r5 M1(a): more than 26,700 unrelated journal lines still reconcile once a recovery-family journal exists` |
| 2,000 unrelated lines with 9,000-character descriptions, `transactionLimits: true` | AVAILABLE, reconciled; harness reads under 1,000,000 bytes (the reviewed head read 18,769,196 against Convex's 16 MiB limit) | `r5 M1(b): unrelated lines with large descriptions stay under the 16 MiB read limit (convex-test transactionLimits enforced)` |
| Actual financial reset completes, POSTED header remains | AVAILABLE, complete, empty currencies; deleted authority retired | `completed financial reset retires retained opening headers` |
