| Fresh 100 JOD opening after actual reset | AVAILABLE; GL and subledger 100,000; difference 0 | `fresh opening after financial reset ignores the retired header` |
| Current opening journal deleted, account/GL evidence remains | UNAVAILABLE; current loss is not a reset | `a missing current opening journal is not treated as completed reset history` |
| Journal/chart/recovery lost, opening GL lines remain | UNAVAILABLE; metadata loss does not prove retirement | `retained opening GL lines cannot be mistaken for a completed reset` |
| Journal/chart/recovery and lines lost, opening snapshots remain | Independent snapshot GL 100,000; UNAVAILABLE | `retained opening GL snapshots cannot be mistaken for a completed reset` |
| Opening marked REVERSED, recovery lost | UNAVAILABLE, no false match | `a reversed opening with a lost recovery refuses instead of disappearing` |
| Duplicate live opening journals, recovery lost | UNAVAILABLE, no false match | `duplicate live opening journals refuse even with no surviving recovery` |
| Pending manual journal draft on declared opening control 1999 | UNAVAILABLE, PENDING_POSTINGS, empty totals, not complete | `a pending manualJournalDrafts on a declared opening control account refuses the comparison` |
| Pending opening draft on declared opening control 1999 | UNAVAILABLE, PENDING_POSTINGS, empty totals, not complete | `a pending openingBalanceDrafts on a declared opening control account refuses the comparison` |
| Stored margin amountDue NaN | Generic public error; raw conversion detail logged server-side | `a stored NaN produces a generic public error and logs conversion details only on the server` |
| Margin 3,000 from a direct-settled sale | GL 1240 3,000,000 = subledger 3,000,000; AVAILABLE; complete | `CONTROL: a fresh direct-settled sale reconciles - A/R subledger equals 1240` |
| Margin 3,000, receipt 1,000 | both sides 2,000,000 | `a partial margin receipt lowers the subledger and 1240 by the same amount` |
| Margin 3,000 plus supplier-borne cost 200, receipt 80 | both sides 3,200,000 then 3,120,000; recovery part 120,000 | `a supplier-borne cost recovery is part of the 1240 total, and its receipt lowers both sides` |
| Direction 1: receipt in the GL, not in the subledger | GL 2,000,000, subledger 3,000,000, difference -1,000,000, not reconciled | `a receipt posted to the GL but not recorded in the subledger is a difference` |
| Direction 2: receipt in the subledger, not in the GL | GL 3,000,000, subledger 2,000,000, difference +1,000,000, not reconciled | `a receipt recorded in the subledger but never posted to the GL is the reverse difference` |
| USD receivable 400.00 with no USD GL balance | USD shown with GL 0, subledger 40,000, not reconciled; JOD still reconciled | `a currency with subledger rows and no GL balance is a difference, not zero` |
| Receivable with no supplier identity | counted in the total and in the Unlinked bucket; a stored `supplierId` moves it to the linked part, and an id-less row with a real supplier name also counts as linked (SCRUM-868 P4a round 3) | `rows with no supplier identity fall in the Unlinked bucket and still count toward the total` |
| CANCELLED receivable, REVERSED recovery | contribute nothing to the subledger | `a CANCELLED receivable and a REVERSED recovery carry no balance` |
| Queued posting that touches 1240 | UNAVAILABLE, PENDING_POSTINGS, no currencies, not reconciled, not complete | `a queued posting that touches 1240 reports pending, not a difference` |
| Queued posting that cannot touch 1240 | AVAILABLE | `a queued posting that cannot touch 1240 does not block the comparison` |
| Pending draft on the 1240 account | UNAVAILABLE, PENDING_POSTINGS (a draft on another account does not block) | `a pending draft that touches the 1240 account reports pending` |
| More rows than the read limit | UNAVAILABLE, OVER_LIMIT, never a partial total | `more rows than the read limit is UNAVAILABLE (OVER_LIMIT), never a partial total` |
| A `toDate` in the past | UNAVAILABLE, AS_OF_DATE_UNSUPPORTED, not reconciled: a margin receipt carries no date, so the subledger cannot be rebuilt at that cutoff and comparing it with the GL at that cutoff would mix two dates | `a real 3,000 error is not reported reconciled at a past toDate` |
| Activity after the cutoff on a control account | UNAVAILABLE, ACTIVITY_AFTER_CUTOFF: the GL is read as of 	oDate but the subledger is current state, so a posting dated after the cutoff on 1240 (or on the account of an opening recovery in force) would hide or invent a difference. A post-dated sale cannot hide a lost row; a 	oDate that covers the post-dated line reconciles; an account that is not a control account does not block. The line's own ccountingDate governs, as in getPostedLines | `a post-dated sale cannot hide a lost row behind it` |
| A 	oDate that is not a finite number | UNAVAILABLE, INVALID_AS_OF_DATE for NaN, Infinity and -Infinity (.number() accepts them and NaN < now is false) | `NaN, Infinity and -Infinity are refused as UNAVAILABLE, never read as a cutoff` |
| More than 25 OPENING recoveries | UNAVAILABLE, OVER_LIMIT: each opening recovery costs a bounded but large number of reads, so the default caps (4,000 rows, 400 in-flight, 2,400 GL, 25 opening) are derived so the worst case stays under Convex's 32,000 documents scanned and 4,096 index ranges per function (https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits) | `more OPENING recoveries than can be verified within the per-function limits is UNAVAILABLE (OVER_LIMIT)` |
| GL side larger than the read limit | UNAVAILABLE, OVER_LIMIT: the GL read is scoped to the control account and every period, snapshot, line and entry it reads counts against the limit | `more control-account snapshot rows than the read limit is UNAVAILABLE (OVER_LIMIT), never a partial total` |
| Native cost recovery claimed by a POSTED opening draft | UNAVAILABLE, OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE (the check A/P applies to every payable) | `a native recovery that a POSTED opening draft claims as its result is UNAVAILABLE` |
| Permission and tenancy | caller without view:finance refused; a finance user of another organization refused | `is protected by finance read and scoped to the caller's tenant` |
| Report only | no row written; `accountingPeriods.ts` does not read the reconciliation (OQ-10) | `it only reports: nothing is written and period close does not read it` |

Known simplifications, stated rather than hidden: the subledger side is the current state of the rows, so the A/R reconciliation refuses a `toDate` in the past (UNAVAILABLE) rather than compare two cutoffs, and refuses a cutoff that precedes a posting on its control account (ACTIVITY_AFTER_CUTOFF; the check is conservative and may refuse where the post-dated line is unrelated to any subledger row, never the reverse); the A/P reconciliation still takes only its GL side as of `toDate`, so for native payables it can compare two cutoffs (reported on SCRUM-858, not changed here because period close passes `period.endDate`) and its GL read is not yet scoped to the control account; a pending outbox expense is treated as 1240-affecting whatever its cost bearer, so a queued ordinary expense makes the report UNAVAILABLE until it drains; "Unlinked" means a blank or "Unknown supplier" name because no supplier id exists yet (SCRUM-869 P2/P3).
## SCRUM-869-DUE-AGING

Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23883 (2026-10-29, OQ-1), recorded in Jira by the coordinator as the record of a chat ruling; it is not the owner's own text, and the digest in `regression/rulings.json` is the sha256 of the whole markdown body of that comment. A supplier obligation's due date is derived on the server and explained: the agreed date, else the ownership-model trigger read from recorded facts only, else "No due date", which is never invented and never overdue. Aging buckets are Current, 1-30, 31-60, 61-90 and 90+ days overdue, in calendar days of `Asia/Amman`. Disputed amounts are shown separately and stay in the gross outstanding.

Coordinator rulings of 2026-10-29 that fix the open points inside c23883 (the trigger table is `DUE_TRIGGERS` in `convex/utils/supplierDueDates.ts`, one entry per ownership model, so an owner answer changes one entry and its test):

- Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23906 (2026-10-29, given in chat and recorded in Jira by the coordinator; the digest covers the whole comment) replaces the first two coordinator defaults. "Completed delivery" is handing the car to the customer: a commission receivable is due on the recorded vehicle handover and nothing else, `handoverStatus` HANDED_OVER with its `vehicleHandoverAt`. `applications.registerVehicleHandover` writes both for every deal, CASH included (`finalizeDeal` refuses without it), so a CASH deal is read the same way; a sale recorded without a deal application has no handover record. A COMPLETED sale is not delivery.
- "Cleared proceeds" is the receipt of the financing from the financier: a THROUGH_DEALERSHIP payable on a FINANCED deal is due on the application's `disbursedAt`, counted only while `disbursedAmountMinor` is a positive amount (`confirmDisbursement` writes both; `returnFinanceDisbursementCheque` clears both; a requested or pending funding has neither). Rules `FINANCIER_FUNDING_RECORDED` and `FINANCIER_FUNDING_NOT_RECORDED`. The financier's payment to the supplier on the direct route (`supplierDisbursementConfirmedAt`) is a different payment and is never read. A CASH deal has no financier, so the ruling does not cover it: it keeps `CLEARED_PROCEEDS_NOT_RECORDED`, no due date, until the owner rules. An agreed date still wins over every trigger.
- The coordinator defaults below stand because the owner has not ruled on them:
- A cost recovery has an agreed date or none. An ON_ACCOUNT supplier's terms are not stored, so only `paymentDueDate` applies.
- `agreedDueDate` (optional epoch ms) was added to margin receivables and cost recoveries. No door writes it in this slice.

Practice and evidence boundary: the aging layout (days past the due date in 30-day bands, 90 read as "61-90" and "90+" as more than 90) is the usual receivables and payables aging convention, cited from general accounting knowledge. The zone is looked up through `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with the IANA identifier `Asia/Amman` (ECMA-402; IANA tz database), and the tests run against the real ICU data of the Node runtime for winter and summer 2026 (UTC+3) and for winter 2021 (UTC+2, before Jordan's October 2022 move to permanent UTC+3). No external source was fetched in this session. The Convex production runtime's ICU data was not checked, so the Amman day count is proven in `convex-test` on Node only.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Payable with an agreed `paymentDueDate` 10 days ago | rule AGREED_DATE, 10 days overdue, bucket 1-30, outstanding 9,500,000 minor | `an agreed paymentDueDate wins and ages by Amman calendar days` |
| THROUGH_DEALERSHIP payable, sale of unrecorded financing type and no application, no agreed date | No due date (CLEARED_PROCEEDS_NOT_RECORDED), never overdue | `THROUGH_DEALERSHIP payable with a completed sale but no agreed date: no due date, cleared proceeds not recorded` |
| FINANCED THROUGH_DEALERSHIP payable, financier funding recorded 20 days ago | rule FINANCIER_FUNDING_RECORDED, due on that instant, 20 days, bucket 1-30, outstanding 9,500,000 minor | `a financed deal whose financier funding is recorded: due on that instant and aged from it` |
| Financed payable, funding requested or pending (no `disbursedAt`) | No due date (FINANCIER_FUNDING_NOT_RECORDED), never overdue | `a financed deal with no funding received (requested or pending) has no due date and is never overdue` |
| Financed payable, funding reversed (cheque returned after clearing) | the cleared instant and amount are absent: No due date | `a funding that was reversed (cheque returned after clearing) is not received` |
| Financed payable, `disbursedAt` with no amount | not a received funding: No due date | `an instant with no recorded amount is not a received funding` |
| Financed payable, `disbursedAt` with a recorded amount of zero (legacy row) | not a received funding: No due date | `an instant with a recorded amount of zero is not a received funding (kills the > 0 to >= 0 mutant)` |
| Financed payable, only the supplier-direct disbursement recorded | not the dealership's funding: No due date | `the financier's payment to the supplier (direct route fact) is not the dealership's funding` |
| Financed payable, vehicle handed over but no funding | the handover does not stand in for funding: No due date | `a recorded vehicle handover does not stand in for funding` |
| Financed payable with funding and an agreed date | the agreed date wins | `an agreed paymentDueDate still wins over the funding instant` |
| Funded application of another organisation | read as a missing fact: No due date | `a funded application of another organisation is not evidence of this deal's funding` |
| CASH THROUGH_DEALERSHIP payable, even beside stray funding data | No due date (CLEARED_PROCEEDS_NOT_RECORDED), never overdue | `a CASH deal is outside the ruling: no due date, never overdue, even beside stray funding data` |
| CASH sale recorded without a deal application | No due date (CLEARED_PROCEEDS_NOT_RECORDED) | `a CASH sale recorded without a deal application has no due date either` |
| Payable with no sale and no agreed date | No due date (TERMS_NOT_RECORDED) | `an ON_ACCOUNT-style payable (no sale) with no agreed terms has no due date` |
| Agreed date 5 days ahead | Current, -5 days | `an agreed date in the future is Current, with a negative day count` |
| 20:59:59.999Z then 21:00:00.000Z on the due day | Current, 0 days; then 1 day overdue | `the Amman midnight edge: due that day is Current until 00:00 Amman, then 1 day overdue` |
| Disputed payable | disputed part equals the gross, both stay in the 31-60 bucket total | `a disputed payable is shown separately but stays in the gross outstanding and in its aging bucket` |
| PAID, CANCELLED payables | not outstanding | `paid and cancelled payables are not outstanding` |
| Financed handover recorded 70 days ago | rule DELIVERY_RECORDED, 70 days, bucket 61-90 | `financed delivery recorded: due on handover and aged from it` |
| COMPLETED sale, no application | No due date (DELIVERY_NOT_RECORDED) | `a COMPLETED sale is not delivery: no application means no due date` |
| HANDED_OVER without `vehicleHandoverAt` | No due date, never overdue | `HANDED_OVER with no handover timestamp is a missing fact, never overdue` |
| CASH deal, handover recorded 70 days ago | rule DELIVERY_RECORDED, 70 days, bucket 61-90 | `a CASH deal records the same handover and is due on it` |
| CASH sale completed with no deal application | No due date (DELIVERY_NOT_RECORDED) | `a CASH sale completed without a deal application has no handover record: not delivery` |
| Funded financed deal, no recorded handover | funding is not delivery: No due date (DELIVERY_NOT_RECORDED) | `a funded financed deal with no recorded handover is still not delivered` |
| Receivable with `agreedDueDate` and a handover | the agreed date wins | `an agreedDueDate on the receivable beats the delivery trigger` |
| Disputed receivable, 100 days past agreed date | gross kept, disputed shown, bucket 90+ | `a disputed receivable keeps its outstanding in gross and reports it as disputed` |
| Application or sale of another organisation | read as a missing fact: No due date | `a sale from another organisation is not read as a fact of this one`; `a receivable whose saleId is another organisation's sale reads no delivery from it` |
| Cancelled or fully received receivable | not outstanding | `a cancelled receivable and a fully received one are not outstanding` |
| Status PAID or RECOVERED with open amounts | the open amount is still listed | `a status that says settled while the amounts say open does not hide the open money` |
| Recovery with no agreed date | No due date (COLLECTIBILITY_NOT_RECORDED), 200,000 minor | `no agreed date: no due date, collectibility not recorded` |
| Recovery, agreed date 95 days ago, 50,000 recovered | 95 days, bucket 90+, outstanding 150,000 | `an agreed date ages the outstanding minor units` |
| RECOVERED, REVERSED recoveries | not outstanding | `recovered and reversed recoveries are not outstanding` |
| Five rows, page size 2 | `complete` false, false, then true on the last page | `pagination is bounded and \`complete\` is only true on the last page` |
| Page size 100,000 over 101 rows | 100 rows, not complete | `a requested page size above the cap is cut to the cap` |
| Another organisation | its rows never returned; calling with its id is refused | `another organisation's obligations are never returned` |
| Caller without view:finance | refused | `a member without view:finance is refused` |
| Caller with view:finance and without view:cost_price (SCRUM-901, SCRUM-886 class: a SOURCED car's supplier entitlement is its acquisition cost) | refused for PAYABLE, RECEIVABLE and RECOVERY, naming `view:cost_price`; no rows, no totals | `PAYABLE: a role with view:finance but without view:cost_price is refused (SCRUM-901)`; `RECEIVABLE: …`; `RECOVERY: …` |
| Caller with both grants (positive control) | reads the list | `a role holding both view:finance and view:cost_price still reads the list (SCRUM-901 positive control)` |
| Caller with view:cost_price and without view:finance (negative control) | refused naming `view:finance` | `view:cost_price without view:finance is still refused (SCRUM-901 negative control)` |
| Stored due date of 1e20, -1e20, NaN or Infinity (outside the JavaScript Date range of +-8.64e15 ms, or not a number) | the list is refused with the named data fault `not a renderable instant`; the row is never shown as a date and never as "No due date" (a screen that silently dropped money would be worse) | `a stored due date of 1e20, -1e20, NaN or Infinity is refused by name, never shown as a date or as no date` |
| Stored due date of exactly 8.64e15 ms (the last instant a Date can hold) | a date: rule AGREED_DATE, bucket Current | `the last instant a Date can hold is a date and is shown` |
| Caller-supplied day | `nowMs` decides the aging (a day later reads one day older); a value more than a day from the server clock, or not a whole number, is refused; a call without it is refused | `aging follows the supplied nowMs, and a clock far from the server's is refused`, `a call without nowMs is refused rather than silently reading the clock` |

Known simplifications, stated rather than hidden: `complete` is per family and per page walk (a caller must walk to the last page; totals are for the page only); a payable's ownership model is read from its sale's `supplierSettlementRoute` (no sale means OWNED ON_ACCOUNT); alerts (S8) are a separate section.
## SCRUM917-deals-list-net-profit

SCRUM-917 (owner rich-row direction, 2026-11-05): each Deals list row reads its car photo, consignment flag, step rail, financed selling price and net profit from `dealListRow.figures`, which composes the deal screen's own queries (`sales.dealCockpit`; `applications.dealCockpit` plus `dealOverview.financedDealOverview`). The net profit is the cockpit's headline, never recomputed; it is gated on `view:cost_price` in the list query itself, in front of the cockpit's own gate (SCRUM-902, R-PERMISSION SCRUM-760#c22465). Tests in `convex/dealListRow.test.ts`.

| Caller and deal | Expected | Test |
|---|---|---|
| view:sales + view:finance, no view:cost_price; cash and financed, SOURCED and STOCK | `netProfitMinor` null, `netProfitReason` `CostAccessRequired`; neither the figure nor its operands on the wire | `scenario deals-list-row-withholds-net-profit: …`; `deals list financed=%s %s: net profit is null without view:cost_price and the cockpit figure with it` |
| view:sales + view:finance + view:cost_price; same four kinds | `netProfitMinor` equals the cockpit's headline net profit | `scenario deals-list-row-serves-the-cockpit-net-profit: …` |
| Owner | the cockpit's figure | `deals list financed=%s %s: the owner reads the cockpit's figure` |
| view:sales only | step rail served; no price, no profit (each with a reason), no photo | `deals list financed=%s %s: a sales-only caller sees the step but no money` |
| Financed deal with view:finance | selling price equals the overview's `customerSalePrice`; a SOURCED car reads `consigned: true` | `deals list: the financed row's selling price is the cockpit's customer sale price` |
| Another organisation, sale and application | its own org id with a foreign row reads null; the foreign org id is refused | `deals list financed=%s: a caller from another organization cannot read a row` |
| Foreign, missing or deleted sale/application | complete response is null, before any downstream query; a sale's soft-delete flag is honoured; applications have no soft-delete field, so their deletion case is a hard delete | `SCRUM-917 financed=$financed $state: local ownership refuses before delegation` |
| view:sales + view:cost_price, no view:finance; sale and application | complete response keeps the cockpit's non-financial facts, null amounts and currency, and `FinanceAccessRequired` for profit; a financed price has the same reason | `SCRUM-917 financed=%s: finance gate with cost access preserves the complete public response` |

Round 2 verification is local serial `convex-test` evidence, not Convex platform, concurrency or production certification. The row UI tests in `components/deals/DealsListView.queryFailure.test.tsx` cover missing-endpoint and ordinary thrown errors, loading, null and success in EN/AR for cards and table rows, plus failure followed by an org switch. A failed figures subscription renders the existing localized unavailable state without removing the list.

## SCRUM-869-DUE-ALERTS

Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23883 (2026-10-29, OQ-1), the coordinator's Jira record of a chat ruling, not the owner's own text; the digest in `regression/rulings.json` covers the whole markdown body of that comment. Supplier obligations (payables, margin receivables, cost recoveries) raise in-app alerts through the existing `notifications` table: 3 days before the due date, on the due date, at 7 days overdue, then weekly. Disputed obligations and obligations with no due date are never alerted. Recipients are resolved on the server: the Owner, members holding `manage:finance` (Finance Manager) and members holding `view:finance` (authorised accounting staff), never an offboarding member and never a role of another organisation. SCRUM-901: the cost-derived `amount` goes only to the Owner and to roles that hold both `view:finance` and `view:cost_price` (the `STATEMENT_PERMISSIONS` of the supplier statement and `supplierDueAging.list`; OWASP A01:2021); every other recipient gets the same alert (kind, currency, due date, days overdue) with no `amount` key, and the English and Arabic sentences have a `_MessageNoAmount` variant. Idempotency keys carry no amount.

Design facts the cells rely on:

- The alert row is inserted directly. `utils/notifications.dispatch` is not used because it also fans out to email, WhatsApp and push; this ruling is in-app only.
- Idempotency is one `commandIdempotency` record per obligation, step, occurrence and recipient (`supplier-due-alert`, key `<kind>:<id>:<step>:<occurrence>:<userId>`), written in the same transaction as that recipient's alert row. Per recipient, because the fan-out for one obligation can span several transactions (PR #716 round 2).
- The cron `supplier-due-alerts` (05:30 UTC, 08:30 Amman) pages organisations, 20 per page, and schedules one worker mutation per organisation. A worker transaction reads one page (50 rows) of one obligation kind and at most `floor(400 / alerts)` memberships, sends at most 400 alerts (at most three documents written each: the notification, and the idempotency record's insert and patch, so 1,200 at the budget against Convex's 16,000 limit), and schedules its own continuation: the same obligation page with the next membership cursor, then the next obligation page, then the next kind. The budget of 400 is chosen from the documented limits, not measured: `convex-test` has no write meter. The worker does not catch its own failure: a fault that can be seen from a read (an unsupported currency, unconvertible amounts) is skipped with a log line and counted in `skipped` before any write, and any other fault throws, so the mutation rolls back whole and its continuation is dropped with it (a caught error would commit the writes before it and drop the rest of the organisation). Recipients are the active, non-disabled, non-impersonation members of the organisation's memberships, read one bounded membership page at a time rather than the first 1,000.
- Catch-up (coordinator ruling 2026-10-29, closing the missed-day gap): each run finds the latest schedule step whose Amman day has been reached (-3, 0, 7, then 14, 21, ... counted as occurrences) and sends it only when that step's idempotency record does not exist. A day the cron or an organisation worker missed is made up on the next run, once; earlier skipped steps are never sent as a burst, and a row that gains a due date late gets the one step it has reached.

- Stored dates are validated where the worker reads them (PR #716 round 3). A stored date that is not finite, or lies outside the JavaScript Date range of +-8.64e15 ms (ECMAScript TimeClip), cannot be formatted: `Intl.DateTimeFormat` throws a `RangeError`, which is not a `ConvexError`, so it used to escape the unreadable-row skip and starve the organisation. `renderableDate` in `convex/utils/supplierObligationDue.ts` now checks `vehicleHandoverAt`, `disbursedAt` (only when funding is counted as received), `paymentDueDate` and both `agreedDueDate` fields as the facts are built, and throws the named data fault, which the worker's existing unreadable-row path skips, logs and counts. It is not a catch-all: any other error still propagates.
- A worker failure leaves a durable signal (PR #716 round 3). A mutation that throws rolls back every write, including any record of its own failure, and drops its continuation, so the failure cannot be recorded from inside it. The scheduler and the cron run `runOrgSupplierDueAlerts`, an internal action that calls the worker, and on an error records the failure from a separate transaction (`recordSupplierDueAlertFailure`) and rethrows, so the scheduled function still fails visibly and is not retried. The record is one `cronHeartbeats` row (existing table and `by_job_ranAt` index, no schema change) under the per-organisation job name `supplier-due-alerts:<orgId>`, `success: false`, with a `detail` of at most 1,000 characters naming the organisation, kind, obligation cursor, recipient cursor and the error message. Repeated failures update that row in place while no intervening worker transaction succeeds. A later transaction that succeeds inserts one success row only when the newest heartbeat is a failure, which supersedes it; a clean run writes nothing. If each day's retry commits an earlier page and then fails on a later page, it adds two rows that day: one success and one new failure. `supplierDueAlerts.listSupplierDueAlertFailures` (super admin only, paginated) lists the organisations whose newest heartbeat is a failure. The per-organisation names are deliberately outside the fixed `CRON_HEARTBEAT_JOBS` list that drives the cron-status screen.

Evidence boundary: `convex-test` only. It has no runtime read limits, no OCC and no real scheduler clock, so bounded reads and the live cron are not proven here. The page-size constant (50) is not observable in these tests, because a larger page would still alert every row.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Payable due in 3 days | one in-app alert per recipient (Owner, manage:finance, view:finance), type due; `amount` only for the Owner and view:finance + view:cost_price, amount `9500.000` JOD | `an obligation due in 3 days alerts each authorised recipient once, in-app` |
| Recipient with `manage:finance` only (SCRUM-901) | the alert, with `kind`, `currency`, `dueDate`, `daysOverdue` and no `amount` key (not 0, not null) | `a manage:finance-only recipient gets the alert with no amount key` |
| Recipient with `view:finance` without `view:cost_price`, alone or with `manage:finance` (SCRUM-901) | the same alert with no `amount` key | `a view:finance-only recipient gets the alert with no amount key, and so does view:finance with manage:finance` |
| Recipient with `manage:finance` and `view:cost_price` but not `view:finance` (SCRUM-901) | the alert with no `amount` key: `manage:finance` makes it a recipient, but the amount needs both statement permissions | `a manage:finance + view:cost_price recipient without view:finance gets no amount key` |
| Recipient with `view:finance` and `view:cost_price`, and the Owner (SCRUM-901) | the alert with `amount` `7654.000` for a 7,654 JOD payable | `a view:finance + view:cost_price recipient and the owner get the amount` |
| Overdue step at day 7 (SCRUM-901) | the redacted recipient keeps `daysOverdue` 7 and gets no `amount` key; the Owner gets `7654.000` | `the overdue step is redacted the same way and keeps its days overdue` |
| Sweep on days -4..21 around one due date | one alert per step as it is reached: days -3, 0, 7, 14 and 21; none in between | `a run sends only the latest step reached, once, as the days pass` |
| Cron skipped the due date | the due alert is sent on the next run, once | `a cron that skipped the due date sends the due alert on the next run` |
| Gap past day 7 | the day-7 alert is sent once; the skipped earlier steps are not | `a gap past day 7 sends the day-7 alert once and none of the earlier skipped steps` |
| Row first seen late | the single latest step | `an obligation first seen late gets the single latest step, not the skipped ones` |
| Weekly occurrence missed by 2 days | sent once; the next is on time | `a weekly occurrence missed by two days is sent once, and the next one on time` |
| Receivable with an agreed date, cost recovery with an agreed date | both alert on their schedule | `a receivable with recorded delivery and a cost recovery with an agreed date alert on their schedule` |
| Same-day re-run, three sweeps | no duplicate | `running the sweep twice on the same day does not duplicate any alert` |
| Two obligations, then a later step | separate alerts, and the later step alerts again | `two obligations alert separately, and a later step of the same obligation alerts again` |
| Sweep given an earlier day than the clock | the given day decides | `the sweep uses the day it is given, not the clock of whichever continuation runs it` |
| Two organisations | each alerts only its own members | `an organisation's alerts reach only its own members`, `two organisations each alert their own members for their own obligations` |
| Sales-only member, offboarding member, foreign role | no alert | `a member without finance access, and an offboarding member, get nothing`, `a role that belongs to another organisation grants nothing` |
| Plan without accounting | no alert | `an organisation whose plan has no accounting is not alerted` |
| No due date (any kind), disputed, paid or cancelled | no alert | `no due date is never alerted, however old the sale`, `a disputed obligation is not alerted`, `settled and cancelled obligations are not alerted` |
| Channels | nothing scheduled to email, WhatsApp or push | `the sweep schedules no email, WhatsApp or push delivery` |
| 53 obligations; 22 organisations | all alerted; all swept | `more obligations than one page are all alerted`, `more organisations than one batch are all swept` |
| An unreadable row (unsupported currency) | skipped and reported (`skipped`), nothing thrown | `an unreadable row is skipped and reported, not thrown` |
| One unreadable payable and one unreadable recovery among 54 good rows over two pages and three kinds | the 54 good rows are all alerted (162 alerts), the two unreadable ones are logged once each and never alerted | `one unreadable row does not starve the rest of its page, its later pages or its later kinds` |
| An organisation with an unreadable row beside a good one | the good organisation still alerts | `one organisation with an unreadable row does not stop the others` |
| Fault injected on the 2nd notification insert | the worker throws; no notification and no idempotency record remain; the retry sends all 6 alerts once | `a fault after earlier writes rolls the whole worker back, and the retry sends everything once` |
| Same worker call replayed | `alerted` is 3 then 0: only new sends are counted | `alerted counts only new sends: a replay reports zero` |
| 603 recipients, 3 obligations | no transaction writes more than 1,500 documents; each recipient gets exactly one alert per obligation (1,809); a replay sends none | `600 extra recipients and 3 obligations: no transaction writes past the ceiling, and each recipient is told once per obligation` |
| Any one of the five stored date fields set to 1e20, -1e20, NaN, Infinity or -Infinity | the row is skipped and counted (`alerted` 0, `skipped` 1), logged once, nothing thrown, no alert | `every unrenderable value in every stored date field is skipped and counted, never thrown` |
| Date range edge: 8.64e15 ms and 8.64e15 + 1 ms | the first is a date, the second is the one skipped row | `the last instant a Date can hold is still a date, and one millisecond past it is not` |
| One unrenderable row of every date field, then 52 good payables, a receivable and a recovery, and a second organisation | the good rows are all alerted (162), five unreadable rows logged, the other organisation alerted (3) | `bad dates in every field starve nothing: later rows, pages, kinds and other organisations are still alerted` |
| An ordinary programming error from a notification write | still thrown, not swallowed | `an ordinary programming error is still not swallowed` |
| The dispatcher runs and the first organisation worker transaction fails on its first send | no notifications or idempotency rows, one failure heartbeat for that organisation naming the fault | `the dispatcher records a failure heartbeat when the first organisation worker transaction fails` |
| A good payable followed by a corrupt non-string sale reference; the fact read inside readRow throws a non-ConvexError | the worker error propagates through the action, no notifications or idempotency rows, one failure heartbeat naming the organisation and error | `a non-ConvexError during a fact read propagates, rolls back the worker and records a failure heartbeat` |
| 100 payables, 23 recipients | 2,300 alerts, each (obligation, recipient) once, 23 distinct recipients; the recipient cursor advances inside an obligation page and is undefined at the next page; at most 12 transactions; a replay sends 0 and walks the same chain | `100 payables x 23 recipients: every (obligation, recipient) once, the cursor resets per page, the chain ends, a replay sends nothing` |
| The worker fails on the first send of the second page | the first page's 150 alerts stay, the failing page wrote nothing, one failure heartbeat with `success` false naming organisation, kind, cursor and error, at most 1,000 characters | `a failed worker records one failure heartbeat naming the organisation, kind and cursor, and rolls its writes back` |
| A failure, then a later successful run; a clean organisation | heartbeats read false then true (superseded), 3 alerts sent on recovery; the clean organisation has no heartbeat | `a later successful run supersedes the failure, and a clean run writes no heartbeat at all` |
| The same organisation's first worker transaction fails on three days, with no intervening success | one failure row, its time the third day's; this does not cover retries that commit an earlier page before failing later | `repeated first-transaction failures update one failure row while no intervening transaction succeeds` |
| One organisation fails, another succeeds | the failing organisation's heartbeat stays failed; the other has none and its 3 alerts are sent | `one organisation's failure is recorded against it alone, and another organisation's success does not clear it` |
| A 50,000-character error message | one row, detail at most 1,000 characters | `the failure detail is bounded however long the error message is` |
| Super admin lists failures: one failing, one recovered, one never failed organisation | only the failing organisation, with its detail; an unauthenticated caller is refused | `listSupplierDueAlertFailures names the organisations whose newest supplier-due-alert heartbeat is a failure (SCRUM-869 round 3)` |
| 1,005 memberships, the last the only late finance member | that member is alerted | `a finance member who is the 1,005th membership is still alerted` |
| Disabled user; super-admin impersonation membership | neither is alerted | `a disabled user and a super-admin impersonation membership are not alerted` |

## Implementation ledger on this branch

The owned configured-financier no-deposit cancellation row drives the public cancellation door before any financier receipt. It checks a CANCELLED finance-company invoice and sale, a REVERSED sale event, literal inverse 1210/4100/5100/1400 journal lines, and zero public open-period trial balance and income. A salesperson with ordinary sales and finance-application access but without `cancel:closed_deal` is refused before the reversal, with all schema tables unchanged. It does not cover cancellation after financier payment or a closed-period reversal.

The SOURCED configured-financier through-dealership cancellation row uses that same public door before any company receipt or supplier payment. It separately checks that the 12,500,000 finance-company invoice and 10,000,000 supplier payable cancel, the sale event reverses, literal inverse 1210/2400/4170 lines post, and the public open-period trial balance and income return to zero. It does not prove safe unwind after either party has been paid.

The SOURCED configured-financier direct-route partial-collection row uses two public supplier receipts of 1,000,000 and 1,500,000 against a 2,500,000 margin claim. It checks the PARTIALLY_PAID then PAID subledger states and receipt sequence, one posted collection event per actual receipt, literal bank/claim journals at each step, and the public trial balance after each. An exact-key retry, a 1,501,000 overpayment against the 1,500,000 remainder, and cancellation after partial collection leave every schema table unchanged. This row does not establish a public correction or reversal path for supplier money already received.

`convex/scrum486Certification.test.ts` currently contains twenty-seven passing **harness-only** rows: owned configured-financier sale with no deposit or contribution and its separate pre-receipt cancellation; owned configured-financier sale with a 200,000 held first payment and a 1,375,000 dealer contribution; foreign-application refusal at the financier forward and receipt doors with a full-table no-change assertion; SOURCED configured-financier sales through the dealership and direct to the supplier, each with no deposit or contribution, plus through-dealership cancellation before either party pays and direct-route cancellation before collection, supplier collection, partial then final supplier collection, and post-collection cancellation refusal; owned CASH full-price-deposit sale recognition and cancellation with literal inverse journals (the deposit returns to HELD); SOURCED CASH full-price-deposit and held/applied-deposit agent sale recognition; SOURCED CASH full-price-deposit sale cancellation and applied-deposit cancellation followed by refund; salesperson refusal to cancel a completed owned or SOURCED CASH sale; owned held/applied deposit at recognition; applied deposit followed by sale cancellation and an authorized refund; held deposit refunded before sale; held deposit forfeited before sale; salesperson refusal to create a deposit and to refund one; salesperson refusal to create a deposit and to forfeit one; cross-tenant quote/deposit/sale ID refusal; new LEASE quote refusal with full-row no-change; and new INTERNAL_INSTALLMENT quote refusal with full-row no-change.

The financed sale rows drive quotation, application approval, handover, expected payment, legal invoice and finalization through public mutations. The owned and sourced-through-dealership rows assert the full 12,500,000 receivable's `payerType` is FINANCE_COMPANY with the exact finance-company ID, 1210 AR rather than 1200 customer AR, literal journals, and the public trial balance. The sourced direct-to-supplier row instead asserts no finance-company receivable: the supplier owes the dealership only its 2,500,000 margin on account 1240 and in the supplier-receivable subledger, against 4170 agency revenue. On the through-dealership row, a `customerId` retained for deal provenance does not change the legal payer. The held-payment row uses an opening 87.4% LTV policy: 12,500,000 × 87.4% = 10,925,000 funded; 12,500,000 − 10,925,000 − 200,000 = 1,375,000 dealer contribution. It asserts Dr 4180 / Cr 2220 for that contribution, Dr 2100 / Cr 2220 for the held customer money, Dr 2220 / Cr 1110 on the 1,575,000 forward, and Dr 1110 / Cr 1210 on the full 12,500,000 financier receipt. Identical-key retries of forward and receipt leave every schema table unchanged. In the held-payment row, a salesperson with sales edit and finance view rights but without `confirm:finance_disbursement` is refused at both the 1,575,000 forward and 12,500,000 financier receipt doors; each denial leaves every schema table unchanged. The public income statement reads the owned no-deposit financed sale as 12,500,000 revenue, 10,000,000 COGS and 2,500,000 net income. With the held first payment and dealer contribution, it reads 12,500,000 vehicle revenue less 1,375,000 contra-revenue in account 4180 = 11,125,000 net revenue, 10,000,000 COGS and 1,125,000 net income, unchanged after the forward and full financier receipt. These are open-period harness reads, not closed-period or production proof. The financed rows do not yet cover partial/returned finance-company receipts, forward void/replacement, commission, handover spending or a completed sale cancellation after its required manual corrections.

The salesperson in the deposit rows has ordinary `view:sales` and `create:sales` permissions, but no money permissions; both rejected calls leave every schema table unchanged. Separate owned and SOURCED CASH rows grant `edit:sales` too, then verify that lacking `approve:requests` still prevents cancellation and leaves every schema table, journal and sale event unchanged. The CASH rows assert literal account-code/JOD-minor-unit debits and credits, event status and balance. Every CASH row first pays the full 12,500 by deposit (SCRUM-802), so the sale's 12,500,000 customer receivable is settled to zero by the deposit application. The four successful SOURCED CASH rows confirm a 12,500,000 customer receivable, a 10,000,000 supplier liability in account 2400 and the matching supplier-payable subledger, and only 2,500,000 agency revenue in account 4170. The held/applied-deposit row additionally asserts that the full 12,500,000 deposit settles customer AR to zero, discharges the 2100 liability, and leaves the supplier's entitlement whole. The full-deposit cancellation rows check literal inverse entries, a reversed sale event, a cancelled supplier payable, the reversed deposit application and the deposit held again (public trial balance: 1100 and 2100 only). The applied-deposit cancellation row checks that the supplier payable cancels, the sale and deposit application reverse, the deposit returns to HELD, and an authorized CASH refund clears the remaining 1100/2100 balances. None has owned vehicle revenue, COGS or inventory release. The separate SOURCED configured-financier no-deposit row confirms through-dealership routing: 12,500,000 finance-company AR on 1210, 10,000,000 supplier payable on 2400 and in its subledger, 2,500,000 agency revenue on 4170, zero COGS, and no owned-stock revenue or inventory line. It checks the public trial balance and income statement in an open period. The separate direct-to-supplier row asserts 1240/4170 only, no gross finance-company receivable or supplier payable, and the same 2,500,000 open-period agency income. Public confirmation that the financier paid the supplier records 12,500,000 as external settlement advice without posting any dealership journal or settling the supplier margin claim; both the financed-deal cancellation door and the sale-update door then refuse, leaving every schema table unchanged. The direct-route pre-collection cancellation row uses the public financed-deal door, cancels the claim, marks the sale event REVERSED, posts the literal 1240/4170 inverse and returns public open-period trial balance and income to zero. The separate direct-route supplier receipt row collects the 2,500,000 claim via the public mutation, asserts Dr 1110 bank / Cr 1240 supplier receivable, PAID subledger, zero claim balance, an unchanged full-table snapshot on identical-key retry, and a specific refusal to cancel the CLOSED application after supplier money arrived. It does not cover a manual correction that would permit cancellation, supplier payment on the through route, or other post-settlement endings. The public trial-balance consumer additionally reports exact account-normal net values for the full-deposit sales, the sourced held/applied sale, the re-held deposit after cancellation, and zero after refund; revenue and liability are credit-positive in that consumer. These are open-period reads, not closed-period snapshot checks. The public income statement additionally reports owned CASH revenue 12,500,000, COGS 10,000,000 and net income 2,500,000; SOURCED CASH agency revenue and net income 2,500,000 with zero COGS; and zero revenue, COGS and net income after each no-deposit sale cancellation. These literals certify only those open-period cells. The owned cancellation row verifies the original sale event reverses, the deposit receipt remains posted while its liability is held again, and a separate refund event clears that liability. It does not cover a paid sale or closed-period reversal. The suite does not yet prove handover cost, commission, full P&L/dashboard/collections parity, all default/custom role decisions or foreign-ID refusal on the remaining money writers. It does not satisfy the required matrix or real-backend release gate. The two retired-mode rows test the current product refusal, not historical lease/installment records.

The public 5,000,000 partial-financier confirmation currently refuses with no schema-table changes. This is a fail-closed characterization, **not** certification of the 5,000,000 + 7,500,000 receipt lifecycle required above. A successful partial or returned receipt remains unavailable under SCRUM-814, which blocks completion of this matrix cell.

A separate owned-CASH tenant-boundary row creates a held deposit in tenant A, then uses an authorized owner identity from tenant B to attempt deposit creation with A's quote ID and refund with A's deposit ID. Both public calls refuse with the specific foreign-row reason and leave a full snapshot of every schema table unchanged; tenant A's 200,000 held liability remains. This covers those two deposit writers, not all finance or sale writers and not real-platform concurrency.

### SCRUM-760 unwind and cost-correction rows (harness only)

Owner ruling: both workflows are needed for the pilot, so they are enabled cells. Every row drives public mutations only (the one exception is U-REFUND's positive customer credit, which only the pilot-dark helpers can create), compares the exact journal multiset (account, currency, minor units, original or reversal status) to literals written in the test, compares the full content of every table before and after each refusal, and is attacked two ways: each literal expectation is mutated line by line (dropped line, one minor unit, wrong account, flipped status; every mutant must be rejected by the comparator), and the production guard is flipped where the harness can do so (payout switch, pending-disposition availability guard). Other guards were checked by temporarily removing them from production code.

| Row | Scenario | Literal expectation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| U-RET (RETAIN_CREDIT and REFUND with zero credit) | Owned configured-financier deal: car bought for 10,000 cash, 200 held deposit, 1,375 dealer contribution, 1,575 forwarded, 12,500 received into one bank, 250 manual commission accrued. Unwind start, forward return, finish. | Start posts nothing. Forward return posts the mirror Dr 1110 / Cr 2220 1,575,000 and bank returns to 12,500,000. Finish mirrors the sale (1210, 4100, 5100, 1400, 4180, 2220 twice, 2100), the 12,500,000 receipt (1110 / 1210) and the commission (6100 / 2300); originals REVERSED, mirrors POSTED. Net books: 1100 -9,800,000, 1400 10,000,000, 2100 credit 200,000; bank 0; income statement zero. Deposit HELD and active, application REVERSED, SCRUM-712 pending 200,000 PENDING, car IN_INSPECTION at cost 10,000; moving it to AVAILABLE is refused until the deposit decision. Late receipt, forward, finalize, second unwind and commission edits are refused with nothing written. Refusals: each single missing authority, foreign tenant, early finish, wrong rail. |
| U-RET exit | The same deal, then refund (Dr 2100 / Cr 1100 200,000) or forfeit (Dr 2100 / Cr 4200 200,000) by an authorized approver. | Pending decision leaves PENDING, the car may return to AVAILABLE; income other 0 or 200,000. A salesperson cannot release the deposit. |
| U-RET x cost correction | The returned car's cost is corrected by a cash refund. | GL 1400 equals purchasePrice before and after (10,000,000 then 9,500,000). |
| U-RET closed period | The deal is sold and paid in a prior year, that period is closed through the public close, and the unwind runs in the next year. | The prior period's stored account snapshots and the public prior-year trial balance and income statement are unchanged; all 14 reversal lines are dated in the current period and carry it in its snapshots. |
| U-ABANDON | Abandon directly after start and again after the forward return, then complete a third unwind. | Journals stay at the paid or forward-returned state, only the unwind row, audit log and idempotency record change, both authorities and tenant are enforced, the lock is released. |
| U-REFUND | `finishDealUnwind` REFUND with positive active invoice credit, with and without payout fields, BANK and CASH, and RETAIN_CREDIT with payout fields. | `CUSTOMER_CREDIT_PAYOUT_DISABLED`, every table unchanged; `FORBIDDEN` for MANAGE_FINANCE first. RETAIN_CREDIT without payout finishes: retained 3,000 minor units, 2110 credit 10,000 conserved, no RETAINED_CREDIT_REFUNDED event. |
| LREF | Legacy collection REFUND, public and internal doors (SCRUM-760 ruling 11, c23812). Public: `collections.requestApproval` requestType REFUND; `collections.respondToApproval` APPROVED on a REFUND request, including a request created before the switch and a stored replay of an approval already APPROVED. Internal: `accountingLedger.post` eventType COLLECTION_REFUND, the `hookCollectionRefund` producer, `subledger.recordPayment` direction OUT with a customer, `subledger.reverseAllocationMutation`. Not a required pilot route. | `LEGACY_COLLECTION_REFUND_PILOT_DISABLED` from `convex/utils/legacyCollectionRefundContainment.ts`, every table and the scheduler unchanged. A request refused at creation is never inserted; a request created before the switch stays PENDING when its approval is refused; a stored replay of an already APPROVED request is refused with the request left as it was. Rejecting a REFUND request, RESCHEDULE, CANCEL_RECEIVABLE, other ledger event types, customer IN payments and the cancel, unwind and cheque-return allocation reversal still work. A refund event queued before the switch is NOT blocked: it records a payout that already happened, so it posts (nothing dropped or held). Expected-refusal cells in `convex/legacyCollectionRefundContainment.test.ts`, one scenario record each under `regression/scenarios/money/`; harness-only, real-backend replay still owed (SCRUM-762). |
| U-SOURCED | SOURCED car financed through the dealership: 1210 12,500,000 / 2400 10,000,000 / 4170 2,500,000, then the 12,500,000 receipt. | All reversed, supplier payable CANCELLED, no forward step applicable, books and bank zero, car IN_INSPECTION. Direct-to-supplier deals (1240 / 4170 claim) refuse to start an unwind. |
| CC-CASH | 12,500 cash purchase corrected to 9,800 by CASH_REFUND, then sold after a full-price deposit (the SCRUM-802 pilot rule); cash +2,700,000, no receivable. | Dr 1100 / Cr 1400 2,700,000, purchasePrice 9,800, one cashbook IN of 2,700, later COGS 9,800,000 and margin 2,700,000; repeat refused NO_CHANGE; a SOLD car is refused COST_CORRECTION_SOLD. Refusals include missing VIEW_COST_PRICE, wrong type, restatement in an open period and a foreign tenant. |
| CC-AP | 12,500 purchase on account corrected to 11,900 as a supplier invoice error. | Dr 2400 / Cr 1400 600,000, payable amountDue 11,900, no cashbook row; a cash refund type is refused. |
| CC-RESTATE | A purchase in a closed prior year restated to 9,800. | Dr 3100 / Cr 1400 2,700,000 dated now; the closed period's snapshots, trial balance and income statement are unchanged; no profit effect. |
| CC-SOURCED | Correction of a consigned car. | `COST_CORRECTION_SOURCED`, nothing written. |
| U-RET CASH remittance | The same deal with the finance company's 12,500 received as cash (no bank), then unwound. | Receipt reverses Dr 1100 / Cr 1210 mirrored; a bank-transfer finish and cash finish without acknowledgement both refuse; bank balance -1,575,000 after the deal and 0 after the unwind. |
| U-RET / CC period not open | Today's period is closed (public close) before the forward return, before the finish, or before a correction. | `DEAL_UNWIND_PERIOD_NOT_OPEN` or `COST_CORRECTION_NOT_POSTABLE_NOW`, every table unchanged. |
| CC-UP | 12,500 cash purchase corrected up to 13,000. | Dr 1400 / Cr 1100 500,000, one cashbook OUT of 500. |

| CC-AP VENDOR_CREDIT | 12,500 on-account car credited to 11,000; then a partly paid payable. | Dr 2400 / Cr 1400 1,500,000, payable 11,000; after a 1,000 payment both AP types refuse `COST_CORRECTION_PAYABLE_NOT_ADJUSTABLE`. |

A repeated (fresh-key) cancel of an already-reversed deal changes nothing but its idempotency record: the deposit hold the unwind reinstated stays active (asserted in U-RET). The forward return can be re-recorded after an abandon (U-ABANDON).

### SCRUM-760 finance-company cheque return cells

`applications.returnFinanceDisbursementCheque` is part of the pilot (owner ruling 2026-10-19: the block was declined, the door stays on). Four rows in `convex/scrum486Certification.test.ts` drive it on an owned configured-financier deal whose 12,500,000 receipt was a CHEQUE that cleared into a named bank (opening literals: sale Dr 1210 / Cr 4100 12,500,000, Dr 5100 / Cr 1400 10,000,000, receipt Dr 1110 / Cr 1210 12,500,000). The first row is the certified happy path; the others are refusals and the deferred-reversal state:

- **Return by the bank.** An authorized owner returns the cleared cheque with a valid reason and key. The receipt entry becomes REVERSED and an exact inverse (Dr 1210 / Cr 1110 12,500,000) is POSTED, so the journal holds the eight literal lines and balances; the public trial balance returns to the pre-receipt 1210 12,500,000, 1400 -10,000,000, 4100 12,500,000, 5100 10,000,000; the named bank's book balance goes from 12,500,000 to 0; the financier receivable is OPEN at 12,500,000 again with its payment VOIDED and no ACTIVE allocation; the deal stays CLOSED, expects the next receipt (`disbursementVersion` 2, no `disbursedAt`) and the cheque is RETURNED with `returnedAfterClearing`; one audit row names the application. A before/after snapshot of every schema table shows exactly these tables changed and no others: accountBalanceSnapshots, accountingEvents, bankBalanceAggregateItems, bankBalanceGrossShards, canonicalPayments, commandIdempotency, financeApplications, financialAuditLog, journalEntries, journalLines, paymentAllocations, postDatedCheques, receivableDocuments. A replay with the same key returns the same result and leaves every table and the bank balance unchanged.
- **Permission refusal.** Three roles are each refused with `FORBIDDEN` naming the permission(s) they lack (a sales role lacking both, a finance role lacking `confirm:finance_disbursement`, a confirm role lacking `view:finance`), with a full-table snapshot unchanged each time.
- **Foreign tenant.** Four legs, each asserting its specific refusal code and an unchanged full-table snapshot. Another organization's authorized owner naming their own organization with this organization's application and cheque ids is refused `FINANCE_RETURN_NOT_FOUND`. This organization's owner naming this organization is refused `FINANCE_RETURN_NOT_FOUND` for a cheque id that belongs to another organization, and for an application id that belongs to another organization (each swapped independently, so removing the ownership check on either row load turns a leg red). The other organization's owner naming this organization is refused `UNAUTHORIZED` by tenant authorization.
- **No open period.** With every accounting period closed through the public `accountingPeriods.close` mutation (checklist warnings acknowledged), a role without authority is still refused `FORBIDDEN` with nothing written. For the authorized owner the door does not refuse: the return succeeds with `reversal: DEFERRED`. The row asserts that state: no journal row changes and the ledger still shows the receipt, the payment is VOIDED, the receivable is OPEN at 12,500,000, the deal is EXPECTED at version 2, the cheque is RETURNED, one PENDING REVERSE outbox row waits, the exact changed-table list is pinned, and a replacement confirmation from a version-1 screen is refused `FINANCE_CONFIRM_STALE_REQUEST`. After the periods are reopened through the public `accountingPeriods.reopen` mutation and the outbox drains, the exact inverse posts once (the eight literal rows, the pre-receipt trial balance, bank balance 0) and the outbox row is POSTED. A second drain changes no journal row. The deferred reversal is certified by those drain assertions.

These are convex-test cells, not Convex-runtime or real-backend evidence (the SCRUM-762 replay is still owed).

### Deal commission lines (SCRUM-854)

The salesperson's own sale commission is the amount on the sale row, paid by payroll. A SALE commission line for the sale's own salesperson would pay that one role twice, so `dealCommissions.addLine` refuses it with `DEAL_COMMISSION_LINE_DUPLICATES_SALESPERSON` before any write. The certification row drives the paid owned configured-financier deal with a 250,000 (JOD minor) sale-row commission: the duplicate SALE line is refused three times, each with an unchanged full-table snapshot. The first two refusals (amounts 250 and 1) run before any line exists: zero line rows and a payable total of 250,000 before and after. A SALE line of 40,000 for another employee and PURCHASE 30,000 and EXECUTION 20,000 lines for the salesperson are then allowed, every line row carries the sale's organization, and the total is 340,000, checked against an organization-scoped recomputation. The third refusal (amount 5 JOD) runs after those three lines exist: three line rows (the same rows, unchanged) and a total of 340,000 before and after. A second organization with its own sale (999 commission), its own lines (a SALE line for its own salesperson, a SALE line naming this organization's salesperson id, and a PURCHASE line) is read before the first refusal, then after the first two refusals together, after the three allowed writes together, and after the final refusal: each time its rows are unchanged and its total stays 1,023,000. The commission-line scans and refusal snapshots are of four kinds. (1) Organization-and-sale scans through `by_org_sale` read this organization's rows (`scopedRows(orgId, saleId)`) and feed the total recomputation. (2) Organization-only scans through `by_org_sale` (`scopedRows(orgId)`) read each organization's line rows, including the foreign organization's before and after snapshots. Both kinds assert the organization of each returned row. (3) One intentional unscoped count of `dealCommissionLines` asserts that the foreign rows exist, so a scan that forgot the organization would see them. (4) The refusal snapshots (`expectRefusedAndUnchanged`) compare every schema table across all organizations before and after the refused call. The cell was proven red by removing the guard and by making a scan unscoped. Existing duplicate SALE lines are not migrated, and payroll (`payroll.ts`, around lines 882 and 1147) would still pay such rows. The owner stated on 2026-10-20 at 11:59 UTC that there are no production deals. No agent has verified production data, so the decision to skip a cleanup of existing duplicate lines rests on that statement. It is a convex-test cell, not real-backend evidence (SCRUM-762 replay still owed).

### SCRUM-853 commission policy transition row

`commission-policy-auto-to-manual-unpaid` covers the public `orgSettings.setCommissionMode` door. Complete a cash sale with a positive automatic commission under unset, AUTO_MEMBER or AUTO_TIERS, then request MANUAL while the sale is live and its commission is unpaid. Expect `VALIDATION_FAILED`, unchanged database content, unchanged effective mode and continued `SALE_ALREADY_COMPLETED` refusal from `sales.setCommissionAmount`. A genuine zero or missing amount with no accrual does not block; after the explicit MANUAL choice, entering a new 250 USD commission recognizes 25,000 minor units. Negative or nonfinite stored amounts require accounting review. The backend regression lives in `convex/commissionDefaultMode.scrum778.test.ts` under `SCRUM-853`.

This row protects future policy changes. It does not repair commissions already unlocked in MANUAL organizations or historical ledger drift hidden by a zero/missing sale amount. The compound index selects live completed unpaid rows with a stored amount; ascending and descending first-row reads inspect both extrema. There is no paid-history cap. Local harness evidence covers valid refusals, corrupt-value refusals, paid/cancelled/deleted eligibility, tenant isolation and a positive unpaid row after a large paid history. Required cloud-rehearsal case `S853` in `scripts/accountingRehearsalCases.mjs` uses genuine owner API calls: a 12,500 JOD owned cash sale, 10,000 acquisition and 10% member rate accrue 250 JOD (250,000 minor); MANUAL selection must refuse without changing the automatic amount lock or payable journal lines. The member rate is restored through the public API. The case runs before the final closed-period scenario. Its assertion harness catches acceptance, wrong refusal, a write-before-refusal and absent accrual; this model is not platform evidence. Real-backend execution, nonfinite ordering, concurrency, rendered UI and historical inventory remain **UNAVAILABLE** until separately measured. No measured I/O reduction or pilot certification is claimed.

### SCRUM-760 post-delivery door: expected-refusal cell

Door-census gap 3 found post-delivery money doors that no switch covered. Per the owner ruling, deal unwind, `vehicles.correctAcquisitionCost` and cancellation of a COMPLETED sale or CLOSED deal stay enabled for the pilot. The one door blocked server-side is `collections.returnClearedCheque`, by the compile-time switch in `convex/utils/postDeliveryDoorContainment.ts` (`CLEARED_CHEQUE_RETURN_PILOT_DISABLED`), raised immediately after tenant authorization, before any write and before the idempotency wrapper. `convex/postDeliveryDoorContainment.test.ts` holds its expected-refusal cell: authorized actor, valid arguments, the specific code, a snapshot of every schema table unchanged, and an in-suite mutation attack that flips the switch and requires the call to be accepted. Rehearsal case RV1 (`scripts/accountingRehearsalCases.mjs`) is the same expected-refusal cell against a real backend. These are convex-test cells and a rehearsal definition only; the real-backend replay (item 5) is still owed.

### SCRUM-811 pending sale trade-in reference row

`sale-update-trade-in-tenant` protects `sales.update` even when `tradeInValue` is zero. An authorized owner creates a PENDING cash draft in organization A and submits a valid vehicle ID belonging to B. The actor owns both organizations; that does not authorize storing B's vehicle on A's sale. Expect the specific trade-in ownership refusal and unchanged state. The backend regression also covers a missing and a soft-deleted vehicle, compares the constructed sales, vehicles, transactions, notifications and accounting outbox tables, and permits a live same-organization reference without moving money. Before the guard, all three invalid references were accepted and persisted; the red report reads storage after the accepted call.

Required real-preview rehearsal case `S811` constructs its two organizations and draft through public APIs. It reads back the sale, both trade-in vehicles and the tenant journal inventory before and after the foreign-reference refusal, then requires a same-organization positive control to persist while those vehicles and journals remain unchanged. Its assertion model rejects acceptance, the wrong refusal, a write before refusal and rejection of the positive control. Local assertion and backend harness tests are not platform evidence: real-backend execution remains **UNAVAILABLE** until the reviewed final main SHA passes the trusted disposable-preview rehearsal. This row does not certify trade-in completion, arbitrary foreign relationships, concurrent calls, historical reference repair or production data. Deferred financial switches are unchanged. The requirement is SCRUM-811's foreign IDs as metadata criterion under SCRUM-760 c22619.

### SCRUM-811 expense, customer and relation-read reference-ownership rows

Matrix rows `SCRUM811-expense-reference-ownership`, `SCRUM811-customer-reference-ownership`, `SCRUM811-relation-read-ownership` and `SCRUM811-relation-vehicle-cost` bind `convex/expensesReferenceOwnership.test.ts`, `convex/customersReferenceOwnership.test.ts` and `convex/referenceReadOwnership.test.ts` (two rows). Invariant: every foreign document id a public function accepts or stored link it follows is proven to belong to the caller's organization, and to be live where it drives a write, before any write or any returned field. Write doors enumerated from each file's public `mutation(` exports: `expenses.create` (`vehicleId`, `payerId`, `bankAccountId`), `expenses.update` (`vehicleId`, `payerId`, `bankAccountId`, `expenseId`, plus the STORED `expense.vehicleId` when the update marks the expense PAID), `expenses.remove` and `expenses.reverseExpense` (`expenseId`), `customers.update` and `customers.softDelete` (`customerId`); `customers.create` and `customers.importBulk` accept no id besides `orgId`. The actor owns both organizations, so only the reference check can refuse the second one. Each door receives an organization B id, a hard-deleted id and a soft-deleted id (a user with an offboarding membership stands in for a soft-deleted user; a payer whose membership exists but whose user row is gone is the dangling case) and must refuse with the door's own message and leave every table of the app schema, read generically, unchanged, including the dashboard expense summary in its READY state. A same-organization live reference is accepted at each door. Failing-first defects found by the two review seats at head f7f71acf9 and fixed in this change: (1) `expenses.update` paid against the stored `expense.vehicleId` without validating it, so a PENDING expense whose car was later deleted, missing or foreign posted a payment; the payment boundary `recordPaidExpenseSideEffects` now validates the effective vehicle, while replay, non-accounting edits and reversal of an already PAID expense stay unblocked and a stale car can be cleared or replaced in the same call that pays; (2) `expenses.list` and `customers.getRelations` (sales, leads, tasks, quotes) hydrated stored ids with no organization check and returned foreign vehicle, user and company fields, now an organization-checked read returns the neutral fallback, and a user is shown only with a membership of any status in the organization (removed members no longer resolve and read as unknown or unassigned); (3) the payer check passed a member whose user row no longer exists, now refused locally in `convex/expenses.ts` without changing the shared `requireOrgMember`; (4) permission and privacy, found by Seat 2 at head 658115a49: `customers.getRelations` returned the whole same-organization vehicle in `quotes[].vehicle`, so a role holding only `view:customers` received `purchasePrice`, `sourceCost`, `landedCostTotal`, `minimumProfit` and the consignment supplier name; the join now passes through `projectOptionalVehicleForRole` (`convex/utils/vehicleCostVisibility.ts`, the shared SCRUM-886 cost classification that replaced this PR's own allowlist module), which serves display fields to every reader but cost fields only to the system owner or a holder of `view:cost_price`, the rule `vehicles.get`, `vehicles.list` and `dealOverview` already apply (OWASP API3:2023, property-level authorization), and the row `SCRUM811-relation-vehicle-cost` pins it with a customers-only denial and cost-permission and system-owner positive controls. The tests are top-level so that the SCRUM-761 runner filter selects and executes exactly one test each. Their discrimination is shown by removing a single guard, after which the matching door or read is reported as accepting the reference, changing stored state or returning the foreign field. Local harness evidence only; no concurrency, real-backend, `sales`, `sourcingPayables`, `financingEconomics` or `accountingReports` claim. The scenarios claim only what their bound test executes: the expense scenario's paid-history replay, edit and reversal run inside its bound test, and so does the read scenario's offboarding attribution policy. Not covered and tracked as follow-ups: `vehicles.list` and `vehicles.get` still serve `sourceCost`, `landedCostTotal` and `minimumProfit` to any `view:vehicles` reader (they withhold only `purchasePrice`); `customers.getRelations` returns sale rows to a `view:customers` reader without requiring `view:sales` (their money fields are classed PUBLIC_LEGACY in `convex/utils/saleRowProjection.ts`, pending owner ruling Q8); and it returns whole quote rows, as `quotes.get` and `quotes.listQuotesByCustomer` already do.
### SCRUM-811 direct query-id slot rows

Matrix rows `SCRUM811-expense-list-vehicle-id`, `SCRUM811-expense-total-vehicle-id`, `SCRUM811-customer-get-id`, `SCRUM811-customer-exclude-id` and `SCRUM811-customer-relations-id` bind one top-level test each: `expenses.list` and `expenses.totalByVehicle` (`vehicleId`) in `convex/expensesReferenceOwnership.test.ts`, `customers.get` (`customerId`) and `customers.checkDuplicates` (`excludeCustomerId`) in `convex/customersReferenceOwnership.test.ts`, and `customers.getRelations` (`customerId`) in `convex/referenceReadOwnership.test.ts`. They close the gap Seat 2 found at head 339c9cf90: with the organization check removed from `customers.get` in memory, all eight earlier ownership tests still passed. Only `customers.get` dereferences its id and refuses (one identical message for a foreign, missing and soft-deleted customer, so existence is not revealed). The other four slots never read the id: it only selects a range of an organization-scoped index (`by_org_vehicle`, `by_org_phone`, `by_org_email`, `by_org`, `by_org_customer`) or the explicit `orgId` filter on the unscoped `quotes.by_customer` index, or is compared with a row id. For them the guard is that scoping, and the certified behavior is the actual one: a foreign or missing id selects nothing, returns no refusal, and no field of another organization appears. Each test caller owns both organizations (the `getRelations` caller reads organization A only), plants organization B data carrying unique markers, and also plants cross-linked rows (an organization A expense naming an organization B car; organization B sale, lead, task, quote and deposit naming an organization A customer; an organization B customer with the same phone and email as an organization A one) so that the org scoping, not the id, is what is proven. Every test ends with a same-organization positive control that returns the caller's own data. Not asserted, on purpose: a soft-deleted car or customer id on the read slots returns the caller's own history, as it does today, with no refusal to pin.

Failing-first: each guard was removed in turn and the matching test reported a failure with the production file restored afterwards. `expenses.list` and `expenses.totalByVehicle` with the org-scoped index replaced by a `vehicleId`-only filter returned the other organization's expense (987654 total). `customers.get` without the organization check, and without the soft-delete check, returned the foreign and the deleted customer. `customers.checkDuplicates` with the lookup scope derived from the exclude id's organization returned the organization B match, and with the `!== excludeCustomerId` comparison removed the same-organization control still matched. `customers.getRelations` with the organization scoping removed from quotes, sales, leads, tasks or deposits (five separate removals) returned organization B rows for a foreign customer and for the same-organization customer. The design follows the OWASP IDOR Prevention Cheat Sheet (scope every lookup to the caller or verify ownership after the read, answer a missing and an unauthorized object identically, and test with two tenants that each own objects, across read operations too) and OWASP API1:2023 (broken object-level authorization), both read on 2026-11-01. The Convex functions-authentication page read the same day covers identity only and defers to the Convex Auth authorization guide for ownership checks, so ownership is not asserted from Convex documentation. Local convex-test evidence only: no concurrency, real-backend, `sales`, accounting or vehicle-cost claim. The vehicle cost fields that `vehicles.get`, `vehicles.list` and `vehicles.listAll` return without `view:cost_price` (`sourceCost`, `landedCostTotal`, `minimumProfit`) are tracked in SCRUM-886, which blocks SCRUM-760, and not here; the remaining LOW follow-ups are in SCRUM-879 comment 24219.

### SCRUM-729 qualified-owner UI parity row

Desktop and mobile navigation, the separate billing card, and the Team page's actor controls use that same qualified status.
