Settlement and accounting component regressions use the real permission hook rather than copied owner shortcuts. Browser-unit navigation controls cover all owner-only settings links and the Team controls. These do not establish authenticated platform behavior or the mobile application's separate authorization consumers.

Release order is backend projection, backend verification, then frontend promotion. The live Vercel project was observed with automatic custom-production-domain assignment disabled and main builds staged; that setting must be revalidated before release. Never promote the new frontend while its backend still omits the qualified flag, and never restore a role-name fallback.

`qualified-owner-ui` requires the membership projection, permission hook and owner-only route guard to agree with the backend's `isSystemOwnerRole` predicate. An explicitly unqualified role named OWNER with only view:finance must not gain manage:finance, manage:users or owner-only controls. A genuinely flagged system owner remains qualified even after its display name changes. An unflagged legacy OWNER qualifies only when it holds the frozen pre-413 owner set; the current permission list alone is not that historical set. The public query returns explicit qualified status plus server-resolved permissions; the hook checks that permission list and the owner-only guard checks the explicit status. An unresolved projection or missing status never promotes a display name.

The backend cell invokes the actual public query against constructed membership/role rows. The permission-hook cells use the real organization provider. RoleGuard and navigation cells use real organization/language providers; the guard renders the actual access-denied screen. These cells simulate Convex SDK and Next router boundaries, and navigation also stubs the Clerk profile button. The accounting setup cell mocks its organization/language hooks and child views. The supplier-authority cells use the real organization provider, mock language/currency and accounting helpers, and exercise the actual permission hook in both containers. These cells are local harness/UI-unit evidence, not an authenticated real-backend browser sweep. The shared-hook consumer census is scoped to hooks/, components/ and app/; the production typechecks verify their compilation, not their runtime subscriptions. Real role/action coverage, offboarding/account-state behavior, production data and the full SCRUM-616/811 acceptance remain **UNAVAILABLE** until separately executed. No backend permission is granted by this change and no financial activation switch changes.


### SCRUM-858-management-settlement

The separate management settlement stores seven immutable minor-unit installments (six floor shares and one remainder) against a verifiable posted opening, with classification attestation and a different finance approver. It creates no journal, expense, prepaid, capital, cash or paid/applied record. The report reuses the actual native income-statement GL period-flow reader; it does not relabel operational data as an official balance or replace closed-balance snapshots. Recognised installments are deducted only in this management reconciliation. Future rows remain scheduled; approval is not backdated into historical reports. Delayed approval catches up at its approval month or the next available open/future period. No general organization allowance or no-plan cap is introduced.

Public-handler harness controls measure exact rounding, retry/tamper refusal, duplicate-plan prevention, different-actor approval, foreign organization and permission refusal, missing opening/journal/line/reset refusal, native expense debit and credit reversal, period close/reopen flow parity, unchanged closed balance-sheet readback, and retained organization-deletion cursor cleanup. Real-provider UI tests measure protected query skipping, stored seven-part review, separate approval identity and exact minor-unit retry arguments. Deployment-flag-only overrides in existing legacy accounting suites retain dormant profit-distribution mathematics; those suites do not certify the current pilot door.

The additive UI draft adapter compares an opaque server-observed canonical opening/reset version inside the mutation before idempotency replay. It shares the legacy draft operation and economic fingerprint; the legacy native document-ID validator and completed-key ordering remain unchanged. Native-handler controls compare command, plan, event, journal, line, bank account, cash session/movement, partner balance/movement, expense and prepaid records around stale/foreign/version, authority and basis refusals, including an unrecorded old intent after reset to a same-date/same-currency replacement. Legacy completed-key replay with a missing basis remains a named compatibility limitation. The UI form is keyed by tenant and observation version: provider tests measure blank replacement intent, same-version retry identity and isolation from an old inflight completion. Frontend no-call controls do not establish native zero writes. The current-month rehearsal observes the context version and uses the adapter; the original future-zero rehearsal keeps the legacy route. These are serial harness and SDK-boundary checks, not OCC, authenticated rendered-platform UI or deployment certification. The state reset follows [React's keyed-form guidance](https://react.dev/learn/preserving-and-resetting-state#resetting-a-form-with-a-key); the atomic precondition follows [Convex mutation transactions](https://docs.convex.dev/database/advanced/occ), with both public routes retaining [native argument validators](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/validation). No client-cache ownership or generic extractor proof is claimed.

The maintained `S858-CONTAINMENT` callback also prepares a second governed synthetic opening with explicit partner allocations for a current-month management plan. A different native actor approves the literal seven-part split `[14286,14286,14286,14286,14286,14286,14287]`. The report cutoff is the stored server `approvedAt`; its UTC month, actual open period and seven stored recognition months must match the intended month without retry or a fake platform clock. Exactly the first installment is recognised: recognised amount `14286`, native official income `0`, management profit `-14286`, and distribution unavailable. Complete before/after native journals with lines, trial balance including cash/bank/prepaid controls, physical bank book balance, cash session and movements, prepaid schedules, and partner balances and transactions must agree. Bank `asOf` values are balance cutoffs, retained separately from comparable economics. The baseline cutoff must not exceed approval; the after-read explicitly requests `asOf: approvedAt` and refuses a different returned cutoff. This does not prove when a query executed or whether its result was cached. An SDK-boundary cache model with a declared harness-only clock step exposed the original false refusal by reusing a legitimate response for identical arguments. The final positive test requests a different argument set for the approval cutoff, so it establishes neither an actual cache hit nor platform caching behavior. A separate negative control replays an earlier response for a different requested cutoff and must fail. Other corruptions exercise recognition, amounts, month and each financial witness. This follows [Convex's explicit-target-time guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/understanding/best-practices#dont-use-datenow-in-queries). The original future-zero control remains. These local controls establish assertion capability; actual platform proof requires the amended trusted-main runner to execute in the existing owned Preview with exact-SHA evidence and strict cleanup. This first-month scenario does not certify seven elapsed months, all-family UI, OCC, production data or real financial classification.

Per SCRUM-760 c23526, the uncertified PROFIT_DISTRIBUTION public mutation is refused server-side immediately after authorization and before idempotency or financial writes. Its permanent default-switch control compares movements, outbox events, journals, lines and command records before/after refusal. DRAW and contribution logic is unchanged. These are source and serial convex-test/UI harness controls only: actual platform execution, OCC concurrency, authenticated rendered UI, release acceptance and classification of real amounts remain UNAVAILABLE. No payout activation or pilot certification is claimed.


### SCRUM-858-opening-employee-commissions

Opening employee commissions are immutable source obligations, separate from sale commissions. Actual native payroll imports the still-owed amount into its own payslip fields without accruing it again, applies native approval/self-beneficiary authority and advances, then debits the source-declared canonical or custom control exactly once. There is no standalone opening payout or fake sale. The source-proven commission query preserves own-versus-manager scope and returns UNAVAILABLE with null amounts when proof is missing; departed staff remain owed but unavailable for native payroll. Voluntary offboarding refuses while debt remains.

The serial public-handler harness covers exact minor units, a decoy account, approval/payment source damage, tenant/permission boundaries, cancellation, drift reapproval, double payment, native queued claim/refusal/backoff/retry, historical settlement cutoffs and the actual closed-period snapshot balance consumer. A synthetic out-of-band closed journal line proves that a journal-sum alternative disagrees with the native balance consumer. Reset continuation and retained organization-deletion cursor tests exercise atomic clearing of the new reverse payroll reference and destructive invalidation of only new source-result references, preserving historical allocation economics. This destructive lifecycle exception is not a normal amendment or correction path. Targeted new opening supplier-payable cleanup derives identity from immutable allocation results; legacy AP policy remains separate.

These are source and local harness measurements, not authenticated platform, OCC concurrency, rendered UI, production data or pilot acceptance evidence. The maximum admitted read-shape test records actual harness database returns; it does not measure Convex billed bytes or platform transaction cost. Final disposable-backend execution and complete reviewed release verification remain UNAVAILABLE.



The opening commission authority regression refuses a non-owner finance/payroll beneficiary at both draft and approval through the actual public handlers, preserving drafts, liabilities, payroll, outbox, journals and snapshots. The pending-draft query reports the same approval blocker. An independent non-owner may prepare another employee’s obligation; the native system-owner exemption remains supported. Two permanent permission records bind the failing-first DRAFT and APPROVE checks. These are serial harness measurements, not Convex platform or OCC evidence.

### SCRUM-858-remaining-native-liabilities

Five literal backend replays in `convex/openingRemainingFamilies.test.ts` bind the permanent records for supplier recoverables, supplier payables, other named payables, unknown customer deposits, and distinct unidentified receipts. All amounts and identities are synthetic; no guide balances or names are embedded. An opening produces the selected control journal and native source row without a historical expense, vehicle, sale, receipt or payment. Native supplier snapshots remain snapshots because no supplier master exists. Custom controls require the declared family, active own account, matching currency and normal balance; incompatible families and unknown-pool purposes cannot share one control. Existing native cash/bank/control families cannot be reclassified as custom obligations.

The actual harness flow covers partial recovery and receipt reversal; selected-control partial payable settlement; closed-period snapshot reconciliation; authentic queue claim/worker refusal of altered stored economics and a fresh retry after correction/reopening. Same-key engine attacks with changed amounts or stripped source proof leave journals unchanged. Reset continuation with batch size one deletes new occurrence children before opening payables, preserves legacy and foreign-organization payables, and permits a fresh cash opening. Recovery reset removes only its new result reference while retaining the historical allocation; a new-generation opening claim can then be collected through the native handler and returned by current-source readback despite retained historical POSTED headers. The permanent recoverable replay binds this authorized in-progress reset → new opening → receipt → current readback path; selection follows the unique actual live opening journal, never newest timestamp. Duplicate owning headers, detached/foreign sources, multiple live journals and bounded-enumeration overflow refuse; lone missing/reversed historical sources remain explicitly UNAVAILABLE. Source loss is UNAVAILABLE, not revived debt or a fabricated zero.

Unknown pools have no spendable customer document or attributed party. Real current retained-credit application and an independently approved legacy deposit refund leave both held opening controls unchanged. Deposit reconciliation reports source-proven held amounts separately with `attributionStatus: UNALLOCATED`; a missing result produces UNAVAILABLE and a non-reconciled result. The existing period-close warning/acknowledgement surface explicitly describes held pools rather than claiming full attribution; no blanket close-policy override is introduced. The public scenario metadata claims only its listed public steps and stage-specific observations; internal corruption/reset controls are additional harness evidence.

This is harness/source evidence only. Actual Convex platform behavior, OCC/concurrency, authenticated end-to-end family usage, retained production data, final exact-head reviews and release verification remain UNAVAILABLE until measured. Existing pilot containment, including disabled new retained-credit payout and manual journals, is unchanged; supported legacy refund/application doors remain supported.


### SCRUM-858-opening-closure-readers

PR #710 round-5 adds fifteen literal backend bindings: impossible capital/recoverable kind retags (including matching result retags), contradictory payable and pool-purpose results, mixed declaration families/purposes, a genuine public unallocated liability, five real legacy employee-fee posting/reversal variants, and native advance issuance/cash repayment/payroll deduction. Unsupported employee GL families refuse `SOURCE_FAMILY_UNSUPPORTED` even when reversed/net-zero; writer-impossible or contradictory opening declarations refuse `OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE`. The permitted custom unallocated liability remains outside supplier payables. Mixed families preserve an existing refusal; mixed purposes and the two assigned findings failed first. The six scoped restored mutations cause 2/2/1/5/1/1 failures and the restored control passes15/15. Accounting witnesses stay unchanged by reads. These are harness proofs, with final-head seats and real backend evidence still separate obligations.

PR #710 population remediation (reviewed base `25f89c5a383f8c3aa58e4eb43195db3dd75c9be6`): actual opening journal lines establish the account inventory. Missing allocation coverage for a posted custom payable, recoverable or capital control refuses even when its materialized row is also gone. All four readers refuse past/non-finite cutoffs and current/future cutoffs preceding future effective GL activity. A bounded cumulative supplier-control provenance check refuses `SOURCE_FAMILY_UNSUPPORTED` for DIRECT commissions, including deleted subledger evidence, offsetting recovery tampering and cancelled sales whose GL nets to zero. A valid opening-recovery receipt remains supported. Twelve new scenario records bind literal test titles in `openingClosureReaders.scrum858.test.ts` and `supplierReceivables.test.ts`. These are serial harness proofs; final-head seats, real backend and rendered UI remain separate obligations.

The thirteenth population-remediation record binds `accountingPhase1.test.ts` / `can close and lock a period`: historical payables refuse with the exact date warning, unacknowledged close refuses, and explicit acknowledgement retains CLOSED/LOCKED transitions. It is a CI-exposed fixture integration case, not permission to bypass warnings.

Owner rulings 2026-10-28 and SCRUM-858#c23884 (2026-10-29), binding: never invent a balancing entry, never adjust partner capital to absorb an unexplained difference, and keep a reported difference unresolved until it is supported. A subledger-to-GL difference in an organization that has an opening balance is REPORTED, never resolved. No journal, plug, clearing line or equity entry is ever posted to force the two sides together. The label is EN "Unexplained opening-balance reconciliation difference" and AR "فرق أرصدة افتتاحية غير مفسر قيد المطابقة". It is attached only when the organization has opening history; the same difference without one is reported unlabelled.

Four current-state readers carry it: employee advances (`EMPLOYEE_ADVANCES`), supplier recoverables (`RECEIVABLE_FROM_SUPPLIERS`), partner capital, and supplier payables including custom-control opening payables. Opening controls use the existing GL-side cutover source identity and its owning posted header, independently of surviving subledger rows. An orphan opening journal or retained header without live source authority refuses with `OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE`. A queued or failed posting, an unavailable opening source or too many rows make a reader UNAVAILABLE rather than a false difference. The three new readers also refuse a past or non-finite cutoff with `AS_OF_DATE_UNSUPPORTED`; mutable current balances cannot certify historical balances. The close checklist surfaces each difference as a fixed English warning (matched verbatim by `close`) that the close dialog translates at display time. Missing payable provenance has its own EN/AR warning, and the family unavailable warnings include unsupported dates.

PR #710 remediation cases in the same suite cover deleted payable plus deleted header with a retained 30,000-minor journal, all three new readers before cutover and earlier today, a native 700-minor draw offsetting an equal opening tamper, unexpected async database failures at each public boundary, and truthful close warnings. Current partner discrepancy remains -700 and labelled; the historic query refuses instead of matching. Each new boundary has explicit return validation, preserves deliberate `ConvexError` refusals and logs unexpected errors server-side before returning a generic client error. No source, journal or snapshot is repaired by these readers. See [remediation evidence](scrum858-pr710-remediation.md).

Literal backend rows in `convex/openingClosureReaders.scrum858.test.ts` build the opening through the public handlers, tamper one subledger row and assert the exact discrepancy in minor units, the label in both languages, and that the whole ledger (journals, lines, snapshots, events, pending events, accounts) is unchanged before and after the read. Four non-parameterized rows (one per family) bind regression records; the same assertions also run over the full 14-case tamper table. Dialog rows in `components/accounting/setup/ClosePeriodReviewDialog.test.tsx` assert that the English text equals the server text and that the Arabic and label prefix render.

### SCRUM-858-opening-suspense

Owner ruling SCRUM-858 c24449 (2026-11-04): when the entered opening lines do not balance, the difference posts to the `OPENING_SUSPENSE` account (code 1900) and stays open until classified; it is never posted to equity. `convex/openingSuspense.scrum858.test.ts` drives the public direct-post and draft handlers with literal minor units: credits short by 40,000 posts a suspense credit of 40,000 and leaves the retained-earnings line at the entered 60,000; debits short posts a suspense debit; a balanced set posts no suspense line; the close checklist emits `OPENING_SUSPENSE_OPEN_WARNING` and posts nothing; the draft stores the suspense line the approver reads.

PR #710 round 6: the difference is computed exactly (BigInt). A debit or credit total above `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` refuses with `OPENING_TOTAL_TOO_LARGE` on both paths and posts or saves nothing (9,007,199,254,740,991 + 2 against 9,007,199,254,740,991 had a true gap of 2 but posted 1 before the fix; both rows failed first). The largest representable gap (SAFE_MAX against SAFE_MAX - 2) still posts a suspense credit of exactly 2. The warning and the opening-card notice say that clearing needs a manual journal and that manual journals are turned off during the pilot, so the balance stays open; a text row pins this. Whether to keep posting to suspense while nothing can clear it is an open owner question; this change enables no route. Harness evidence only: OCC, a real backend and rendered Arabic/English UI remain UNAVAILABLE until measured.

### SCRUM-858-opening-journal-reversal-doors

The opening-balance journal (`sourceType: cutover`, category `OPENING_BALANCE`) has no accounting event and no public reversal. `convex/openingReversalDoors.scrum858.test.ts` attacks every door that could undo it and compares the whole ledger before and after each attempt: the generic reversal engine given a non-event id, and given an accounting event seeded so that it links to the opening journal (refused with a fixed message before any write; no public path creates such an event, so this guard is defence in depth); the manual-journal create and approve doors (refused by the SCRUM-795 pilot switch, so flipping that switch turns the row red); and period close then reopen, which leaves journals, lines, events and pending postings unchanged and leaves the linked-event reversal still refused.

### SCRUM-858-opening-prepaid-rounding

An opening prepaid whose amount does not divide evenly by its remaining term follows the general schedule convention: each month recognises floor(remaining / remaining months) and the final month takes the remainder. `convex/openingPrepaidRounding.scrum858.test.ts` drives the actual amortisation mutation over an opening schedule posted through the public handler. Expected months are literals worked by hand: 70.003 JOD over 3 months at scale 3 is `[23334, 23334, 23335]`; 10.000 over 3 is `[3333, 3333, 3334]`; 5 minor units over 4 is `[1, 1, 1, 2]`. Each row asserts the recognised parts sum exactly to the allocated amount, the schedule ends FULLY_AMORTIZED with `recognizedMinor` equal to the total, the prepaid control nets to zero and the expense equals the total.

Industry-practice check (owner rule 2026-10-17, proportionate): the [IFRS Foundation page for IAS 8](https://www.ifrs.org/issued-standards/list-of-standards/ias-8-accounting-policies-changes-in-accounting-estimates-and-errors/) (page summary read; the paragraph text was not retrievable) treats an error in an earlier period as something to correct by restating the affected amounts, not by an offsetting entry in the current period. That matches reporting the difference and leaving the opening source to be corrected under accountant approval. IFRS 1 (first-time adoption) likewise expects an opening position to be reconciled to the prior basis with the differences identified and disclosed (paras 6 and 24 as recalled; the IFRS 1 page could not be fetched, so that part is unverified). Neither standard is applied here as a rule: the harness proves only the owner rulings above. OCC, the 4,000-row over-limit branches, rendered Arabic/English UI or release acceptance, which remain UNAVAILABLE until measured.
### SCRUM-858 named opening deposits (S1, OB-B1 to OB-B16)

Owner ruling SCRUM-858#c23884 admits named refundable security and reservation deposits per customer through the existing opening draft and accountant approval only. Literal backend replays in `convex/openingNamedDeposits.scrum858.test.ts` bind the permanent records (matrix rows OB-B1 to OB-B16). All identities and amounts are synthetic.

- OB-B1: approval creates one HELD native deposit row per named customer (origin OPENING, not hold-active, no vehicle-hold row) and one opening journal with two lines; no cash, payment, voucher, transaction or accounting event is created, and nothing exists before approval.
- OB-B2: eleven refused drafts (foreign or deleted customer, foreign or sourced vehicle, duplicate row key, sum mismatch, zero or fractional amount, a security deposit naming a car, duplicate customer and car, and an unnamed pool mixed onto the same control) each leave no draft, row, journal or payment.
- OB-B3: a named deposit on a custom liability account is refused; the family uses the system deposits control only.
- OB-B4: release (refund and forfeit) and void of an opening deposit refuse server-side with the coded bilingual `OPENING_DEPOSIT_PAYOUT_REFUSED` before any write. The three pilot switches in `customerCreditPayoutContainment.ts` are asserted unchanged.
- OB-B5: the deposits reconciliation matches the deposits control to the opening rows. A subledger-only drift shows the owner label (EN "Unexplained opening-balance reconciliation difference", AR as ruled) and posts nothing: no balancing journal is invented.
- OB-B6: the only door that spends an opening deposit is the sale. A reservation naming the car pays that car's cash sale once (one application, liability released, retry and second pass add nothing). Another car, another customer, a security kind or a row larger than the remaining bill pays nothing and leaves the deposit HELD. A reservation with no VIN is customer credit for that customer only. A receipt deposit and an opening reservation can pay one sale together; an opening row is never taken part-way. A financed-plan sale (applications.finalizeDeal) leaves a named opening reservation HELD, because the plan reads quote-keyed rows only (`convex/openingDepositFinancedSkip.scrum858.test.ts`, mutation M16 killed).
- OB-B7: cancelling a sale that applied an opening deposit refuses with `OPENING_DEPOSIT_REVERSAL_REFUSED` before any write; the payout executor and the application reversal each refuse by themselves when every door is bypassed.
- OB-B8: an organization with no opening balance sees a plain difference and never the opening-balance label.
- OB-B9 (round-1 review F1): the owner's direct post (`postOpeningBalanceDirect`, self-approved) refuses any named-deposit allocation with the coded bilingual `OPENING_DEPOSIT_DIRECT_POST_REFUSED` before any write (no draft, journal, deposit row or application); the shared poster refuses it again when `autoApproved`. The draft plus second-user approval route still posts the same rows with `autoApproved: false`.
- OB-B10 (round-1 review F2): `customerDepositsReconciliation` with a `toDate` before any later deposit activity (an application, release, void or later-created row) returns `status: UNAVAILABLE` / `POST_CUTOFF_ACTIVITY` with no currencies and `isReconciled: false`, never a difference invented by comparing the dated GL to current applications. A non-finite `toDate` is `INVALID_DATE`. With `toDate` absent the comparison is the unchanged current-to-current one. Period close shows its own acknowledged warning for UNAVAILABLE.
- OB-B11 (round-1 review, LOW): a 123.457 JOD (123457 fils) named deposit stays exact in the deposit row, the two journal lines and the reconciliation. The six row refusals of OB-B2 are coded EN/AR.
- OB-B12 (round-1 review F3): `customers.getRelations` returns the customer's own current (non-deleted) deposits, newest first, bounded to 100 with a truncation flag, only for a caller with the sales view authority or the owner role; another customer's rows and a customers-only reader see none. This is a read of the existing `by_org_customer` index. Round 3 adds the linked vehicle's description to each row, only when that vehicle belongs to the caller's organization (a foreign `vehicleId` yields none), and the customer dialog's Deals tab renders the list (classification, JOD amount at three decimals, status, vehicle, newest first, empty state, truncation notice) only for a caller with VIEW_SALES; without it nothing is rendered, not even an empty state. The screen evidence is jsdom component tests in EN and AR; a rendered browser check is UNAVAILABLE here.
- OB-B13 (round-2 review P1a): a named opening deposit whose opening date (the draft's `asOfDate`, the date of its opening journal) is after the reconciliation cutoff returns `UNAVAILABLE` / `POST_CUTOFF_ACTIVITY` with no figures, never GL 0 against deposits 100 and an invented -100. The deposit row's `createdAt` is the approval time and is not the accounting date.
- OB-B14 (round-2 review P1b): with `toDate` omitted the cutoff is resolved once as now and the same activity guard runs against it, so a deposit applied to a sale dated after now is `UNAVAILABLE`, never +100 between a GL read to now and deposits read to the present.
- OB-B15 (round-2 review MEDIUM): every dated clause of the guard is pinned. A deposit received (`createdAt`), refunded, voided or forfeited (`resolvedAt`), or deleted (`deletedAt`, alone) after the cutoff, a vehicle hold created, allocated or resolved after it, and an application applied, reversal-started or reversed after it each make the comparison `UNAVAILABLE`. A stamp equal to the cutoff counts as on it; one millisecond later is refused. The same holds for the opening date. Sixteen source mutants of the guard were each killed by these tests.
- OB-B16 (round-2 review P1): an opening deposit whose draft is missing or not POSTED cannot be dated, so the comparison is `UNAVAILABLE` / `OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE`, and period close shows its own warning for it. A period close whose end precedes the opening date warns `UNAVAILABLE`, never an invented difference. A reconciliation writes no journal, entry or correction in any of these cases.

Pilot policy that these rows pin, not a general rule: security deposits are never auto-applied; a financed-plan sale skips the opening pass; opening deposits do not hold the vehicle; VIN-less and security opening deposits have no native deposits screen (the deposits list is vehicle-keyed) and appear only in the opening readback; `customers.getRelations` returns them (OB-B12) and the customer dialog renders them for a sales reader.

This is serial `convex-test` harness evidence only. Convex platform behavior, OCC and concurrent sale races, authenticated end-to-end use, rendered UI in a real browser (the UI tests are jsdom) and a disposable-backend run remain UNAVAILABLE. Boundaries follow [Convex's testing guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/testing/convex-test) and [transaction atomicity guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/database/advanced/occ).

### SCRUM-858 required disposable-platform opening rehearsal

`S858-OPENING` and `S858-CONTAINMENT` are required named callbacks in the existing public-API accounting rehearsal, before P1 (which remains last on its original organization). They use a separate synthetic organization, both pre-existing authenticated QA identities and native roles; they never invite a new user or select a new deployment. The existing preview attestation/provider scrub and native preview egress sink remain load-bearing. The native adapter executes the same callbacks through actual public handlers: twelve source allocations/all ten admitted families, authentic historical close without override and unchanged later snapshot balance, custom-control claim/payable settlement with replay, native commission/advance payroll without reaccrual, duplicate/foreign-source/uncertified-profit-distribution/opening-prepaid-correction refusals, distinct-person immutable seven-part management approval without formal GL writes, and current identified-credit application leaving unknown opening pools unallocated. SDK-return corruptions test wrong source, selected account, amount, fake expense, payroll net, future management recognition and attributed-credit inflation. These are harness controls, not platform/OCC evidence.

The legacy deposit/finance test model explicitly records the two unsupported opening callbacks UNPROVEN; its original29 cases must still execute, and its whole-run summary is incomplete. It does not manufacture an opening engine or a complete PASS. The production registry requires exactly31 cases; old29-only, missing, failed or UNPROVEN evidence cannot certify a release. A pre-merge PR controller running trusted old main still requires the old set and cannot certify candidate-only new cases. This bootstrap boundary is not relaxed: after this registry/runner merges, a final exact-main disposable-platform run must actually execute all31 required cases before pilot acceptance. No current cloud, retained-data, authenticated browser or production opening acceptance is claimed by these records.


### SCRUM-858 six-family native use, authority and consumers

Once the original opening's native settlements succeed, its fixture is retained before the separate six-use proof starts. Failure of that later proof still fails `S858-OPENING`; independent original-fixture containment executes instead of being hidden as UNPROVEN. A causal SDK consumer failure checks that the aggregate remains incomplete with one failed and one passed callback. The public-handler composite fixes only Date to a mid-month UTC instant for every test and restores it afterward; native scheduler timers remain real and a callback control checks that boundary. This prevents wall-clock month changes from changing harness expectations. It does not set the trusted cloud runner's clock or prove elapsed management months.

The six-family native-use witness requires every pre-use journal ID to remain in the complete post-use inventory and its full native `getJournalEntry` detail to remain unchanged. Additions-only reconciliation cannot establish preservation. SDK controls independently remove the original opening only from the list while retaining genuine direct reads/reports, or rewrite a preexisting non-opening detail with balanced debit/credit increments; each must fail the case. These controls alter SDK responses, not native stored journals, and do not claim platform execution.

The existing `S858-OPENING` callback additionally owns a separate synthetic opening for six subsequent native uses, leaving the original containment and future-benefit fixtures intact. Public commands collect customer AR 100,003, recover the distinct refundable supplier deposit 13,001, settle supplier AP 100,001, draw ordinary partner capital 10,000, recognize the genuine current-month prepaid share 23,334 of 70,003 over three months, and sell opening stock costing 200,007 for a full CASH deposit of 300,000. Public `retryAmortizationFailure` catches up the ACTIVE prepaid without fabricating a failure, invoking an internal helper or advancing the clock; its next call posts nothing. Ordinary DRAW stays enabled while the existing profit-distribution refusal remains intact. Every command/deposit/sale replay preserves the native economic witness. Five foreign-source commands refuse with source books and the other ledger unchanged. Literal new-journal account deltas reconcile to cash +303,003, official revenue 300,000, COGS 200,007, prepaid expense 23,334 and income 76,659; original opening/source readback, unknown pools, physical bank/cash-session records and closed historical balance remain unchanged. SDK-boundary missing, duplicate and misallocated journal responses must fail the registered case. Permanent record `opening-six-family-native-use` binds the public-handler composite test. This extends the existing case, not its registry or producer. Local harness results do not certify platform execution, OCC, all-family rendered UI, production books or seven elapsed management months. Boundaries follow [Convex's testing guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/testing/convex-test) and [transaction atomicity guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/database/advanced/occ).

The same public flow temporarily assigns only its second QA identity's membership in this synthetic organization to the actual native SALES role. It attests the actual user/membership, non-owner status and absence of manage:finance, confirm:finance_disbursement and create:sales; it never infers authority from a role label. Seven valid-own-source public attempts refuse before their permitted owner commands: the five finance operations, CASH deposit receipt and stock-sale completion. Full native economic witnesses, including the new consumers, remain unchanged around refusals and retries. The original finance membership and permissions are restored and attested even after an evidence failure; a second restoration failure preserves the first failure as the leading error. The original organization, approver fixtures and pilot flags are unchanged.

Post-settlement operator consumers must agree: sourcingPayables.list names the paid 100,001 supplier obligation, supplierPayablesReconciliation reports AVAILABLE with GL/subledger zero, and deposits.listByVehicle names one 300,000 APPLIED cash-sale deposit. customerDepositsReconciliation deliberately covers CUSTOMER_DEPOSIT only: its held, GL and subledger amounts remain 20,001 with UNALLOCATED attribution. The distinct UNIDENTIFIED_RECEIPT source/control remains 23,001; 43,002 is only the measured sum of these two separate held sources. Confusing that combined sum with customer-deposit reconciliation was a corrected test-plan assumption, not a product fix. Positive summary fields return the measured/asserted native values and the permanent record uses those exact keys. SDK acceptance, overgrant, reconciliation, missing consumer row and restoration-failure controls prove the assertions reject false evidence. This remains local harness evidence until exact-source trusted native execution.

### SCRUM-858 retained reset history and current-source identity

Six literal backend replays in `openingAllocatedReceivables.test.ts` exercise a genuine public first opening, authorized in-progress reset continuation preserving its historical POSTED header, and a fresh public opening followed by native AR collection, advance recovery, partner readback or management context/draft. The immutable reference inventory is complete within its explicit bound before a row may be called ordinary. Missing/reversed/foreign/detached journal, duplicate live journal/owning header and inventory overflow refuse without collection writes; lone missing/reversed historical presentation remains unavailable.

AR retains its existing optional-marker contract: complete immutable reproof still identifies OPENING, refuses a before-basis collection, and permits a valid current-date collection. The initial same/current-date AR marker-erasure success was diagnostic, not itself a defect; only the corrected before-basis attack is causal evidence. Advances retain their existing strict marker refusal. No reset-retention, legacy-reference, account/line/amount proof, posting authority or containment policy is relaxed. The earlier readback mistake and this separate cross-family recurrence triggered `correct`; the preventive change is shared source authority plus permanent behavior checks, not weaker evidence or another unenforced reminder.

Both historical and current headers remain POSTED, the vehicle becomes SOLD, and its inventory journal balance returns to zero. Vehicle authority uses the unique live opening journal and its owning header within the existing 100-header bound. ### SCRUM-858 opening vehicle inventory and per-bank opening balances (S2 and S5, OB-C1 to OB-C7 and OB-G1 to OB-G7)

Owner ruling SCRUM-858#c23884: owned vehicle inventory opens per vehicle at cost and a consigned (SOURCED) vehicle is never recognised as owned inventory; cash and each bank account open as their own balances; any unexplained difference is reported under the owner's label and is never posted or forced to balance. Literal backend replays in `convex/openingInventoryCap.scrum858.test.ts` and `convex/openingBankReconciliation.scrum858.test.ts` bind the permanent records. All identities and amounts are synthetic.

- OB-C1: sixty owned cars open in an organization that also holds forty-five consigned and ten sold cars; the inventory control equals the sum of per-VIN cost, the vehicle inventory reconciliation is reconciled, and `vehicles.list` shows each opened car with its cost and opening markers.
- OB-C2: a consigned car named in a direct post or a draft refuses with the coded bilingual `OPENING_INVENTORY_SOURCED_REFUSED` before any write, whatever import proof it appears to carry.
- OB-C3: the cap counts owned, unsold, non-deleted cars only (exactly 100 open, 101 refuse; legacy rows with no `sourceType` or `isDeleted` and every unsold status count). A live imported car behind 101 deleted rows opens and reconciles. The indexed candidate reader returns at most 101 live documents across its 12 buckets, even with 1,000 deleted rows. The 100-header and 100-row bounds are unchanged. PR #715 Seat 2 correction, 2026-10-30: the former crowded-bucket refusal contradicted the unchanged ruling by counting deleted cars; its scenario ID is retained for history while its expected result is corrected.
- OB-C4: an owned car with full import evidence that is flipped to consigned refuses at the opening row; the flip itself stays behind the RT11 door (`VEHICLE_OWNERSHIP_CHANGE_PILOT_DISABLED`) and the existing `openingStockCashBankReplay` reclassification test.
- OB-C5: selling an opening car after the cutoff and refusing a sale before its parent exists stay in the existing `openingStockCashBankReplay` tests (`fresh opening stock imports and sells against its current header after retained financial reset`, `tracked opening stock resists a stripped marker and a pre-parent sale`).
- OB-C6: a consigned car of another organization stays a plain not-found, never a coded consignment answer that would confirm it exists.
- OB-C7: a financial reset leaves old opening stock unavailable and a fresh import reusable (existing `financial reset leaves old opening stock unavailable instead of reusing its parent`).
- OB-G1: two banks with distinct openings each reconcile to their own posted line and to the bank control, and each bank's `getBookBalance` equals its own line.
- OB-G2: a bank-control line with no bank identity is refused on live writes (existing `openingBalanceBankIdentity.test.ts`); historical lines with no bank make the opening `OPENING_PROOF_UNAVAILABLE`, including when their net is nonzero. No partial figures are claimed.
- OB-G3: `bankAccounts.openingBalanceMinor` is stated reference metadata and never changes a balance. A stated amount that differs from the posted line (either direction, a bank with no posted line, offsetting differences) is reported through `bankAccounts.openingReconciliation` under EN "Unexplained opening-balance reconciliation difference" and the ruled Arabic label, net and absolute per currency, and nothing is posted: journal, line, event and transaction counts are unchanged. A bank in another currency is listed and never compared.
- OB-G4: a later receipt on one bank moves only that bank's `getBookBalance`; the opening reconciliation does not change.
- OB-G5: when the opening cannot be proven (no unique posted opening, over the read bounds, no currency) the report says `OPENING_PROOF_UNAVAILABLE` and shows no per-bank figures.
- OB-G6: another organization's owner, and a same-organization user without `view:finance`, cannot read the report.
- OB-G7: after a financial reset the retained opening header is not read as a current opening (`NO_OPENING`).
- OB-G8: every operand and accumulated total of the opening reconciliation (posted lines, each stated bank balance, bank total, control, unattributed, per-currency net and absolute differences) must be a safe integer in minor units; otherwise the report says `OPENING_PROOF_UNAVAILABLE` and never `OK` (1e308, 2^53, NaN, fractional minor units, sums that leave the safe range).
- OB-G9: an opening bank-control line naming a bank this organization cannot resolve (hard-deleted record, or a bank of another organization) makes the report `OPENING_PROOF_UNAVAILABLE` whatever its amount, including two unresolved lines that offset to a net of zero; it is never `OK`, never a disclosed net, and no figure is shown. Nothing is posted and no balance or partner capital is adjusted (c23884). When every bank resolves, offsetting banks still report `OK`.
- OB-G10: the native bank screen formats each opening-reconciliation amount and the selected bank's book balance in that bank's own currency at that currency's ISO 4217 minor-unit precision (JOD 3, USD 2), in English and Arabic with Western digits; a currency of unknown scale shows a dash, never a guessed scale. Evidence is a component test; no browser replay has been run.
- OB-G11: unnamed legacy bank-control lines of +3,000,000 and -3,000,000 minor units with no surviving banks make the opening `OPENING_PROOF_UNAVAILABLE`, never `OK` with nothing disclosed. Presence is independent of the net. The same-bank variant (one hard-deleted named bank, two cancelling lines) remains unavailable under OB-G9. Both queries preserve journal, line, event, transaction and opening-draft counts; no balancing entry or partner-capital adjustment is made (c23884).

Pilot policy that these rows pin: the report is read only, the GL side is the posted opening journal's own BANK_ACCOUNT lines (movement after the opening is read with `getBookBalance`), and finance resolves a reported difference through an accountant-approved correction, never an invented balancing entry. The bank screen renders the report (`BankOpeningReconciliationPanel`, jsdom test in EN and AR) and the vehicle record shows an "Opening cost" marker for an owned car and never for a consigned one.

This is serial `convex-test` harness evidence only. Convex platform behavior, OCC, authenticated end-to-end use, rendered pages in a real browser and a disposable-backend run remain UNAVAILABLE. Boundaries follow [Convex's testing guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/testing/convex-test), [Convex's query and index guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/database/reading-data/indexes/) and [IFRS IAS 2 on inventory cost](https://www.ifrs.org/issued-standards/list-of-standards/ias-2-inventories/).

### Bounded opening cleanup after retained draft history

The maintained `playwright/opening-proof/opening-onboarding.spec.ts` adds two real browser-boundary witnesses to the existing opening matrix: `owner opening form posts exact native allocations once` and `finance opening form drafts for a different reviewer and posts once`. They use separate public-created synthetic organizations, real owner/finance identities, and rendered form controls for customer receivable, genuine prepaid benefit and partner capital. Actual browser mutation payloads and successful responses are bound to the observed workflow Preview connection, then reconciled against the native opening journal and operational readers; reload and separately labelled API replay refusals must preserve that basis. This is a three-family UI witness, not all-family browser or lifecycle certification.

These tests inherit the trusted workflow's earlier control-plane/marker/seed authority; they do not freshly read the backend marker. Before writing they require an observed response from the exact workflow-configured Preview socket and reject other observed Convex sockets and known production/development targets. They run only in the two named trusted CI contexts, with scoped retries zero. Backend Preview egress sinks remain inherited; only the PR trusted lane adds its browser proxy. No private guide figures, emails, authentication states or credentials are published. The isolated `playwright.opening-proof.config.ts` selects exactly these two cases with no authentication-setup project, one worker and zero retries. Default and scenario suites exclude this directory. After merge, future PR trusted-swarm execution runs the batch after all attack workers on a separate run/attempt-specific, pinned and seeded Preview with its own URL-baked candidate frontend; the hunter Preview and its artifact are not reused. Deal Scenarios supplies the exact-main terminal phase on its own workflow Preview after the original matrix through schedule or manual dispatch. Both inherit earlier proven Preview marker/seed authority and run a strict pre-batch assertion before either fresh organization exists; no marker assertion is inserted between the two organizations. The default Trusted Main E2E workflow is not an accepted fixture context. An older trusted PR controller cannot execute new candidate test source. Collection/typechecking or static HTML paint is not execution proof: inspect the exact trusted terminal browser results before claiming these scenarios passed.

The maintained preview extension adds required callbacks `S858-CLEANUP-HISTORY` and `S858-CLEANUP-SPLIT` after all original 31 callbacks, including P1. The existing strict baseline QA preflight runs before secondary organizations are created. Cleanup authority uses exact successful public organization-create receipts, current native QA membership/role checks, and the existing Preview marker, URL, deployment-class and provider guards. The new 33-case cloud set becomes executable from trusted main after merge; a pre-merge trusted run still uses its main 31 driver against the candidate backend and cannot certify the new callbacks.

`opening-preview-cleanup-runtime-closure` binds the literal native composite test `cleanup cloud callbacks execute real fixture, reset and scheduled worker handlers`. Each isolated fixture publicly originates one JOD commission of 50,000 minor units and supplier recovery of 100,000 minor units, with paid amounts 0. Finite historical preparation inserts 101 rejected headers before that authentic late POSTED header, mirrors only verified native source references into the first rejected header, and establishes a historical reset generation or RUNNING deletion request. These historical admission/reference states are synthetic; no public super-admin authorization or fresh-reset admission is claimed. Real reset continuations remove sources and clear references while retaining 102 historical headers. A separate real deletion-worker kickoff uses its native scheduled successors and removes its organization.

Cap evidence requires the actual reset invocation to emit exactly one fixed server-only discovery/reference marker immediately before the existing generic refusal. The trusted bridge binds that response log to the exact refused function and explicit preview target; missing, duplicate, wrong-phase, stale or quoted evidence refuses certification. Snapshot comparisons require unchanged checkpoints, header references and sources. No raw `pageStatus` field is claimed: the marker measures execution of its `SplitRequired` branch, and padding volume alone proves nothing. The local Convex harness models byte caps and scheduled calls; it provides no platform or OCC proof. No payment-child, nonzero paid-history, production-data or runtime-cost claim is made by these two-source cases. A real exact-main 33 cloud execution is still required, within the unchanged 40-minute workflow budget.

The `SCRUM-50-opening-balance-paths` scenarios `opening-reset-large-retained-history` and `opening-reset-to-org-deletion-checkpoint` bind literal backend tests in `orgFinancialReset.test.ts` and `adminOrgs.test.ts`. Actual batch-one reset continuations traverse 101 rejected drafts plus a late POSTED header, drain payment children first, clear own references across all draft statuses before deleting opening debt, retain unrelated-org references, and complete. Interrupted reset followed by authorized org deletion clears the obsolete payable-discovery queue before deleting its targets; the old deletion cursor remains fixed until reference scanning completes. Negative controls reject another source's completion checkpoint, a stale reset generation, and completed proof on an unsuspended org. Discovery and reference scans use separate bounded invocations with one pagination maximum. These are synthetic Convex harness controls, not platform/OCC or production-data certification. Reset dry-run counters explicitly name their batch scope and do not claim full inventory.

The same matrix row also includes `opening-reset-recovery-checkpoint-org-delete`: a public, verified JOD supplier-recovery opening enters a real suspended reset reference scan, then authorized org deletion clears that obsolete checkpoint at initialization. Natural deletion batches remove the recovery while the org and RUNNING request still exist, without retaining its ID. Existing admin reference-scan continuation remains covered separately. `opening-cleanup-discovery-split-required-refusal` and `opening-cleanup-reference-split-required-refusal` bind literal adverse-result controls: substituting the database's incomplete `SplitRequired` page contract makes the real helper refuse with checkpoint, draft references and source unchanged. Literal `SplitRecommended` controls still advance. Both scans request 50 items while retaining the 100-row and 512,000-byte ceilings; the ordinary 101-rejected-plus-late-POSTED scenario still progresses. These substituted database results prove local fail-closed behavior, not actual Convex page splitting, large-document resource usage or concurrency; the required large-history preview remains unavailable until measured on the platform.

The recovery checkpoint scenario additionally invokes an internal destructive reset continuation after public deletion admission. It must refuse without recreating the abandoned checkpoint; natural deletion still removes the recovery without leaving a retained organization reference. Literal sibling controls cover public approval and a FAILED deletion followed by a new hard-delete retry. Dry-run inspection remains available. A bounded request-pointer read reserves destructive progress for RUNNING/FAILED deletion, including persisted start/purge markers; missing, foreign or closed request metadata refuses before writes. Verified same-org PENDING_REVIEW/APPROVED requests without destruction markers preserve ordinary reset continuation, so the existing reset-completion prerequisite for rejection does not become stranded. These are serial lifecycle harness controls, not platform/OCC proof or evidence that operators have used the interleaving in a deployment.

### SCRUM-868 P2 canonical supplier identity on vehicles

| Intake screen control | Required result | Bound check and evidence limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| SCRUM939-final-confirmation | Photos Continue mounts a different button for Availability; an early create submit calls neither create nor request-create; final confirmation and edit submit still save | `components/vehicles/VehicleDialog.submit.test.tsx`, red at `8574e06bd6aa658a46d1ca92b81bb95abdee4c30`; strict EN/AR journey in `playwright/tests/supplier-identity.spec.ts` asserts an empty vehicle read before the final click. Local component evidence only; the authenticated backend journey has not been run for this fix. |

Matrix rows `SCRUM868-P2-supplier-identity` (money, 33 scenarios) and `SCRUM868-P2-supplier-isolation` (permission, 3 scenarios) bind literal tests in `convex/vehicleSupplierIdentity.test.ts`. Owner ruling SCRUM-868 c23794 and the SCRUM-760 pilot scope: every pilot-enabled door that sets a SOURCED vehicle's consignment supplier (`vehicles.create`, `update`, `createSourced`, `importBulk`, `vehicleEdits.requestCreate`, `requestUpdate`, `resolve`) or an OWNED ON_ACCOUNT creditor takes a supplier master id. The server resolves the id inside the caller's organization, stores the id and writes the name snapshot itself; a typed name is never trusted. Independent expected values: a name-only call (the shape the mobile app sends) refuses with `SUPPLIER_IDENTITY_REQUIRED` and no table changes; a foreign-organization id and a deleted id refuse with the same code and the same message (`SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND`); an inactive supplier refuses with `SUPPLIER_INACTIVE`; a creditor id on a consignment car, or a consignment id on an owned car or a cash purchase, refuses with `SUPPLIER_ROLE_CONFLICT`. Once a supplier payable or a supplier-borne expense exists, changing the consignment supplier refuses with `SUPPLIER_LINK_FROZEN` at request time and at approval, while re-sending the stored id is a no-op. The freeze is proven for each money record on its own: a supplier payable, a supplier receivable with no payable (a direct-to-supplier sale), a supplier cost recovery, a supplier-borne expense and a finance application holding a supplier concession each refuse a supplier change with nothing written (the held concession also refuses ehicleEdits.requestUpdate at request time with HELD_BY_DEAL and stores no request), and a vehicle with 199 dealership-borne expenses may still change supplier while the 200th fills the scan bound and refuses (fail closed). A purchase import whose (import, row) evidence already exists with the same fingerprint is recognised as already recorded (inserted 0, alreadyRecorded 2, one payable of 9,000, no table changes) even after its creditor was deactivated, including a row number that is negative (-1) or beyond 2^53, which the purchase validator accepts; a different import using that inactive creditor still refuses with `SUPPLIER_INACTIVE`, and a changed or foreign-supplier replay of the same key still refuses with `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. An UPDATE request stores no typed `purchaseSupplierName`: only the server snapshot name beside the creditor id. The on-account payable carries the creditor id, snapshot version 1 and the server name for 10,000 JOD, and the acquisition journal is the unchanged single VEHICLE_ACQUIRED event (no posting or amount change). A master rename after creation does not rewrite the stored vehicle name, and a retry of the same idempotency key replays the first vehicle. The data browser refuses a change to `sourcedSupplierId` as a locked ownership field, and a stored link that points at another organization is dropped by the buy-out conversion reader. This is serial `convex-test` harness evidence only. It does not prove concurrency (the harness has no OCC), Convex platform behaviour or production data. A legacy name-only vehicle already stored keeps its name and no id: it is not backfilled by this change, and a legacy name-only pending request cannot be approved. The mobile app is outside the pilot and is not changed; it now receives the coded refusal on a name-only supplier write. The web supplier picker, import mapping and their English and Arabic strings are covered by component tests only; no browser replay record exists for them yet and the rendered EN/AR desktop and phone screens were not inspected by this change.

### SCRUM-868 P3 supplier settlement identity

Matrix rows `SCRUM868-P3-supplier-settlement` (money, 13 scenarios) and `SCRUM868-P3-supplier-isolation` (permission, 4 scenarios) bind literal tests in `convex/supplierSettlementIdentity.test.ts`. Owner ruling SCRUM-868 c23794 and design rule N5: an obligation that cannot be tied to one tenant-owned supplier master row is never created, and never settled. Independent expected values:

- Sale completion of a SOURCED car on either route (THROUGH_DEALERSHIP payable, DIRECT_TO_SUPPLIER receivable) refuses with `SUPPLIER_IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED` when the car has a real supplier name but no `sourcedSupplierId`, when the id belongs to another organization, and when the id no longer exists. The foreign id and the deleted id return the identical message (no existence oracle, TEN-1). The refusal comes before any write: a snapshot of every table is equal before and after, and replaying the same command key is refused the same way. The earlier "Unknown supplier" name refusal is unchanged.
- A resolved own-organization supplier completes on both routes, and the payable or receivable carries the supplier id and the supplier's snapshot version (2 in the first scenario, 3 in the controls), for an active and for an inactive supplier (N10: deactivating a supplier never strands an obligation that already exists). Amounts are unchanged by this phase.
- The six settlement doors (`sourcingPayables.markPaid`, `recordPartialPayment`, `setDisputed`; `supplierReceivables.recordReceipt`, `setDisputed`; `supplierCostRecoveries.recordReceipt`) refuse a legacy row with no supplier id, a row whose supplier id belongs to another organization, and a row whose supplier id no longer exists, each with the coded refusal and nothing written. A deactivated supplier's existing payable (13,000) and receivable (3,000) can still be paid and collected.
- `supplierReceivables.outstandingSummary` groups identified rows by supplier id (two suppliers with the same display name are two entries; one renamed supplier is one entry of 7,000 with two claims) and keeps every unresolved row, claim or recovery, as its own entry keyed by the row id. Two same-name unresolved claims of 1,000 and 2,000 stay separate; with one identified claim of 4,000 and two same-name 500 recoveries the total is 8,000 and the cost-recovery part 1,000, there are five entries, and no entry holds more than one claim.

Deviation from the design, recorded here and on SCRUM-868: the doors exempt rows with `origin === "OPENING"` from the supplier-id requirement, because the opening-balance flow validates those rows against the approved draft and refusing them would strand every opening supplier balance until P5. A follow-up (P5) removes the exemption once opening rows carry ids.

Not proven: concurrency (the harness has no OCC), behaviour on the Convex platform, and production data. The round-1 text of this section said existing id-less obligations are not converted (owner ruling c23871, "no migration needed"). That ruling assumed no such rows exist, and P2 is deployed while main's sale completion, cost-recovery opener and concession recorder still write rows without `supplierId`; the next subsection replaces that precondition. The deployment inventory of such rows was not checked.

#### P3 round 2: bounded repair and counts-only release check

Matrix row `SCRUM868-P3-supplier-repair` (money, 11 scenarios) binds literal tests in `convex/supplierIdentityRepair.test.ts`, `convex/supplierConcession.scrum766c.test.ts` and `scripts/supplierIdentityRollout.test.ts`. This is a coordinator decision under the owner's delegated authority (SCRUM-868, 2026-11-01), not an owner ruling; it supersedes the "no migration needed" precondition because its assumption was false. Independent expected values:

- A payable written by P2 code (a name, no `supplierId`) is refused by `markPaid` with `SUPPLIER_IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED`; after `supplierIdentityRepair:repairPage` it carries the supplier id of its vehicle's frozen `sourcedSupplierId` and `markPaid` settles it (status PAID). The stored name is not rewritten and no snapshot version is invented. The same holds for a receivable (collectable after the repair).
- The id comes only from the vehicle link. A vehicle with no link stays unrepaired even when a supplier of that exact name exists; a vehicle linked to another organization's supplier, a deleted supplier, a vehicle that is no longer SOURCED, a row whose name is neither the master's display name nor one of its aliases, and a row whose snapshot version is ahead of the master all stay refused and are counted by reason. A renamed supplier needs the old name as an alias.
- A second run changes nothing, including the first stamp's timestamp. A row that already has an id is never rewritten. OPENING rows are neither repaired nor counted. A page is clamped to 100 rows.
- A handed-over concession deal whose basis has no supplier id (the Seat 2 reproduction, both routes) is refused with the coded `SUPPLIER_CONCESSION_BASIS_UNBOUND` and nothing written; after the repair binds the basis it completes against the vehicle's supplier with payable 9,100 (THROUGH_DEALERSHIP) or receivable 2,400 (DIRECT_TO_SUPPLIER). A basis whose recorded supplier name is not the vehicle supplier's stays `BASIS_STALE`.
- `supplierIdentityRepair:countPage` recounts from the data, read-only: id-less native payables, receivables and recoveries (terminal ones included), held concessions whose basis has no supplier id (a missing basis counts), and SOURCED vehicles with no supplier link. `scripts/rolloutRelease.mjs` runs repair then check for every organization after the bank rebuild. A failed call, an unreadable result, the shared deadline, a page cap or a cursor that does not advance fails the release; leftover rows fail it after the Social Inbox verification, so that verification still runs. Output is counts and opaque organization refs only.

Not proven: the real Convex runtime (this is serial `convex-test`; the harness has no OCC), the `convex run` path with the operator key (its scope is unverifiable from here), and production data. The release check inspects organizations listed when the rollout starts. No browser replay exists because no screen changed beyond two refusal strings, which are covered by the i18n parity test. The expense door (`costBearer.ts`) still accepts a supplier name without an id when it opens a cost recovery; that is P4 scope and is not changed here. No browser replay exists because no screen changed.

#### P4/P5 round 4: the first supplier link of a consignment car that already has money

Matrix row `SCRUM868-P4-first-link` (money 12, permission 3 scenarios) binds literal tests in `convex/vehicleSupplierFirstLink.scrum868.test.ts`. Defect (Seat 1 and Seat 2, SCRUM-868 PR #732): a SOURCED car with no `sourcedSupplierId` and money already recorded could be neither linked (`SUPPLIER_LINK_FROZEN`, N11) nor repaired (the repair takes the id only from the car's link), so `markPaid` stayed refused with `SUPPLIER_IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED` and the release count could never reach zero. Production inventory read before the change: 17 unlinked SOURCED cars, one of them with money (one supplier cost recovery and one supplier-borne expense), none sold, no payable or receivable. This is a coordinator decision under the owner's delegated authority (SCRUM-868), not an owner ruling. Independent expected values:

- The one door is `vehicles.update`. It gives the first link only when ALL hold: the car is SOURCED and has no `sourcedSupplierId`; the picked supplier is active and in the caller's organization (`SUPPLIER_NOT_FOUND`, `SUPPLIER_INACTIVE` as before); no finance application holds a supplier concession (`HELD_BY_DEAL`, checked first and unchanged); the caller holds `manage:finance` (else `SUPPLIER_FIRST_LINK_NEEDS_FINANCE_MANAGER`); and the picked supplier is confirmed by names. The name stored on the car, and the stored name on EVERY id-less payable, receivable and cost recovery of the car, must each be the supplier's display name or one of its aliases (ACTIVE or RETIRED) under the registry's `normalizeNameKey`. A row with a blank name, a different name, a snapshot version ahead of the master, or an id of another supplier, a row of another organization, and a table that fills the 100-row read bound each refuse with `SUPPLIER_FIRST_LINK_NOT_PROVEN` and nothing written. A supplier-borne expense carries no supplier name, so an expense-only car is confirmed by the car's name alone (an interpretation of the brief, recorded here). The name only confirms the id the operator picked: nothing searches or merges by name.
- Taking the door writes the car's `sourcedSupplierId` and the master's name snapshot, plus one `adminAuditLog` row `vehicle.supplierFirstLink` (actor, vehicle, before: no link and the stored name, after: the supplier id, how the name matched and the count of rows checked). Every money table (payables, receivables, recoveries, expenses, accounting events, pending events, journal entries and lines) is equal before and after: no amount, journal or row is written. The existing repair then binds the rows (one recovery, `repaired 1, unresolved 0`), the release count reads 0, and a sold car's payable settles (PAID).
- Every other refusal is unchanged: a link to another supplier or removal of a link, on a car with money, still refuses with `SUPPLIER_LINK_FROZEN` even for a finance manager and even to a supplier with the same name; a car that is not SOURCED cannot take the exception (tested on the shared check, because two earlier guards stop that case at the door).
- A sales-originated edit request gains no power: `vehicleEdits.requestUpdate` by an editor and `vehicleEdits.resolve` by the owner both refuse with `SUPPLIER_FIRST_LINK_NEEDS_FINANCE_MANAGER` and write nothing (the pending request stays PENDING), because those doors pass no role to the shared check.
- The refusal texts now name a step that exists: a finance manager links the vehicle on the vehicle edit screen, and the next supplier identity repair (run with every release) binds the record. The test executes that step end to end instead of matching the wording. English and Arabic texts exist for both new codes.

Mutants killed: removing the no-current-link condition, the per-row name check, the permission check, the car-name check, the other-supplier row check, the snapshot-version check, the read-bound refusal, the row organization check, the held-concession check, the SOURCED check, the audit write, and handing the approver's role to `vehicleEdits.resolve`. Not proven: concurrency (serial `convex-test`, no OCC; two finance managers linking the same car race on the car row, and the second sees an existing link and is frozen), the real Convex runtime, and production data. Residual, not guessed: a car with money AND a held concession is refused with `HELD_BY_DEAL` first, so it cannot be first-linked while the deal holds the concession, and completing that deal needs the car's link. The brief keeps `HELD_BY_DEAL` first and gives no policy for releasing a concession, so this is reported for an owner decision; the production inventory above shows no such car. No browser replay exists; the existing edit dialog already sends the supplier id, and only refusal strings were added (covered by the i18n parity test).

### SCRUM-887 supplier management read and write authority (slice A)

The Suppliers management screen reads through `suppliers.listForManagement` and
`suppliers.searchForManagement` and writes only through the existing registry
mutations. It moves no money and posts no journal. These rows certify authority
and tenancy only; the registry itself is certified under SCRUM-868.

| Row | Obligation | Executable check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `SCRUM887-suppliers-management-permission` | The management reads need VIEW_FINANCE. VIEW_VEHICLES alone is refused and the picker projection (`suppliers.list`) stays narrow. VIEW_FINANCE without MANAGE_FINANCE reads, and every write (create, update, deactivate, reactivate, addAlias, retireAlias) is refused by the server, not hidden in the UI. | `convex/suppliers.management.scrum887.test.ts` |
| `SCRUM887-suppliers-management-tenancy` | The management list never returns another organization's suppliers. | `convex/suppliers.management.scrum887.test.ts` |
| `SCRUM887-suppliers-management-read-bound` | The management list read is bounded on the server: `paginate` receives `maximumRowsRead` (the smaller of the caller's value and 100), so a distant `endCursor` returns `SplitRequired` with a split cursor instead of an oversized page, and the halves cover the range. Resource-consumption control (OWASP API4:2023); not a money or authority obligation. | `convex/suppliers.management.scrum887.test.ts` |

Not certified by these rows: relationship navigation to payables, receivables and
recoveries (slice B), and the registry initialisation in production (SCRUM-888).

### SCRUM-808 per-organization deal number

Every deal gets a shareable number (`D-000123`) allocated in the transaction that
creates it. The number is an identifier: it moves no money, posts no journal and
changes no amount. These rows certify uniqueness, scope and authority. ADR:
`docs/adr/2026-11-04-per-organization-deal-number.md`.

| Row | Obligation | Executable check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `SCRUM808-deal-number-allocation` | Cash drafts and finance applications share one sequence per organization, in creation order. A sale finalized from an application carries the application's number and takes none. A refused creation consumes no number. A counter that fell behind refuses instead of issuing a duplicate. | `convex/dealNumber.scrum808.test.ts` |
| `SCRUM808-deal-number-tenancy` | Two organizations number independently, and a lookup for another organization is refused, never answered with that organization's deal. | `convex/dealNumber.scrum808.test.ts` |
| `SCRUM808-deal-number-permission` | `dealNumbers.find` needs VIEW_SALES, and `search.globalSearch` returns no deal to a caller without it. | `convex/dealNumber.scrum808.test.ts` |
| `SCRUM808-deal-number-backfill` | `dealNumberBackfill:runPage` numbers existing deals per organization in creation order across both tables, a finalized sale sharing its application's number; a re-run reads no row and changes nothing. | `convex/dealNumber.scrum808.test.ts` |

Not proven: concurrency (serial `convex-test`; two creations racing for one
number rest on Convex's documented serializable OCC), the real Convex runtime,
and production data.

## SCRUM-486 pilot route cells: SOURCED deposit endings and SOURCED financed deposit with contribution

Three harness cells in `convex/scrum486Certification.test.ts` close matrix rows that the ledger listed as Required for the SOURCED routes. Each figure is derived from the owner ruling SCRUM-407 c21031 and ACC-1 (SOURCED is consignment, never salePrice minus cost), not read from code. Each has a regression record under `regression/scenarios/money/`.

| Row | Scenario | Literal expectation (JOD minor units) | Record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SCRUM486-SRC-CASH-REFUND` | SOURCED CASH, 200 held deposit, refunded before any sale. | Dr 1100 / Cr 2100 200,000, then Dr 2100 / Cr 1100 200,000; trial balance empty; revenue, other income and net income zero; no sale, no supplier payable, no 4170, 4100, 5100, 1400 or 2400. | `sourced-cash-held-deposit-refunded-before-sale-moves-only-customer-money` |
| `SCRUM486-SRC-CASH-FORFEIT` | The same, forfeited. | Release is Dr 2100 / Cr 4200 200,000; cash stays 200,000; other income and net income 200,000; revenue zero; same absences. | `sourced-cash-held-deposit-forfeited-before-sale-moves-only-customer-money` |
| `SCRUM486-SRC-FIN-DEP` | SOURCED configured financier, THROUGH_DEALERSHIP, approved 12,500,000, supplier entitlement 10,000,000, 87.4% policy, 200,000 held first payment. | Funded 10,925,000; contribution 12,500,000 - 10,925,000 - 200,000 = 1,375,000. Sale: Dr 1210 12,500,000; Cr 2400 10,000,000; Cr 4170 2,500,000; Dr 4180 / Cr 2220 1,375,000 (contra-revenue, not a cost); Dr 2100 / Cr 2220 200,000. Forward Dr 2220 / Cr 1110 1,575,000, so 2220 nets to zero. Agency revenue net 2,500,000 - 1,375,000 = 1,125,000, COGS zero, supplier payable 10,000 JOD. Full receipt Dr 1110 / Cr 1210 12,500,000 clears 1210 and leaves 2400, 4170 and 4180 unchanged. | `sourced-financed-held-deposit-contribution-is-contra-revenue` |

Mutation attack on `SCRUM486-SRC-FIN-DEP`: booking the contribution to an expense account instead of 4180 turns the cell red (the 4180 line becomes 6900). Evidence class: serial `convex-test` harness only; no real-backend replay (SCRUM-762), no closed-period or concurrency proof. Receipt check on `SCRUM486-SRC-FIN-DEP`: after `confirmDisbursement`, `subledger.getReceivableBalance` reports the financier receivable `PAID` with outstanding 0, one ACTIVE allocation of 12,500,000, and both 1210 lines carry the financier. Two mutants turn it red: the allocation amount reduced by the customer's 200,000 (outstanding 200,000 left) and the status written `OPEN` after a full allocation. The `receivableStatusForBalance` change at `applications.ts:350` is not reachable here, because it sets only the customer-portion receivable and this deal has no customer gap; the equivalent status line for the financier receivable is in `subledger.ts` `allocatePaymentToReceivable`. The supplier is asserted on the payable row (`supplierId`), since journal lines carry no supplier dimension. Round 3: no public door reaches `receivableStatusForBalance`. Its only caller is `transferFinancedAmountFromCustomerReceivable`, whose only caller is the pre-plan branch of `finalizeDeal` (`!directToSupplier && app.companyId`). `financedSaleRecognitionApplies` is true for every such deal, so the plan branch is always taken first (INSPECTED, not executed). The helper is therefore pinned at its exported seam in `convex/applicationsIndexReads.test.ts`: a customer allocation equal to the non-financed balance gives `PAID`, and a fully financed sale gives original 0 and `PAID`. Mutating the `PAID` return to `OPEN` turns both tests red, and `>=` to `>` turns the first red. The `<= 0` to `< 0` mutant survives as an equivalent mutant: the portion is `max(0, ...)` and allocations are non-negative, so `allocated >= 0` already covers it.

Pilot status of the refund and forfeit cells: they count as pilot evidence only once the owner confirms the SCRUM-486 c23529 scope for the narrow pilot. SCRUM-760 c23812 keeps deposit endings in the pilot's CASH flow, but this document does not read that as covering SOURCED endings until c23529 is confirmed. Until then these two cells are certification coverage, not a pilot sign-off.

Not covered here: a SOURCED financed deposit refunded or forfeited, and cancellation after the forward. The DIRECT_TO_SUPPLIER route with a held deposit is covered by `convex/scrum411ForwardOrdering.test.ts`, not by a new cell.

G8 binds four literal public-API certification tests in

financier bank receipt regression. These bindings add no financial capability
and do not certify a real Convex deployment.

| Route | Public completion | Independent expected JOD amounts | Permanent record |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SOURCED CASH, THROUGH_DEALERSHIP | Full 12,500 deposit, then completeFromQuote | Supplier payable 10,000; agency revenue 2,500; COGS 0; customer AR settled by deposit | g8-sourced-cash-agency-certification |
| SOURCED configured finance, THROUGH_DEALERSHIP | finalizeDeal, approved 12,500, no deposit | Full financier AR 12,500; supplier payable 10,000; agency revenue 2,500; COGS 0 | g8-sourced-financed-agency-certification |
| SOURCED configured finance, THROUGH_DEALERSHIP, full bank receipt (`SCRUM-760-G8-SOURCED-RECEIPT`) | finalizeDeal, then confirmDisbursement for 12,500 to a same-org JOD bank | Receipt Dr1110 / Cr1210 12,500 each (12,500,000 minor); financier AR PAID and zero; supplier payable remains 10,000; revenue remains 2,500; COGS 0; retry writes nothing | g8-sourced-financier-receipt-certification |

The exact backend titles are in those records. The paired browser witnesses in

12,500 bank receipt, and reloads the closed deal. None of these witnesses pays a
supplier, refunds a deposit, corrects a cost, or enables an excluded door.
The direct-route browser witness records the supplier advice and asserts no
