## SCRUM-889-RECON-ORG-REFS

Finding SCRUM-889 (MEDIUM, tenancy and money integrity): the A/R and A/P reconciliation readers followed two stored references without an organization check. `ctx.db.get(id)` returns a document whatever organization owns it, so authorization is the function's job ([Convex authorization](https://docs.convex.dev/auth/functions-auth); [OWASP API1:2023, Broken Object Level Authorization](https://owasp.org/API-Security/editions/2023/en/0xa1-broken-object-level-authorization/)). Rule: an inconsistent reference makes the result UNAVAILABLE. A line is never skipped, because skipping one could report an unreconciled ledger as reconciled. No public writer that creates such a link is known, so every row is seeded directly and read through the real public query. Evidence is convex-test only.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Org A line on 1240 whose parent POSTED entry belongs to org B (org A's period id) | UNAVAILABLE, `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE`, incomplete, no currencies; was AVAILABLE with the foreign amount in the GL | `a line whose parent entry belongs to another organization makes the result UNAVAILABLE, not a balance` |
| Same, foreign parent in DRAFT | UNAVAILABLE, `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE`; was AVAILABLE and reconciled (line skipped) | `a foreign parent in DRAFT is UNAVAILABLE too: its status cannot decide whether the line is skipped` |
| Line whose parent entry was deleted | UNAVAILABLE, `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE`; was silently skipped | `a line whose parent entry no longer exists is UNAVAILABLE, never silently skipped` |
| Queued reversal of an org B event (a type that cannot touch 1240), A/R | UNAVAILABLE, `PENDING_POSTINGS` (treated like a missing original); was AVAILABLE and reconciled | `A/R: a queued reversal whose original belongs to another organization is unresolved, PENDING_POSTINGS` |
| Same, A/P | UNAVAILABLE, `PENDING_POSTINGS`; was AVAILABLE | `A/P: a queued reversal whose original belongs to another organization is unresolved, PENDING_POSTINGS` |
| Controls: same-org POSTED parent (GL 123,400 minor units); same-org non-affecting original (A/R and A/P); deleted original (A/R and A/P) | AVAILABLE and counted; AVAILABLE and reconciled; PENDING_POSTINGS | `CONTROL: a same-org POSTED parent counts toward the GL balance`; `A/R CONTROL: ...`; `A/P CONTROL: ...`; the two "no longer exists" tests |

Bound file: `convex/reconciliationOrgReferences.scrum889.test.ts`. The five bug rows failed on ed0c4e1b and the controls passed there. The only caller of the bounded GL read is the A/R reader; the A/P reader shares the reversal-original resolver.

Round 2 (seat findings B1, B1-reverse, B2 at 8c6c486d0). The A/P GL side reads through `getCumulativeBalancesAsOf`, which sums every child line of the organization's own entries without checking the line's `orgId` and never visits an org-A line whose parent is missing or foreign. The A/P reconciliation now reads through `getCumulativeBalancesAsOfChecked`, which refuses (UNAVAILABLE, `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE`, no balances) in those states. Silently filtering a foreign line is not acceptable: with an offsetting line it reports "reconciled". Rule: the check applies on the journal-line path only; closed-period and certified-snapshot balances still come from snapshots. Every row is seeded directly and read through the real public `supplierPayablesReconciliation` query.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B1: org-B line under an org-A POSTED entry on the A/P account | UNAVAILABLE, `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE`, no currencies; was AVAILABLE with the foreign 123,400 minor units in org A's GL | `B1: an org-B line under an org-A POSTED entry on the A/P account is UNAVAILABLE, not org A's GL balance` |
| B1 offset: the same foreign line plus an offsetting org-A line | UNAVAILABLE; was AVAILABLE, zero discrepancy, `isReconciled: true` | `B1 offset: a foreign line plus an offsetting org-A line must not read as reconciled` |
| B1-reverse: org-A A/P line whose parent entry belongs to org B (POSTED, and DRAFT) | UNAVAILABLE; was AVAILABLE, empty balances, reconciled | `B1-reverse: an org-A A/P line whose parent entry belongs to org B is UNAVAILABLE, not silently omitted`; `B1-reverse: a foreign parent in DRAFT is UNAVAILABLE too, its status cannot decide whether the line is skipped` |
| B2: org-A A/P line whose parent entry was deleted | UNAVAILABLE; was AVAILABLE, reconciled | `B2: an org-A A/P line whose parent entry no longer exists is UNAVAILABLE, never silently dropped` |
| Controls: same-org POSTED parent; same-org DRAFT and VALIDATED parents; same-org REVERSED parent | counted (GL 123,400); skipped, AVAILABLE and reconciled; counted | `CONTROL: a same-org POSTED parent with an org-A line counts toward the A/P GL balance`; `CONTROL: a same-org DRAFT parent is skipped, not refused`; `CONTROL: a same-org VALIDATED parent is skipped, not refused`; `CONTROL: a same-org REVERSED parent still counts: its original lines remain postings` |

The five round-2 bug rows failed on 8c6c486d0 (commit ce4edcd2c adds only the tests) and the controls passed there. Not changed, and recorded as follow-ups on SCRUM-889: the other readers of `getCumulativeBalancesAsOf` (trial balance, income statement, balance sheet, ledger, VAT, deposit control, commission payable) still sum an org-B line found under an org-A entry, because a refusal there is a report-behaviour decision that needs an owner ruling.

Round 3 (seat findings M1 and B4 at 383a8233e). M1: `supplierPayablesUnavailableWarning` sent `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE` and `OPENING_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` to the "too many records" text, so the signed close record named the wrong cause. Each reason now has its own server text (the acknowledgement key), its own EN and AR display key, and its own dialog mapping. B4: the line-first verification pass in `readCumulativeBalances` had no budget. It now spends one budget of documents (`SUPPLIER_PAYABLES_RECON_READ_LIMIT`) on each control-account line page and on each parent entry read outside the entry set, reads with `.take(remaining + 1)`, and answers `OVER_LIMIT` before the budget is exhausted. The budget counts documents, not bytes. Convex caps one function at 16 MiB read, 32,000 documents scanned and 4,096 index ranges ([Convex limits](https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits), read 2026-11-01), and `convex-test` enforces none of the three, so the budget logic is proven here and the platform limits are not.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M1: close with a control-account line whose parent entry belongs to another organization, cutoff strictly inside a period | warning is `SUPPLIER_PAYABLES_RECON_INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE_WARNING`; was the "too many records" text | `M1: period close names the inconsistent journal link, not 'too many records'` |
| M1: close with an opening supplier payable whose source records cannot be verified | warning is `SUPPLIER_PAYABLES_RECON_OPENING_SOURCE_WARNING` | `M1: period close names an unverifiable opening-balance source, not 'too many records'` |
| M1 mapper and dialog | each reason maps to its own constant; the dialog renders EN and AR text for each and acknowledges the raw server string | `convex/utils/closeWarnings.scrum889.test.ts` (four tests); `ClosePeriodReviewDialog supplier payables $name warning (SCRUM-889)` |
| B4: more control-account lines than the budget | `OVER_LIMIT`, never a balance; the public reconciliation answers UNAVAILABLE / `OVER_LIMIT` | `B4: more control-account lines than the budget is OVER_LIMIT, not a balance`; `B4: the reconciliation answers UNAVAILABLE / OVER_LIMIT when the verification pass exceeds its budget` |
| B4: a parent entry read outside the entry set | charged one document to the budget | `B4: a parent entry read outside the entry set is charged to the budget` |
| Controls: lines exactly at the budget; lines plus parent reads exactly at the budget; ordinary small subledger; unchecked reader unchanged; default budget through the public query; healthy A/P ledger and same-org parent at period close | read and summed; reconciles; no "could not be completed" warning | the `B4 CONTROL:` and `M1 control:` tests in `convex/reconciliationOrgReferences.scrum889.test.ts` |
| B4 bytes: control-account lines whose serialized size exceeds the byte budget (document budget not reached) | `OVER_LIMIT`, never a balance; the public reconciliation answers UNAVAILABLE / `OVER_LIMIT` | `B4 bytes: lines whose serialized size exceeds the byte budget are OVER_LIMIT, not a balance` |
| B4 bytes: a parent entry read outside the entry set | its serialized size is charged to the byte budget | `B4 bytes: a parent entry read is charged to the byte budget` |
| B4 bytes: budget boundary; 12 journals with 400,000-character line descriptions under convex-test `transactionLimits` | a total equal to the budget is read, one byte less is `OVER_LIMIT`; the reconciliation answers `OVER_LIMIT` instead of throwing "Read too much data" | `B4 bytes: the budget is exact - a total equal to it is read, one byte less is refused`; `B4 bytes: 12 journals with 400,000-character line descriptions answer OVER_LIMIT, they do not throw` |
| B4 bytes controls: the same lines under the budget; the public reconciliation with the default 4 MiB budget | read and summed; reconciles | the `B4 bytes CONTROL:` tests |

Callers with no verification list (`getCumulativeBalancesAsOf`) are unchanged and unbudgeted. The M1 and B4 bug rows failed on 383a8233e (commits 7e947b6f5 and af2a66f30 add only the tests) and the controls passed there. The close door reaches `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE` only when the cutoff lies strictly inside a period (`startDate <= asOf < endDate`); a cutoff at a period end reads the snapshot path.

Round 4 (seat finding at f9b98439, bytes). The round 3 budget counted documents only, but the verification pass rereads control-account lines that the entry walk already read, and `journalLines.description` and `journalEntries.memo` are unbounded strings. Seat 2 fixture: 12 balanced journals with 400,000-character descriptions, 9.6 MB for the unchanged reader and 19.2 MB for the checked reader, past the 16 MiB a function may read ([Convex limits](https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits)); on the backend that is a thrown query error, fail-closed, never a wrong number. The pass now also spends a byte budget (`SUPPLIER_PAYABLES_RECON_READ_BYTES_LIMIT`, 4 MiB, one quarter of the function limit) on the serialized size of each line and each parent, reads lines as a stream so a refusal stops at the budget plus one document, and answers `OVER_LIMIT` before exceeding either budget. `getControlAccountBalancesAsOf` is unchanged: it reads each line once and has no reread. Evidence: the new tests failed on 6f8d2c790 (a test-only commit) and the controls passed; the 400,000-character case runs under convex-test `transactionLimits`, an emulation of the limit. The real-backend byte measurement, and how closely `serializedSize` tracks what Convex meters, is not produced here and is deferred to the release rehearsal (SCRUM-762).
Round 5 (seat finding at 45d93c2ef, headroom). The round 4 byte budget is a constant, but Convex's 16 MiB read limit is per transaction, and the verification pass rereads lines the entry walk already read. Seat 2 fixture: 16 balanced journals, 32 lines with 400,000-character descriptions, 12.8 MB. The unchanged reader reads them under the limit; the checked reader's 4 MiB cap could not see that, and the public A/P reconciliation threw "Read too much data in a single function execution" at the line stream instead of answering `OVER_LIMIT`. Round 4's `serializedSize` was also not a faithful measure of what Convex meters (JSON length 400,078 against 400,096 Convex bytes for one string-heavy document, Seat 2). The pass is now admitted before every read, line or parent, against the transaction's own remaining headroom from `ctx.meta.getTransactionMetrics()` ([Convex: checking transaction headroom](https://docs.convex.dev/database/writing-data); [limits](https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits)). A read is taken only if the transaction can still read one more worst-case document (1 MiB) and keep a reserve of 2 MiB, 2,000 documents and 256 index ranges for the caller; the metrics are read afresh each time, and a context that cannot report them has no headroom. The caller caps stay as upper bounds and are now measured with `getDocumentSize` instead of `serializedSize`. The reserve follows the custody proof's (`CUSTODY_PROOF_CALLER_RESERVE_*`) and is a policy, not a derivation. Evidence: the new tests failed on bc2fd36eb (the counterexample) and 57b7bd09f (the boundary tests), both test-only commits, and the controls passed. Unproven here, and deferred to the release rehearsal (SCRUM-762): that the backend's metering matches `getDocumentSize`, the cost of one metrics call per read, and that a streamed `next()` does not read ahead past one document.

| Cell | Expected | Bound test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 16 journals with 400,000-character descriptions (12.8 MB) under convex-test `transactionLimits`, unchanged reader succeeds | the public reconciliation answers UNAVAILABLE / `OVER_LIMIT`, no currencies; was a thrown "Read too much data" | `B4 headroom: 16 journals (12.8 MB) the unchanged reader reads answer OVER_LIMIT through the public query, they do not throw` |
| Reported remaining headroom at the edge and one below it | admitted at 3 MiB, 2,001 documents, 257 queries; `OVER_LIMIT` one below any of the three; ample headroom sums both lines | `B4 headroom: a read is admitted at exactly 1 MiB + 2 MiB of bytes, 1 + 2,000 documents and 1 + 256 queries, and refused one below any of them` |
| Context without metrics | `OVER_LIMIT` (fails closed) | `B4 headroom: a context that cannot report its metrics has no headroom (fails closed, never open)` |
| Line spends the headroom, parent is foreign | parent not fetched, `OVER_LIMIT`; with room the parent is read and refused as `INCONSISTENT_REFERENCE` | `B4 headroom: the metrics are read afresh before the parent fetch, which is not made once the line has spent the headroom` |
| Control: 4 lines of 400,000 characters under `transactionLimits` | AVAILABLE, GL 493,600 minor units (the check does not over-refuse) | `B4 headroom CONTROL: under transaction limits, a heavy ledger that fits with its reserve still reconciles (the headroom check does not over-refuse)` |

## Vehicle cost and margin fields, SCRUM-886

Matrix row `SCRUM886-vehicle-cost` binds `convex/vehicleCostFieldVisibility.scrum886.test.ts`, tests `scenario vehicle-readers-withhold-cost: SALES, RECEPTION, ACCOUNTANT and EDITOR receive no cost or margin value from any vehicle reader`, `scenario vehicle-readers-serve-cost: MANAGER and OWNER still receive the cost each reader served before`, `scenario sale-concession-finance-tier: only a caller with view:finance receives the supplier concession, and only a caller with view:cost_price receives the entitlement and margin`, `scenario sale-readers-do-not-let-cost-be-derived: SALES, RECEPTION and a custom role cannot recover a consigned supplier's entitlement or a dealer-owned car's cost from any sale reader`, `scenario sale-trail-supplier-payable-amount-is-cost-tier: sales.getSaleTrail serves the supplier payable amount only to a caller who may read vehicle cost` and `scenario sale-row-revenue-and-commission-base-tiers: financedNetRevenue needs view:cost_price and commissionBase needs view:finance; cost and finance roles still receive them`. Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (broken object property level authorization) and the Convex rule that the function, not the screen, decides what a caller may read. The existing permission `view:cost_price` (or the system owner) decides; no role grant changes. Before this change `vehicles.get` and `vehicles.list` withheld only `purchasePrice`, `vehicles.listAll` withheld `purchasePrice` and `sourceCost`, and `getByVin`, `getLandedCosts`, `sales.get`, `sales.getSaleTrail`, `customers.getRelations` (whose quotes carried the whole stored vehicle), `leads.get`, `applications.get`, `vehicleEdits.listPending` and `vehicleEdits.getHistory` returned the stored row, the landed-cost breakdown or the edit payload untouched. Independent expected values: a car stored with a distinct number in each of `purchasePrice`, `landedCostTotal`, `minimumProfit`, `sourceCost`, a landed-cost line, and an edit request carrying cost keys; for each reader and each of SALES, RECEPTION, ACCOUNTANT and a view-and-edit role without cost, no one of those numbers appears anywhere in the serialized response and the four cost keys are absent (not zero); every non-cost field of the stored row is still present; MANAGER and the system owner still receive every figure each reader served before. The restricted shape is an allowlist: `VEHICLE_READ_TIER` is total over the stored row, so a field added to the schema later is a type error until classified and is withheld until then. The sale row is covered on the same terms: a sale stores the supplier entitlement (the vehicle's frozen cost) and the consigned margin (sale price minus it), so `saleRowProjection` classifies `consignedSupplierEntitlementMinor` and `consignedMarginMinor` COST-tier (served only with `view:cost_price` or to the system owner), while `consignedSupplierConcessionMinor` stays FINANCE-tier (`view:finance`); a sale seeded with a distinct number in each is read through `sales.get`, `sales.getSaleTrail` and `customers.getRelations` by every role, and `sales.getSaleTrail` now projects the sale. Round 2 closes two derivations the sentinel sweep could not see. On a consigned THROUGH_DEALERSHIP sale `supplierPayable.amountDue` (returned by `sales.getSaleTrail`) is the entitlement, so the amount is COST tier and absent for a caller without `view:cost_price`; the supplier's name, status and currency stay served. On a consigned financed (plan v3) sale `financedNetRevenue` is `K - E - contra` beside `considerationMinor = K`, so `considerationMinor - minor` is E when contra is 0; `financedNetRevenue` is now COST tier (the whole object) and `commissionBase` (approved amount and contribution) is FINANCE tier, matching the application projection. Independent expected values: a consigned financed sale with K = 20,000,000 fils, E = 15,432,100 fils, contra 0 and a matching payable of 15,432.1 JOD, and a dealer-owned financed sale with K = 14,000,000 fils and cost 9,876.5 JOD; SALES, RECEPTION and a custom role without cost, calling `sales.get`, `sales.getSaleTrail`, `sales.list`, `sales.listCommissions`, `sales.listCommissionsPaginated`, `leads.getLinkedSale`, `customers.getRelations` and `vehicles.getRelations`, receive no number that equals either cost, nor two numbers that subtract to it (each read as fils and as JOD); MANAGER and the system owner still receive the payable amount and `financedNetRevenue`, ACCOUNTANT receives `commissionBase` and not `financedNetRevenue`. Not closed (follow-up): a percentage-of-margin `commissionAmount` together with a readable commission rate would also disclose the margin on an automatic-commission org; Bloom is MANUAL. This row is serial `convex-test` harness evidence for the 13 readers listed in the test (three of which also carry a sale row); it is not platform, browser or production certification. Not covered and awaiting owner ruling (SCRUM-886): whether `view:reports` or `view:finance` grants cost visibility to ACCOUNTANT, which decides `reports.getInventoryReport`, `sourcingPayables` (`sourceCost` and the unprojected vehicle in `get`), the `dashboard.stats` cost metrics, `sales.consignedSalePreview` and the cost figures `reports` derives per sale; `approvals.profitApprovalStatus` still returns the minimum price uplift to every vehicle viewer by design (it is the figure the sales gate shows), reports and the sourcing pipeline read costs under their own finance permissions, and supplier identity on the vehicle (`sourcedFromName`, `sourcedSupplierId`) stays visible as before. The web vehicle dialog and the mobile sales wizard were changed to cope with the absent fields; their rendered behaviour is unit-tested, not visually verified.

Matrix row `SCRUM886-vehicle-cost-writes` binds `convex/vehicleCostDisclosureWrites.scrum886.test.ts` (round 4, two Seat 2 findings on the WRITE doors). Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (broken object property level authorization applies to writes and to what an error discloses) and the OWASP error-handling guidance that an error message must not carry protected data. (1) `vehicles.importBulk` needs only `create:vehicles`; it used to put the stored supplier cost and the stored acquisition cost into its refusal. The refusal is kept (nothing is imported, no vehicle changes), but a caller without `view:cost_price` now gets the same generic already-recorded-under-this-VIN refusal whether the submitted cost matches the stored one or not, so there is neither a stored amount in the text nor an equality signal; a caller with `view:cost_price` or the system owner still gets the detailed reasons. (2) `vehicleEdits.requestUpdate` (the default SALES door) answered No changes detected only when a submitted cost equalled the stored one, so a guessed cost could be confirmed. A caller without `view:cost_price` who submits any cost-tier key (`purchasePrice`, `purchasePaymentMethod`, `bankAccountId`, `purchaseSupplierName`, `purchaseSupplierId`, `minimumProfit`, `sourceCost`) is refused with the coded `VEHICLE_COST_EDIT_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` (English and Arabic) decided by key presence, never by value, before any comparison or precision check; non-cost requests, and the unchanged-field report for them, are unchanged, and a caller with cost access still requests cost changes. The web vehicle dialog already omits these keys for a caller without cost access, so no screen flow changes; the buy-out of a consigned car was already refused for requests. Independent expected values: a consigned car stored at 7,100,004 and an owned car stored at purchase price 7,100,001, minimum profit 7,100,003 and acquisition cost 10,000; a submitted cost of stored and of stored plus one produce one identical refusal, and that refusal contains neither stored number. Fourteen `permission` scenarios (`regression/scenarios/permission/import-bulk-*`, `request-update-*` and `update-*`) bind the tests; `bugRef.failingFirst` is the commit where the new tests were red (7 failed before the round-4 fix, 3 before the round-5 fix). Controls: the system owner still receives `recorded at a supplier cost of 7100004, this file says 7100005` and `recorded at 10000, this file says 12000`, and a `view:cost_price` requester still queues a cost change. Fixture note: three suites that proposed costs through `requestUpdate` with a role lacking `view:cost_price` (`vehicleOwnershipIntake`, `vehicleSupplierIdentity`, `supplierCostRecoveries`) now grant that role `view:cost_price`; they test ownership and creditor logic, not cost privacy. (3) Round 5: the same oracle on the direct door. `vehicles.update` (needs `edit:vehicles`) compared a submitted cost with the stored one in later checks (the first-purchase posting, the precision check), so an editor without `view:cost_price` could confirm a guess. A caller without `view:cost_price` who sends any cost-tier key (an unclassified key counts as cost) is now refused with the same coded `VEHICLE_COST_EDIT_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS`, before any comparison, by key presence alone. One exemption, decided by the server and not by a client flag: a call that changes ownership (`requestedVehicleSourceTypeChange`, which reads only the stored and submitted `sourceType`, both open fields) from a caller who holds `manage:finance`, because a consignment buy-out must carry its purchase terms. A caller without `manage:finance` who sends ownership-change keys gets the cost-access refusal, and the system owner and `view:cost_price` holders are unchanged. The mobile vehicle editor now omits the purchase-price field and the settlement keys for a caller without `view:cost_price`; the web dialog already did. Known limit, recorded and not closed: a finance editor without `view:cost_price` completing a buy-out still meets the buy-out precision check against the stored consignment price, which can confirm a guess for that narrow, finance-authorised caller; the ACCOUNTANT ruling on SCRUM-886 decides whether those callers may read cost. Independent expected values for round 5: the same stored 7,100,001 / 7,100,003 / 7,100,004 rows; an equal and a different submitted cost produce one identical refusal, nothing is written, a no-cost edit and a `view:cost_price` edit succeed, and a finance editor buys out a consigned car at 9,800 in cash. One more fixture note: `financedConsignedSettlement` grants its manager role `view:cost_price` because it corrects a supplier cost. Not closed and reported: `directSettlementBelowEntitlementRefusal` and the `postingRules` entitlement refusal echo the entitlement to callers who hold finance authority; whether those callers may read cost is the open ACCOUNTANT ruling on SCRUM-886. (4) Round 7: two more equality oracles, bound to `convex/vehicleCostOracles.scrum886.test.ts` (3 of 9 tests failed before the fix). `vehicles.upsertLandedCosts` (needs `edit:vehicles`) compared the submitted paid items with the stored ones in the RT11 door: an edit-only caller submitting the stored paid item was accepted and one submitting a different amount was refused, although `getLandedCosts` already returns nothing to that caller. A caller without `view:cost_price` is now refused with `VEHICLE_COST_EDIT_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` before the vehicle or the stored items are read, for every source type (the write gate matches the read gate), with no exemption; the vehicle dialog no longer renders the landed-cost section for that caller. `vehicles.importBulk` with `OPENING_STOCK` and an import id (needs `create:vehicles`) runs a retry proof that compares cost by design; the import id and row id are open fields on the vehicle, so a create-only caller could replay a known row and tell a proven replay (stored price) from `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` (any other price). A caller without `view:cost_price` may not run an opening-stock import at all: the same coded refusal is raised before any evidence lookup, fingerprint or comparison, because the proof cannot be neutralised without weakening it for readers; readers keep the proven replay and the conflict, and the ordinary non-reader import behaviour of rounds 2 to 5 is unchanged. Round 7 reported, and did not close, the PURCHASE-mode import replay by a non-reader on the argument that its import id is a client-minted token that no reader returns; round 8 below closes it, because an idempotency key is not an authorization credential. Independent expected values: owned car landed cost 100 paid in cash, edit-only caller submitting 100 and 101 gets one identical refusal; opening stock row priced 8,000, create-only caller replaying at 8,000 and 8,001 gets one identical refusal, nothing is written. Fixture note: `vehicles.test.ts` grants its ADMIN role `view:cost_price` in the two tests that write a landed cost. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform or production certification. (5) Round 8 (Seat 2 at 7cd0cebf), bound to `convex/vehicleCostOracles.scrum886.test.ts` (3 of 15 tests failed before the fix, 0 of 15 after). Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (property-level authorization, including what an observable mutation outcome discloses) and the Convex rule that the function authorizes from `ctx.auth`, never from a client argument. Invariant: a caller who cannot read vehicle cost never submits a cost-bearing value into a path that compares it with a stored value or a stored fingerprint of it. `vehicles.importBulk` in `PURCHASE` mode (needs `create:vehicles`) hashes every row with `importRowFingerprint` (price, supplier cost, supplier, payment method, bank) and looks the (import id, row id) evidence up with it twice, in the proven-replay probe and in the evidence loop; a create-only caller replaying a known import key and the vehicle's facts got `alreadyRecorded: 1` with the stored price and `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` with any other, so a guess was confirmed. The import key is client-supplied, so "no reader returns it" was no defence. The round 7 gate (opening stock with an import id) is a special case and is replaced by one general gate: a caller without `view:cost_price` (system owner excepted) who submits either mode that runs the proof, `PURCHASE` or `OPENING_STOCK` with an import id, is refused with the coded `VEHICLE_COST_EDIT_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` before the evidence lookup, the fingerprint, the supplier read and every comparison. It is decided by the caller's role and the request's mode alone, never by a row's values or by anything stored; a payment method is mandatory in `PURCHASE` and a priceless row still hashes `purchasePrice: null` against a stored price, so no row shape is exempt. Readers keep the proven replay on a match and the conflict on a mismatch. Still open to a create-only caller, because it keeps no proof and reads no stored cost: the legacy `OPENING_STOCK` call with no import id (an existing VIN is counted as skipped; a VIN is an open field). Consequences: the round 4 non-reader detail suppression in the `PURCHASE` classification pass (`!mayReadCost` branches) is now defence in depth and unreachable through `importBulk`; its tests still hold and a new test pins the coded refusal. Every import the web vehicle dialog can send carries an import id, so the dialog is not mounted for a caller without `view:cost_price` and the Import button on the vehicles page is hidden for one (screen-only; unit-tested in `VehicleImportDialog.permission.test.tsx`; rendered behaviour is not visually verified). The mobile app has no vehicle import. Default roles: only the system owner and MANAGER hold `create:vehicles`, and both read cost. Independent expected values: a cash purchase imported by the owner at 8,000; a create-only caller replaying that key and facts at 8,000 and at 8,001, with an unknown key at 8,001 and with no price, gets one identical refusal each and nothing is written; a `view:cost_price` caller gets `alreadyRecorded: 1, inserted: 0` at 8,000 and `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` at 8,001. Two `permission` scenarios (`regression/scenarios/permission/import-bulk-purchase-replay-*`) bind the tests. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform or production certification. (6) Round 9 (Seat 2 at a38cdb86), bound to `convex/idempotencyActorBinding.scrum886.test.ts` (6 of 12 tests failed before the fix, 0 of 13 after; the 13th, an actor-less caller replaying a user's key, was added after a mutant survived and is killed by it). Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (property-level authorization; its own guidance notes that side-effect analysis is needed when a property is not returned in the response) and the Convex rule that the function authorizes from `ctx.auth`. `vehicles.create` (needs `create:vehicles`) authorizes with that permission alone and then asks `runWithIdempotency` to compare the caller's fingerprint, which carries purchase price, supplier cost, payment method, bank account and supplier ids, with the one stored under the key. The key is a client token, not a credential, and the stored row recorded its minting actor (`createdBy`) but no replay read it, so a custom role with `view:vehicles` and `create:vehicles` and without `view:cost_price` could replay an owner's key and get the original vehicle id for the stored price and "Idempotency key reused with different request content" for any other: an equality oracle on a COST-tier field, reached through the outcome of a mutation. Default roles that hold `create:vehicles` all read cost, so only a custom role reaches it. Rounds 5 to 8 closed one door each (`update`, `requestUpdate`, `upsertLandedCosts`, `importBulk`); this round closes the class where it lives. Invariant: a command identity belongs to the actor that minted it. `runWithIdempotency` now refuses a replay unless `existing.createdBy` equals the caller's `actorId`, before the fingerprint comparison, before `onFingerprintConflict`, and before anything is written, with the coded `COMMAND_IDENTITY_FOREIGN_ACTOR` (English and Arabic). The comparison is strict, so unknown ownership fails closed: a user replaying a row with no recorded actor is refused, and so is an actor-less caller replaying a user's row; an actor-less caller retrying its own actor-less command still replays. All 65 call sites (economic and non-economic) get the rule; 64 pass `actorId` (`openingManagementSettlement` passes it as a shorthand property) and the one that does not, `supplierDueAlerts`, is an internal, non-economic notification whose key already includes the recipient. The minting actor keeps today's behaviour exactly: the proven replay on a match and the existing conflict message on a changed fingerprint. A reader of cost replaying someone else's key is refused too: the rule is the actor, not the role, and no caller needs cross-actor replay (every web key is a fresh `crypto.randomUUID()` per intent). Not changed and recorded: `findCommandUnit` (import-row evidence) compares a fingerprint without an actor, but both import modes that call it are refused for a caller without `view:cost_price` since round 8, and the opening-stock evidence rows carry no actor, so binding it would strand rows that exist. Independent expected values: an owner creates a cash-purchased car at 8,000; a create-only caller replaying that key at 8,000 and at 8,001 gets one identical refusal, nothing is written, and the owner replaying at 8,000 gets the original id back and at 8,001 the conflict message; a second operation (`expenses.create`, 120 and 121) behaves the same; a stored row with its actor removed is refused for a user. One existing test changed expectation: `convex/scrum413bDealDoors.test.ts` used to assert that a `cancel:closed_deal`-only holder replaying a `create:finance_application` holder's cancel key is served the stored outcome; it now asserts that holder is refused as another user's request, that nothing changes, and that the minting actor's own retry is still served (the no-change half is kept verbatim). The comment in `applications.ts` that calls a replay "not by actor" is left as is on purpose, because editing the file stales 13 contract-skew baseline entries bound to its blob; correct it with a baseline renewal in a follow-up. Twelve `permission` scenarios (`regression/scenarios/permission/command-*`) bind the tests. Not verified: production rows written before `createdBy` existed (a user retrying such a row is refused, which is the fail-closed choice), the Stripe idempotency documentation checked for this round does not state a per-principal key scope, and the rendered UI. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform or production certification. (7) Round 10 (reviewed against 41d1222c4), bound to `convex/saleCompletionCostDisclosure.scrum886.test.ts` (6 of its 12 tests failed before the fix, 0 of 14 after; two helper tests were added after the fix) and to a new describe in `convex/consignedDepositResolution.test.ts` (2 of the new tests failed before the fix; all 59 tests in that file pass after). Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (the outcome of a mutation, error text included, is a read channel for a property the caller may not read) and Convex's guidance that `ConvexError` data reaches the client unredacted in production while a plain `Error` message is redacted (docs.convex.dev/functions/error-handling); both are cited from the review brief and were not re-fetched in this session. Finding: `completeSale` (`convex/utils/saleCompletion.ts`) threw a `ConvexError` that embedded the stored supplier entitlement when a consigned sale would pay the supplier less than his entitlement. It is reachable by `create:sales` through `sales.create`, `sales.completeFromQuote` and `sales.completeDraft`, and by `confirm:finance_disbursement` through `applications.finalizeDeal`, none of which needs `view:cost_price`. Invariant: no mutation response (success, refusal, a different refusal or error text) reachable by a caller without `view:cost_price` may carry or confirm a stored COST-tier value (purchase price, source cost, landed cost, minimum profit, supplier entitlement). Fix: the role is derived on the server, with no client flag. `actorMayReadVehicleCost(ctx, orgId, actorId)` in `convex/utils/vehicleCostVisibility.ts` reads the authenticated actor's membership (`by_org_user`) and answers false for no actor, no membership, an offboarding membership, a missing role or a role of another organization; otherwise it applies `canReadVehicleCost`, so the system owner and `view:cost_price` holders keep the detailed sentence. Every door already passes the authenticated user id as `actorId`, so no door changed and no contract-skew baseline entry went stale (938 matched, 0 stranded, 0 stale). A non-reader gets `CONSIGNED_SALE_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` (English and Arabic pair, English text equal to the exported `CONSIGNED_SALE_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS_MESSAGE`), which names no amount and says nothing was changed. Where this differs from the proposal: the "no supplier cost is recorded" refusal uses the same coded message, because a distinct sentence would reveal whether a cost exists. The sibling refusals in `convex/utils/depositSettlementPlan.ts` ("no recorded supplier cost" and "deposit exceeds the dealership's margin", thrown from the `APPLY_TO_TRANSACTION_SETTLEMENT` branch of `saleCompletion.ts`) were found by the class sweep and fixed the same way: the refusal variant carries `costDerived: true` and the door swaps in the coded message for a non-reader, while a refusal not derived from cost (for example the deposit exceeding the customer's bill) keeps its sentence for everyone. The preview query ignores the flag. Class sweep, by file and verdict. Fixed in round 10: `saleCompletion.ts` shortfall and no-cost refusals; `depositSettlementPlan.ts` no-cost and margin refusals. Already gated: `vehicles.ts` `importBulk` (round 8, `mayReadCost`); `sales.ts` `supplierPayable.amountDue` (stored amount shown only to readers); `applications.ts` `finalizeDeal` redaction below the finance tier. Held for the finance-authority ruling (SCRUM-886 c24247), not fixed here: `accounting/postingRules.ts` (a plain `Error`, so redacted for the client, but the stored `failureMessage` is visible to finance viewers); `utils/vehicleOwnership.ts` supplier-entitlement refusal reached through `applications.ts` (`manage:supplier_settlement`) and `financingEconomics.ts` (`approve:finance_application`); `financingEconomics.resolveAppraisalGap` (`manage:finance`; the stale concession-basis and equality checks); the read-side `sales.consignedSalePreview` query, which serves `supplierEntitlement` to `view:expenses`, `view:reports` and `view:finance`; and `approvals.profitApprovalStatus`, which returns `margin` and `minimumProfit` to `view:vehicles` (pre-existing design, flagged). Mutants, each restored byte for byte after the run: M1 to M10 against the sale-completion tests (detail for everyone, amount appended to the coded message, system owner refused, refusal removed, missing actor treated as a reader, offboarding ignored, role organization ignored, no-cost branch not coded, fail-open flag, reader and non-reader swapped) were all killed, 1 to 10 failing tests each; D1 to D5 against the deposit tests (coded refusal for readers too, no coded refusal, coded refusal for a non-cost refusal, either `costDerived` mark removed) were killed. D6 (a missing actor treated as a reader in the deposit door only) survived: no public door reaches `completeSale` without an authenticated actor, the same condition is killed in the helper by M5, and the deposit door reuses that helper. Residual (corrected in round 11; the round 10 wording "accepted" was too wide): whether a sale at a given price is accepted or refused is the business rule itself, and a caller who commits a sale learns that bit by committing it, once, because the car is then SOLD. What round 10 did not close is that the caller could turn the accept branch into a refusal on purpose: a trade-in the sale cannot accept was checked AFTER the cost gate, so a probe refused for the caller's own reason answered differently on the two sides of the entitlement and wrote nothing, and could be repeated about 25 times. Round 11 below orders the refusals so that this probe is answered identically at every price; only the text and the presence of a recorded cost were uniform in round 10. Known consequence: an accountant without `view:cost_price` calling `sales.create` or `applications.finalizeDeal` on a consigned sale now sees the neutral message until the c24247 ruling decides whether finance roles read cost. The existing deposit tests that assert the detailed sentence now run as a cost reader (`view:cost_price` added to that file's permission set), and a control keeps a non-cost deposit refusal unchanged for a non-reader. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform or production certification. (8) Round 11 (reviewed against c705c9dcf), bound to `convex/saleCompletionRefusalPrecedence.scrum886.test.ts` (12 of its 17 tests failed against `convex/utils/saleCompletion.ts` at c705c9dcf and the 5 control tests passed; 17 of 17 pass after the fix) and to twelve permission scenario records under `regression/scenarios/permission/` (bugRef SCRUM-886, failing-first commit 524875333). Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (the outcome of a mutation, which refusal comes back included, is a read channel for a property the caller may not read) and Convex's `ConvexError` guidance (data reaches the client unredacted in production); both are cited from the review brief and were not re-fetched in this session. Finding (accepted from the round 10 review): a threshold oracle by refusal precedence. In `applySaleCompletionSideEffects` the cost-derived refusals (the consigned shortfall and no-cost gate, and the cost-derived deposit-plan refusal) ran before the refusals that depend only on arguments the caller controls (the trade-in family). A `create:sales` caller without `view:cost_price` could send the car against itself as its own trade-in: below the supplier entitlement the response was the coded `CONSIGNED_SALE_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS`, at or above it the trade-in sentence. Neither commits anything, so a binary search on the price recovers the entitlement to the fils in about 25 refused calls. Invariant: for a caller who cannot read vehicle cost, every refusal the caller can trigger at will is byte-identical at the prices 1, one fils below the entitlement, the entitlement and 20,000; the only permitted divergence is a coded refusal versus a real sale committed. Mechanism, three stages. Stage 1: `refuseUnacceptableTradeIn` is the first statement of `applySaleCompletionSideEffects`, so it precedes the deposit-plan refusal and the cost gate on every entry path that carries a trade-in (`sales.create` through `completeSale`, and `sales.completeDraft` through `completeExistingSale`); texts and their relative order are unchanged. It also states, with the same text, the allocation overflow (`Allocation amount X exceeds receivable outstanding balance 0.`) that a valid trade-in on a consigned CASH sale used to meet only after the gate while the SCRUM-802 pay-first containment is on (a trade-in never counts toward pay-first, so the invoice is already settled to nil); lifting the containment removes that line with it. Stage 2: the cost gate, unchanged. Stage 3: every throw after the gate was swept and classified in the table below. Not a trade-in door: `completeFromQuote` (`completeSalesForLineItems`) and `applications.finalizeDeal` pass no trade-in, FINANCED is refused outside the finance door and LEASE is refused, so the trade-in family is reachable only through `sales.create` and a draft completed by `sales.completeDraft`, and both are covered by tests. Rejected alternative: refuse any SOURCED completion by a caller without `view:cost_price` before reading the cost. It would close the class by construction, but it removes `applications.finalizeDeal` on financed SOURCED deals from the default ACCOUNTANT role (`confirm:finance_disbursement` and `manage:finance`, no `view:cost_price`); that is a pilot-role workflow change and needs an owner ruling, so it was not taken. Differences from the proposal: no refusal text, role grant, write order, posting or containment switch changed; `convex/sales.ts` and `convex/applications.ts` are byte-identical to c705c9dcf; no neutral routing was added because no throw after the gate turned out to be both caller-aimable and cost-derived (the table). Mutants, each restored byte for byte from a copy of the fixed file: M1 (self-trade-in check back after the gate) 5 failing tests; M2 (Stage 1 moved to after the deposit resolution) 2; M3 (the consigned-CASH allocation overflow pre-check removed) 3; M4 (the SOLD/ARCHIVED check back after the gate) 3; all killed. Held, not fixed here: the round 4b `vehicles.update` buy-out limit; `approvals.profitApprovalStatus` (pre-existing design, flagged in round 10); the trade-in refusal "already has a purchase price recorded" reveals only that the TRADE-IN vehicle (not the sold car) has a purchase price, which is not a threshold on the sold car's cost. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform or production certification; no financed-door behavioural test was added.

Round 11 classification of every refusal after the cost gate in `applySaleCompletionSideEffects` (and the entry points before it). (i) caller-controlled and cost-independent: moved to Stage 1. (ii) unreachable invariant or server state: documented, not aimable at will. (iii) cost-derived: routed through the neutral coded refusal.

| Refusal | Class | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Trade-in: own sale, not found or deleted, SOLD or ARCHIVED, SOURCED record, already has a purchase price, value precision (`requireTradeInValueMinor`) | (i) | Moved to Stage 1; text and order unchanged |
| Valid trade-in on a consigned CASH sale: allocation overflow on a nil invoice | (i) in effect | Stated at Stage 1 with the same text while the SCRUM-802 containment is on. The pre-check does not look at deposits, so the legacy hold-less deposit shape gets the Stage 1 text as well, not a post-gate allocation text (corrected in round 12) |
| Warranty and GAP term required | (i), already before the gate | The checks precede the cost gate, so they are not the late half of an oracle; on a CASH sale they are also unreachable because `assertCashSaleCarriesNoExtras` refuses extras first |
| Agency sale with a tax amount | (ii) | Unreachable on every door: the CASH extras guard refuses tax first, and `finalizeDeal` passes no `taxAmount`; INSPECTED, not exercised |
| FINANCED_DIRECT needs an approved amount, currency mismatch, concession BASIS_STALE | (ii) | All three precede the deposit plan and the cost gate, so they are not the late half of an oracle; INSPECTED, not exercised |
| Effective-entitlement concession refusals | (ii) | Amount-free, finalizeDeal-only |
| Consigned shortfall and no-cost gate; cost-derived deposit-plan refusal | (iii) | One neutral coded refusal for a non-reader (round 10), now after Stage 1 so no caller-aimable refusal can precede it |
| `hookSaleCompleted` posting, `ensureReceivableDocument`, customer balance inconsistent, deposit payment and allocate loop, supplier receivable and payable writes, commission hook, manager notification | (ii) | Server state or invariant; not aimable by the arguments |
| `assertCashSaleInvoiceFullyPaid` (post-allocation backstop) | residual | After pay-first it is reachable only through the legacy hold-less deposit shape; it depends on payments, not on cost |
| Other cost gates in `convex/` | key-presence or role gates | `importBulk` (`mayReadCost`), `supplierPayable.amountDue`, `finalizeDeal` redaction; unchanged |
(9) Round 12 (reviewed against fad4751a3), bound to `convex/saleCompletionBatchPrecedence.scrum886.test.ts` (12 of its 18 tests failed against `convex/utils/saleCompletion.ts` at fad4751a3 and the other 6 passed; 18 of 18 pass after the fix) and to eighteen `permission` scenario records under `regression/scenarios/permission/` (bugRef SCRUM-886, failing-first commit 824e741e0d07de867c7b56fbf6a150320cb4aad0; the six tests that already passed at the red commit are controls and carry the same failing-first commit, as the round 11 controls do). Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (the outcome of a mutation, which refusal comes back included, is a read channel for a property the caller may not read) and Convex's `ConvexError` guidance (data reaches the client unredacted in production); both are cited from the review brief and were not re-fetched in this session. Finding (accepted from the round 11 review): the round 11 order holds inside ONE sale, but `sales.completeFromQuote` (`completeSalesForLineItems`) used to run a whole completion per quote line, one line after another. Line 1's consigned cost gate therefore ran before line 2 was even prepared, so every refusal a caller can trigger on line 2 (the car archived, in inspection, soft-deleted, already sold, the same car twice, held by a finance application, a deposit share that was never allocated or exceeds the bill) came back only when line 1's price cleared its gate. A caller with `create:sales` and without `view:cost_price` could make line 2 refuse on purpose and read the stored supplier entitlement of line 1 off which answer came back, by binary search, with every probe rolled back. Invariant (command level): no cost-tier-dependent decision (the consigned cost gate, the cost-derived deposit refusal) runs before every refusal the caller can trigger at will anywhere in the whole command. For a caller without `view:cost_price` the response to a refusal the caller aimed is byte-identical (`code` and `message`) at one fils below the entitlement, at the entitlement, at 1 and at 20,000; the only permitted divergence is the coded `CONSIGNED_SALE_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` refusal versus a sale that really commits. Mechanism: a completion is now three stages and a quote runs each stage for every line before the next stage starts. Phase 1 (`beginSaleCompletion`, `beginSaleCompletionSideEffects`): the status and OTHER-treatment checks, the CASH extras guard, `prepareSaleCompletion`, the pay-first check, the sale row, the Stage 1 trade-in refusals of round 11, the commitment root, `markVehicleAsSold`, the amounts, the deposit stage, the sale transaction, the leads and the finance-company read. Phase 2 (`passSupplierCostGate`): the only place a stored supplier entitlement meets the caller's price, now for all lines, with the cost-derived deposit refusal first. Phase 3 (`finishSaleCompletionSideEffects`): the posting, the receivable and payable bookkeeping, the deposit payments and allocations, the commission and the notification. Line 2's phase 1 runs after line 1's phase 1, so it sees line 1's car already SOLD and line 1's deposit slices already consumed, which is what makes a repeated car or a shared deposit refuse as it did when the lines ran one after another. A single sale (`completeSale`, `completeExistingSale`) runs the three stages back to back. The mutation is one transaction, so a refusal in any phase rolls every line back. The deposit stage needed one more change: `resolveQuoteReservationDeposits` used to throw the cost-derived plan refusal (no recorded supplier cost, or a deposit larger than the margin) in the middle of phase 1. It now records the reason (`deferredCostRefusal`), applies the deposit as the supplier-settlement destination would (a cost-derived refusal exists only on that destination), and the gate throws the recorded reason first, ahead of the shortfall, no-cost and agency refusals, which is the relative order the gate always had. A refusal that is not cost-derived (the deposit exceeds the customer's bill) is still thrown at once in phase 1 with its own sentence. Differences from the proposal. (a) The proposal was a read-only preflight over all lines before the first write. A preflight has to repeat the begin-phase reads (status, holds, duplicate car, deposit allocation) and every effect one line has on the next (the car marked sold, the deposit slices consumed) in a second implementation, and a mirror drifts from the code it mirrors. Staging runs the real code once, so the text and the order are the same by construction (the batch line-2 refusal equals the single sale refusal in a test), and the cross-line effects need no mirror. (b) For a SINGLE sale the cost-derived deposit refusal now fires after `assertQuoteDepositConservation`, the sale transaction, `closeLeadsAsWon` and the finance-company read instead of inside the deposit resolution. Those are server-state steps the caller cannot aim, they write only inside the transaction the refusal rolls back, and the refusal text is unchanged; the 92 completion-reaching test files pass. (c) The rejected option stays rejected: refusing any multi-line SOURCED completion for a caller without `view:cost_price` would close the class by construction but would change a pilot workflow and needs an owner ruling. Residuals, not closed here. (i) Phase 3 refusals (customer balance inconsistent, shared deposit payment allocation, `assertCashSaleInvoiceFullyPaid`, the posting rules) are server state or invariant refusals the caller cannot aim; they now follow every line's gate, as they followed the single sale's gate before. (ii) The legacy hold-less deposit shape (`SINGLE_VEHICLE_QUOTE`, the quote-level deposit with no per-car hold) is read by every line: line 1 consumes it and later lines see no deposit, as before; because that is decided in phase 1 for every line it does not depend on the price. (iii) The same car on two lines cannot be saved through `quotes.saveQuote` (it refuses); the test reaches it with a stored quote, the shape an older or imported quote can have. (iv) A command that commits is distinguishable from a refusal, by design. Mutants, each run against the new file, `saleCompletionRefusalPrecedence`, `saleCompletionCostDisclosure` and `consignedDepositResolution` (108 tests), each restored with `git checkout HEAD -- convex/utils/saleCompletion.ts` after the fix was committed, and `git status --porcelain` showed afterwards only the two pre-existing entries (` M .gitignore`, `?? .ignore`). M1 (the old sequential loop, `completeSale` per line): 12 failing tests, killed. M2 (the gate merged into the finish loop, so line 1 is gated and finished before line 2 is gated): 0 failing, SURVIVED. It is equivalent with respect to this invariant: the only refusals that can then precede a later line's gate are phase 3 refusals, which are not aimable (residual i); the order of the three phases is kept so that no phase 3 refusal can precede any gate. M3 (the head line's gate run right after its own begin): 11 failing, killed. M4 (the cost-derived deposit refusal thrown at once, deferral removed): 5 failing, killed. M5 (the consigned CASH allocation-overflow pre-check restricted to DIRECT_TO_SUPPLIER, the round 11 fifth mutant): 1 failing, killed by the THROUGH_DEALERSHIP trade-in test. M6 (the deferred deposit refusal dropped, so the deposit is applied and nothing refuses): 9 failing, killed. M7 (the deferred deposit refusal thrown with its detail to a non-reader, neutral routing removed): 4 failing, killed. The proposal's M3 (duplicate-vehicle pre-check removed) and M4 (deposit-allocation preflight removed) presuppose a preflight, which this design does not have; M3, M4, M6 and M7 above are the substitutes at the same joints. Self-attack, five predicted failures. (1) A stale phase 1 read after line 1's writes: line k's phase 1 starts after line j<k's phase 1 ended, so it sees the SOLD car and the consumed slices (the repeated-car and shared-deposit tests); no phase 1 step reads a phase 3 write (INSPECTED across the begin steps, plus the 92-file run for the paths it exercises). (2) The batch text differing from the single-sale text: a test compares the two for the same car. (3) The SCRUM-802 pay-first pre-loop ordered differently: it is unchanged and still runs over every line before any phase (`scrum802CashSaleOrdering` stays green). (4) Idempotency keys replaying across lines: the per-line key (`<key>:<vehicleId>`) and the shared deposit payment key (`deposit_received_<depositId>`) are unchanged and phase 3 keeps the line order, so line 1 still creates the shared payment and line 2 finds it (covered by the existing multi-line deposit suites, INSPECTED for the key itself). (5) The deposit plan computed twice differently: it is computed once in phase 1 and the gate throws the recorded string, it never recomputes. Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform or production certification; no browser check was made, since no screen changed.

Round 12 cross-line refusal table: every refusal a caller can aim at one line of `sales.completeFromQuote`, where it is decided now, and what covers it. "Before the gate" means before any line's cost gate.

| Refusal on any line | Decided in | Before the gate | Covered by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No line items, a non-positive unit price | Entry of `completeSalesForLineItems` | Yes (before any phase) | Unchanged |
| CASH extras (warranty, GAP, tax) and pay-first (SCRUM-802) for every line | Pre-loop over all lines | Yes | `scrum802CashSaleOrdering`, unchanged |
| Car archived, in inspection, soft-deleted, already sold | Phase 1, line by line | Yes | Four tests in the first describe |
| Same car on two lines (line 2 meets line 1's SOLD mark) | Phase 1 | Yes | Same-car test (stored quote) |
| Car held by a finance application | Phase 1 (commitment check) | Yes | Finance-held test |
| Middle line refused, third line behind it; gated car in the middle, last line refused | Phase 1 | Yes | Two ordering tests |
| Deposit share never allocated; deposit larger than the customer's bill | Phase 1 (deposit stage) | Yes | Two deposit tests |
| Archived later line on the deposit door (`APPLY_TO_TRANSACTION_SETTLEMENT`) | Phase 1 | Yes | Deposit-door test at E+1000-0.001, E+1000, 1 and 20,000 |
| Cost-derived deposit refusal (no recorded cost, deposit above the margin) | Recorded in phase 1, thrown first in phase 2 | It is the gate | Deposit-door control; `consignedDepositResolution` |
| Consigned shortfall, no recorded supplier cost | Phase 2, every line | It is the gate | Controls (coded below, two sales at the entitlement, detailed for reader and owner) |
| Agency sale with a tax amount | Phase 2, after the shortfall | It is the gate | Unreachable on every door (round 11 table) |
| Trade-in family | Phase 1, first statement | Yes | Not reachable through the quote door; `sales.create` tests of round 11 and the THROUGH_DEALERSHIP test |
| Customer balance inconsistent, shared deposit payment allocation, invoice-fully-paid backstop, posting rules | Phase 3 | After, not aimable | Residual (i) |

Round 12 batch-door sweep: every door found by `git grep -nE "completeSale|completeExistingSale|completeSalesForLineItems|applySaleCompletionSideEffects" -- convex ':!*.test.ts'` and every multi-item loop that touches a cost-gated decision. Verdicts: fixed, already ordered, unreachable, held.

| Door or loop | What it does with several items | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sales.completeFromQuote` to `completeSalesForLineItems` (`sales.ts`) | One completion per quote line | Fixed (this round) |
| `sales.create` to `completeSale` | One car | Already ordered (round 11); the same three stages run back to back; the cost-derived deposit refusal moved as described in (b) |
| `sales.completeDraft` to `completeExistingSale` | One car | Already ordered (round 11); the same stage code |
| `applications.finalizeDeal` to `completeSale` (`applications.ts`) | One car per call, no loop | Unreachable (nothing earlier to compare against); redaction below the finance tier already in place |
| `applications.releaseRootsForApplicationQuote` (the `for (const item of items)` loop) | Releases the holds on each quote line | Unreachable: no cost-tier decision and no refusal in the loop |
| `quotes.saveQuote` minimum-profit approval loop | Judges each line's margin | Already ordered: it runs after every refusal the caller can aim at the save, and is not applied to a CASH quote; INSPECTED, not exercised |
| `vehicles.upsertLandedCosts` loops | Compare the caller's items with the stored ones | Already ordered: a caller without `view:cost_price` is refused at the door before any loop (round 7) |
| `vehicles.importBulk` row loops | Compare each row with the stored cost | Already ordered: the cost-reader gate refuses a non-reader before the first row of every mode that compares cost (round 8) |

(10) Round 13, permission closure (owner ruling SCRUM-886 c24358, 2026-11-02; reviewed against 4a85b84f5; ADR `docs/adr/2026-11-02-sourced-sale-completion-requires-cost-access.md`). Matrix row `SCRUM886-cost-access-rule` binds `convex/consignedSaleCostAccessRule.scrum886.test.ts` (21 of its 32 tests failed against 4a85b84f5 at the red commit 08e650426; 33 of 33 pass after the fix, the 33rd being the completeDraft archived-car test added after mutant M2 survived; 35 of 35 pass now, the 34th and 35th being the two draft tests described below), the adapted round 10 to 12 suites, and 29 new and 4 changed `permission` and `money` scenario records under `regression/scenarios/`. Standard: OWASP API3:2023 (property-level authorization, outcome and error text included) and API5:2023 (function-level authorization: the function, not the screen, decides who may call it), and the Convex rule that the function authorizes from `ctx.auth`. Invariant: an actor who cannot read vehicle cost can never cause the backend to make a decision that depends on vehicle cost. Rounds 6 to 12 closed that class one channel at a time (the text of a refusal, the order of refusals, the order across the lines of a quote) and every round found the next sibling, because the class was defined by what a mutation answers. Round 13 closes it by permission, in four server-side rules. Rule 1: the default ACCOUNTANT template gains `view:cost_price` (MANAGER and SENIOR_ACCOUNTANT already held it). Rule 2: completing or finalizing a SOURCED sale needs `view:cost_price` on every completion door, with the existing coded `CONSIGNED_SALE_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` (English and Arabic, no amount) as the FIRST decision after authentication, keyed only on the car's `sourceType` and the actor's permission (`assertActorMayCompleteConsignedSale`, which reuses `actorMayReadVehicleCost`, so the system owner passes). The doors are `sales.create`, `sales.completeDraft`, `sales.completeFromQuote` (every line is read and checked before any line is touched) and `applications.finalizeDeal`. These are completion doors only. `sales.create` accepts nothing but `COMPLETED` (its argument validator is `status: v.literal("COMPLETED")`, `convex/sales.ts:646`, and `CreateSaleSchema` fixes the same literal), so the ruling's "`sales.create` COMPLETED" and the door are the same thing. A PENDING draft is saved through `sales.createDraft`, which is not gated, because the draft path makes no decision that depends on a cost value. Read for that claim: `createDraft` (`convex/sales.ts:899-924`) does auth, the write limit, `CreateDraftSaleSchema` (no cost field, `convex/validations/sales.ts:81-87`), `assertOperatedDealMode` and the idempotency claim, then `createDraftSale` (`convex/utils/saleCompletion.ts:2655-2666`) calls `prepareSaleCompletion` with intent `DRAFT`. In that function the vehicle, customer, trade-in, salesperson and org-settings reads (`saleCompletion.ts:519-571`, `627-632`) depend on ids and `sourceType`, not on a cost value. The only cost-tier read is the automatic commission (`saleCompletion.ts:750-768`): `computeAutoCommissionAmount` reads `sourceCost`, `purchasePrice` and `landedCostTotal` (`saleCompletion.ts:930-934`, `convex/utils/vehicleCost.ts:26-75`), but the figure is discarded (`insertSaleRecord(ctx, args, prepared, "PENDING")` passes no `commissionAmount`, `saleCompletion.ts:2665`, `1029-1073`) and the one throw inside it that depends on that read (`saleCompletion.ts:1013`) needs an externally financed sale, which a draft cannot be (FINANCED is refused at `saleCompletion.ts:508-517`, LEASE by `assertOperatedDealMode`). Every cost-derived step of a completion (`saleCompletion.ts:656-658`, `698-723`, the supplier concession at `683-690`, the minimum-profit approval at `816-829`, the cost gate) is behind `intent === "COMPLETION"`. The response is the new sale id. So a non-reader saves the same draft at any price with the same response and the same writes, and meets the refusal when the draft is completed through `sales.completeDraft`; two tests pin both (`a draft is not a completion`, mutant M11). They were green on the first run, because the draft door was never gated: there was no defect to fail first, and the red proof is the mutant. A record the check cannot read (missing, or another organization's) is left to the door's own refusal, so the check says nothing about another tenant. Rule 3: saving a custom role with a sale-completing permission and without `view:cost_price` is refused with the coded `SALE_COMPLETING_ROLE_NEEDS_COST_ACCESS` (English and Arabic) on `roles.create` and `roles.update`; the rule first read only the submitted permission set; SCRUM-896 R4-F1 changed `roles.update` to validate the final set (the stored one when none is submitted), so a stored role that holds the combination is refused a rename-only save until it is repaired, and the Data Browser (`adminUpdateRecord`, `adminRestoreRecords`) and the finance backfill are sealed with the same `assertRoleMayBeStored`. The permission list is `SALE_COMPLETING_PERMISSIONS` = `create:sales` and `confirm:finance_disbursement`, derived from the `requireTenantAuth` lists of the four doors (each door lists one permission: `create:sales` for `sales.create`, `sales.completeDraft` and `sales.completeFromQuote`; `confirm:finance_disbursement` for `applications.finalizeDeal`; `requireTenantAuth` requires all that it lists). Premise correction: the brief named `memberships.updateRole` as a role-save door; it only reassigns a member to an existing role and never edits a role's permissions, so the real role-save doors are `roles.create` and `roles.update`. Rule 4: `migrateRoles:backfillAccountantCostPriceAccess`, a bounded (page size 1 to 500, default 100), idempotent internal mutation that adds `view:cost_price` to stored roles named ACCOUNTANT that hold a sale-completing permission without it (`completesSalesWithoutCostAccess`, the same predicate Rule 3 uses; SCRUM-896 R1-F1, see the paragraph after the Round 15 note), skips deleted roles and any role whose normalized name is not ACCOUNTANT (a custom role of that name in the matching state is repaired too, see the SCRUM-896 ADR disclosed limit), and is paginated by cursor. The Deploy production rollout step runs it page by page after each production deploy (SCRUM-896, owner ruling c24372), reporting counts only; it has not been run against any deployment yet. Stored roles are otherwise not migrated: a stored role that holds a completing permission without `view:cost_price` is refused at the door with the coded message. Why this is closed by construction: a non-reader no longer reaches the cost gate, the deposit resolver or the backstop, because the door answers first and depends on nothing a caller can aim. The round 10 to 12 ordering (Stage 1 caller-aimable refusals, the cost gate, the batch phases) is kept as defence in depth, and `passSupplierCostGate` now also refuses `isSourced && !callerMayReadCost` with the same coded message, so a future caller of `completeSale` that forgets the door check still cannot commit a sale a non-reader could probe. Seat 2's scenario at 4a85b84f5, the legacy hold-less deposit on a multi-car quote answering `CASH_SALE_BALANCE_UNPAID_REFUSED` in the world where the supplier cost is E and the coded refusal where it is E plus one fils, is the seventh sibling of the family: a non-reader never reaches the cost gate, the deposit resolver or the backstop once the rule is in, and the test drives both worlds to one byte-identical response. For a reader nothing changes: the gate still runs for every line, `assertCashSaleInvoiceFullyPaid` is still the last line of defence on the legacy deposit shape, and a reader still gets the detailed shortfall sentence. Disclosed authority change: a default ACCOUNTANT (and a stored ACCOUNTANT after the backfill) now reads vehicle cost wherever `view:cost_price` decides, that is the readers and sale rows listed in the first paragraph of this section (the vehicle readers, `sales.get`, `sales.getSaleTrail`, `customers.getRelations`, `leads.get`, `applications.get`, `vehicleEdits.listPending` and `vehicleEdits.getHistory`) and the screens that render them (the web vehicle dialog and details dialog, the vehicles page including the import button, `SaleTrailDialog`, `DealCockpit`, the mobile vehicles module and sales wizard), and it may correct a vehicle acquisition cost where `view:cost_price` is the second gate (`vehicleCostCorrection`); the screens were not rendered in this change. Fixtures and tests changed, with the reason: seven sale-completing fixture roles that held `create:sales` or `confirm:finance_disbursement` without `view:cost_price` now hold it (`sales.test.ts`, `accountingPhase8.test.ts`, `supplierReceivables.test.ts`, `supplierPayablesReconciliation.scrum651.test.ts`, `supplierReceivablesReconciliation.scrum869.test.ts`, `consignedSettlementRoute.test.ts`, `supplierPayableSettlement.test.ts`; they test settlement and posting, not cost privacy), `scrum415FinancedDoor.test.ts` (the new first refusal was hiding `FINANCED_SALE_REQUIRES_DEAL`), `scripts/accountingUnwindRehearsal.mjs` (its probe roles pass through `roles:create`; not run against a live rehearsal), and `docs/agents/pilot-g8-hunt.md`. The round 10 to 12 suites (`saleCompletionBatchPrecedence`, `saleCompletionCostDisclosure`, `saleCompletionRefusalPrecedence`) were adapted and their test titles renamed where the acting role changed; their ordering assertions are kept and the mutant table shows the backstop removal still fails four of their tests (and one in the new file); `consignedDepositResolution.test.ts` keeps its detailed-sentence control with a reader and gains the non-reader request refused at the door before the bill comparison. `vehicleCostFieldVisibility.scrum886.test.ts` and `vehicleCostCorrection.test.ts` asserted that ACCOUNTANT is a non-reader; they now build the legacy stored role (the template minus `view:cost_price`) for that assertion and add a test recording the new template. `scripts/protectedSourcePins.test.ts` (the `applications.ts` pin) and `scripts/tenantWriteGuard.test.ts` (568 mutations in total, 383 analysed, 15 and 170 skipped without an org id) were repinned for the changed sources. The scenario record `deposit-refusal-not-from-supplier-cost-keeps-its-sentence-for-a-non-reader` was renamed `deposit-refusal-not-from-supplier-cost-keeps-its-sentence-for-a-cost-reader` because it now checks the reader control; every round 13 record is bound to ruling `SCRUM-886#c24358`. Stored roles affected: from fixtures only, the legacy ACCOUNTANT shape is the one affected default; the number of stored roles in any deployment that hold a completing permission without `view:cost_price` was not measured (no deployment was read).

| --- | --- | --- |

| A multi-line quote checks only the first line, or touches line 1 before it meets a SOURCED line 2 | Two-line quote with the SOURCED car on line 2 only, world unchanged and line 1 still AVAILABLE | Covered (M3, M3b) |
| A reader loses a defence: the cost gate skips the last line of a quote, or the pay-first backstop stops running on the legacy hold-less deposit | Two-line and three-line CASH quote with SOURCED cars priced below entitlement on lines 2 and 3, refused as a single-line sale is and nothing written; the reader with the Seat 2 legacy deposit meets `CASH_SALE_BALANCE_UNPAID_REFUSED` | Covered (M8, M9) |
| A role template or a saved role drifts back into the combination, or the shared gate is bypassed by a new caller of `completeSale` | Template tests (every default template that holds a completing permission also holds `view:cost_price`), the four role-save tests and the direct `completeSale` backstop test for a non-reader and for a user with no membership | Covered for templates, saves and the backstop (M5, M6a, M6b, M7). UNCOVERED: a new completion door that needs a permission not in `SALE_COMPLETING_PERMISSIONS`; the list is maintained by hand, and its comment says to extend it |
| The backfill runs unbounded, twice, or on the wrong roles; or the grant never reaches an existing org | Adds the permission, skips a role that has it, a deleted role and a custom role, idempotent on a second run, a page of one row touches at most one role | Covered (M10a, M10b). UNCOVERED by design: the run against a deployment, which needs owner approval |
