Round 14 adds two tests to the "saving a role" group (one for `create:sales`, one for `confirm:finance_disbursement`): each stores a role that completes sales and holds `view:cost_price`, resubmits the whole stored set minus `view:cost_price`, and asserts the coded refusal with the English dictionary text, the stored role row unchanged and no new notification (Seat 2 MEDIUM, Seat 1 M12).

Round 14b records owner ruling SCRUM-886 c24368 (option A, 2026-11-02): the default ACCOUNTANT passes the `vehicles.correctAcquisitionCost` gate through the template grant and may post acquisition-cost corrections, and two tests in the same file prove it (the template-built ACCOUNTANT restates a cash car's cost and the vehicle row, the correction row with `correctedBy` and one journal entry change; a stored ACCOUNTANT without `view:cost_price` is refused FORBIDDEN with every row count unchanged), so M5 is now killed by 4 tests (3 old and the positive one) while the control stays green. `convex/vehicles.ts` still carries an outdated code comment above `correctAcquisitionCost` (it says the default ACCOUNTANT template must not be a way around the gate), left unedited to avoid a contract-skew baseline renewal and recorded in the LOW ledger (SCRUM-879).

Round 15 corrects a false premise in the ADR role policy and in the test docstring (Seat 1 R14-F1): before this PR only SENIOR_ACCOUNTANT and the system owner could run `vehicles.correctAcquisitionCost`, not MANAGER. The gate is `COST_CORRECTION_PERMISSIONS = [manage:finance, view:cost_price]`, `requireTenantAuth` refuses when any listed permission is missing, and the default MANAGER template holds `view:cost_price` but not `manage:finance` (only ACCOUNTANT and SENIOR_ACCOUNTANT hold it). The real widening is from two default roles to three. One test in the same file runs the correction as the default MANAGER built from `DEFAULT_ROLE_TEMPLATES` and asserts FORBIDDEN `Missing required permissions: manage:finance` with every row count and the vehicle cost unchanged; mutant M13 (the gate loses `manage:finance`) survived 100 of 100 tests before it and is killed by it. Matrix row `SCRUM886-cost-access-rule`, scenario record `default-manager-is-refused-a-cost-correction-for-lack-of-manage-finance`, ruling SCRUM-886#c24368.

Round 16 (SCRUM-896, Seat 1 R1-F1 and Seat 2 R3-F1, both at ccd2f7647; ADR `docs/adr/2026-11-02-accountant-cost-price-backfill-runs-in-the-deploy-rollout.md`). Matrix row `SCRUM896-accountant-backfill-predicate` (permission) binds `convex/consignedSaleCostAccessRule.scrum886.test.ts`, describe "SCRUM-896 R1-F1 / R3-F1", and the round 13 rule 4 describe. Invariant: once an organization owner has saved or created a role, its permissions change only through an owner action or a one-time repair the owner approved, never through a recurring system job. Standard: OWASP API5:2023 (function-level authorization: the grant is a function-level authority, `manage:finance` plus `view:cost_price` opens the GL-posting `vehicles.correctAcquisitionCost`) and the Convex rule that a function authorizes from stored state, not from a name a caller can choose. Because the Deploy production rollout runs the backfill on every deploy (c24372), the predicate is now: normalized name ACCOUNTANT, not deleted, holds `create:sales` or `confirm:finance_disbursement`, lacks `view:cost_price` (`completesSalesWithoutCostAccess`, reused from `convex/utils/permissions.ts`, not re-listed). Round 17 (SCRUM-896 R4-F1, Seat 2 R4-F1 and Seat 1 L1/L3, at 94995b213): the writers that could store the repaired state are sealed (`assertRoleMayBeStored`: `roles.update` final set, `adminUpdateRecord`, `adminRestoreRecords`, finance backfill), so only pre-release rows can match; describe "SCRUM-896 R4-F1" in the same file (red at dbb09a7cb, 6 of 57 failed; a mutant dropping the `adminUpdateRecord` check fails two) and five `permission` scenario records bound to the same matrix row. Class table, every role shape a deploy can meet, each through the public `roles.create`, `roles.update` and `roles.remove` doors except the legacy default row, which no door can create:

| Role shape | Test | Backfill result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Legacy default ACCOUNTANT (template minus `view:cost_price`, holds `confirm:finance_disbursement`) | positive control, and the round 13 rule 4 repair tests | Repaired; a member then reads cost and the correction context opens |
| Owner renames the default, then creates a custom `ACCOUNTANT` with `view:vehicles`, `view:finance`, `manage:finance` (Seat 2's reproduction) | rename-then-recreate | Not granted; `vehicles.get` withholds `purchasePrice`, the correction context refuses `view:cost_price` |
| Owner renames a custom role to `ACCOUNTANT` | rename into ACCOUNTANT | Not granted; same two doors stay closed |
| Owner strips `view:cost_price`, `create:sales` and `confirm:finance_disbursement` from a repaired ACCOUNTANT (Rule 3 accepts) | owner strips, next deploy | Not restored; same two doors stay closed (Seat 1's causal chain) |
| Owner deletes the unassigned ACCOUNTANT and recreates the name without completing permissions | delete-then-recreate | Not granted; the deleted row is byte-identical afterwards |
| Two organizations in one run plus a custom same-named role in a third | two organizations | Both legacy defaults repaired, the custom role not |
| ACCOUNTANT-named role with only `view:finance`, and a second legacy row in the bounded-pages walk | round 13 rule 4 control and bounded test | `view:finance` alone not granted; the legacy row holding `confirm:finance_disbursement` repaired across one-row pages |

Red against ccd2f7647 (commit a79d324b3): the 5 new "not granted" rows and the rule 4 control failed, 6 of 47 tests (the first red run of the two-test draft failed 2 of 42); the positive control and the repair tests passed, as they must. Mutants against the fix: dropping the completing-permission condition killed 6 (the same 6); inverting it (repair only roles WITHOUT a completing permission) killed 9, including the positive control. Disclosed limit: a stored role named ACCOUNTANT that holds a completing permission without `view:cost_price` is still repaired whoever created it, because the name alone cannot tell it from the legacy default. Only a role stored before round 13 can match: `roles.create` and `roles.update` refuse that combination today, so an owner who wants such a role to lose cost access must also remove the completing permission, which Rule 3 already requires, and the next deploy then leaves it alone. Not measured: how many stored roles in any deployment match the predicate.

Serial `convex-test` harness evidence, not platform, browser or production certification. Not verified: any rendered screen, the Arabic text in a browser, the rehearsal script against a live rehearsal, the backfill against a deployment.

Matrix rows `SCRUM264-org-day` (money) and `SCRUM265-capped-reports` (money) bind literal tests in
`convex/collectionsOrgDay.scrum264.test.ts`, `convex/collectionsOrgDayKeys.scrum264.test.ts` and
`convex/collectionsOrgDayProvenance.scrum264.test.ts`. Collections "today" and a business date are the
organization's day (`orgSettings.timezone`, default Asia/Amman), computed with `Intl.DateTimeFormat`
and exact at 23-hour and 25-hour daylight-saving days; a calendar date the zone skipped (Pacific/Apia,
2011-12-30) has no instants, and the day before it runs to the start of the next date that exists.
`collectionPayments.paymentDate` is stored as either a calendar key (the Record Payment form: UTC midnight
of the picked date, with `createdAt` stamped at the write time) or an event instant (cheque clearing, which is
the only writer that sets `chequeId`; refund approval; payment links; deposits). Timestamp equality cannot
tell the two apart (a form payment written exactly at the picked date's UTC midnight has `createdAt` equal to
`paymentDate`), so every writer records which one it stored in `paymentDateKind` (`calendarKey`, `instant`, or
`saleDate` for the sale-completion deposit refund, whose date is a key from one screen and an instant from
another) and a row that has it is placed by it (`paymentDateProvenance` in `convex/utils/orgBusinessDay.ts`,
which lists every writer). A row without the field (written before it existed; the field is optional and
there is no backfill) is placed by its footprint: a row with `chequeId`, or a value that is not a UTC
midnight, is an instant and is filed under the zone's wall-clock date; a UTC midnight whose `createdAt`
differs is a key and is filed under the date it names; a UTC midnight whose `createdAt` equals it, and a
`saleDate` at a UTC midnight, cannot be told apart. West of UTC (where the key's date and the instant's date
differ) such a row is left out of both days, `dailyCollectionList` and `summary` report
`paymentDateUncertain` with `incomplete`, and the reconciliation refuses the day (`reason: DATE_UNCERTAIN`)
without attaching any payment; east of UTC both readings name one date and it is filed there. An instant of
2026-11-01T00:00:00.000Z in America/New_York is therefore 20:00 on 31 Oct, not a 1 Nov key, while a form
payment for 1 Nov written at that same instant is stamped `calendarKey` and stays on 1 Nov. The
reconciliation idempotency fingerprint and its stored values are unchanged. The week of upcoming cheques in `summary` is bounded
in organization calendar keys (today through today + 7), because a cheque date is a calendar key. A capped
Collections read (`summary`, `dailyCollectionList`, `upcomingChequeReport`, `agingReport`) reports
`incomplete` instead of a silent bound. Receivables, aging and cheque totals cannot be negative, so for them
it is a lower bound; `collectedToday` and the daily list net refunds, the earliest rows are read, and a hidden
refund makes the shown figure too high, so for them it is only partial (the screen says "higher or lower").
They do not certify `overdueOutstanding` or the aging buckets against the
organization day (those still compare a due date with the current instant), the reconciliation
history list (50 rows), or measured I/O.

| Role with `view:cost_price` and without `view:finance` (negative controls) | refused naming `view:finance`; the preview stays `null` without a finance-side grant | `… (SCRUM-902 negative control)` for sourcing, receivables and the preview |

Deploy rollout phase 2f uses a tested `releaseGuard.ts` helper, pages of at most
25 vehicles, the shared deadline and a 10,000-page cap. Locked and invalid
rows are counted anomalies. Read failures and incomplete pages refuse the final
rollout verdict after Social Inbox verification. The implementer ran no repair against a deployment.

### SCRUM-869 settled history: each figure comes from what its source records

Matrix row `SCRUM869-supplier-settled-provenance` binds the nineteen literal tests in `convex/supplierSettledProvenance.scrum869.test.ts` (one scenario record each, `regression/scenarios/money/supplier-history-*`; seven carry a `bugRef` whose failing-first commit is `cf449671f0e0b95c8b854930e2b6d64971e39874`, and the ten added for the PR #764 review findings carry `c94a39db33cc550c7b4d3c848716d90088cf9d64`, where they were red). Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23881 (2026-10-29): the supplier payable payment and the consignment margin receipt are in the pilot and must be certified. Design: ADR `docs/adr/2026-11-03-supplier-statement-outstanding-query.md`, slice 4 amendment. Invariant: a subledger report shows what each source actually records, and two reports that claim to measure the same thing read it the same way.

- A/P: a payable is read through `settlementView`, as the payments reconciliation and the sale-cancellation guard read it. A PAID payable with no `amountPaid` is fully paid, shown at its `amountDue` and labelled `INFERRED_FROM_PAID_STATUS`. A recorded payable is labelled `RECORDED`.
- A/R: `vehicleSupplierReceivables.amountReceived` includes the customer deposit applied to a DIRECT_TO_SUPPLIER settlement, so it is never shown as cash from the supplier. The history nets the claim's own SUPPLIER_RECEIVABLE_COLLECTED postings on GL 1240 (credits less debits, so a reversed receipt nets out). A deposit-only or fully reversed claim is not a supplier receipt and is left out. A claim whose recorded receipts cannot be read back from the ledger (fewer posted entries than `receiptSeq`, a foreign org or currency on a line, a negative net, or the read budget) is counted in `unverifiedCount`, `complete` is false and the total is withheld, never a guessed figure.
- Currencies stay separate in every total; there is no net field.
- Completion is a separate fact from cash (PR #764 Finding 1). Each row carries a server-derived `settlement` (`COMPLETE` or `PARTIAL`); the screen no longer compares supplier cash with the obligation. A margin claim is `COMPLETE` when the claim's own record says PAID (`recordReceipt` sets it when deposit plus receipts reach the claim) and none of its receipt postings has been reversed; its `settledMinor` stays the supplier's own cash, so a 3,000 JOD claim closed by a 1,000 JOD customer deposit and a 2,000 JOD receipt is `COMPLETE` with cash 2,000, and the deposit is still never counted as a supplier receipt. A reversed receipt leaves the claim row untouched, so a claim with a reversed receipt is `PARTIAL`. A payable and a cost recovery are `COMPLETE` when what was paid or recovered reaches what is due.
- Byte-aware read (PR #764 Finding 2). A row cap bounds documents, not bytes, and Convex limits one transaction to 16 MiB (https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits), while `receiptNotes` and `paymentNotes` are unbounded strings up to the 1 MiB document limit. Every document of the three families and every receipt-provenance ledger read is admitted by the transaction's own headroom (`ctx.meta.getTransactionMetrics()`, the decision `VerificationBudget.hasHeadroom` makes, in `convex/utils/transactionReadHeadroom.ts`): the read stops when the transaction could not take one more worst-case 1 MiB document and still leave a 2 MiB reserve. One budget is shared across the families and the provenance reads. A family cut short is `complete: false` with its totals withheld; it is reported as an incomplete list, not as unconfirmed receipts. This covers records that already exist, which limiting future note lengths would not.
- An unverifiable claim counts only itself (L1). A claim whose receipts cannot be read back adds one to `unverifiedCount`, and the entries it read are spent from the shared 1,000-entry budget; older, fully posted claims are still read. Only when the entry budget or the transaction headroom is exhausted is every remaining claim that holds money counted unconfirmed.

Evidence boundary: convex-test only, with the real `openSupplierReceivable` and `recordReceipt` writers. The byte tests run with convex-test's `transactionLimits: true`, which emulates the 16 MiB ceiling; the budget arithmetic is pinned with hand-set metrics in `convex/utils/transactionReadHeadroom.test.ts`. Not proven against the Convex backend (SCRUM-762): that its metering matches the headroom figures, the cost of one `getTransactionMetrics` call per document, and that a streamed read does not read ahead past one document. `supplierStatement.outstanding` and `paymentsReconciliation` now use the same budget (next section). Not certified: receipts posted outside `recordReceipt`, cost-recovery receipts (still read from `amountRecoveredMinor`, which holds no deposit), and a legacy PAID payable's date (its `lastChangedAt` is the row's `updatedAt`).

### SCRUM-869 supplier statement queries: no read limit is ever thrown (PR #764 round 4)

Matrix row `SCRUM869-supplier-read-limits` binds seven literal tests (one scenario record each, `regression/scenarios/money/supplier-recon-*`, `supplier-outstanding-*` and `supplier-history-spent-budget-*`; failing-first commits `8c98ed31f2633a817689fe615c8eba408082cc0b` for round 4 and `c4fc1e88ec6ff2ac2c9ea8e98989e1840c669cd6` for round 5, where they were red). Owner ruling SCRUM-869#c23881 (2026-10-29). Invariant: no supplier statement query (`outstanding`, `settled`, `paymentsReconciliation`) throws on a Convex read limit (16 MiB, 32,000 documents, 4,096 index ranges per function, https://docs.convex.dev/production/state/limits). Each answers UNAVAILABLE / OVER_LIMIT or `complete: false` with its totals withheld.

- Mechanism: every list read goes through `readBoundedStream` (`convex/utils/transactionReadHeadroom.ts`), which asks `ctx.meta.getTransactionMetrics()` for room before each document (https://docs.convex.dev/database/writing-data, "Checking transaction headroom"). One shared budget, no second one. A row cap alone bounds documents, not bytes: `paymentNotes` can be ~1 MiB.
- `paymentsReconciliation`: the outbox in-flight read, the payables of all seven statuses, each payable's journal entries (shared 1,000-entry budget) and each entry's lines. Any cut is UNAVAILABLE / OVER_LIMIT with no currencies and no differences.
- `outstanding`: payables, margin receivables and cost recoveries are streamed in turn. A cut family is `complete: false`. A section that is not complete now withholds its totals (W14), as the settled section already did, so a partial sum never sits next to the flag; the row cap path changed with it.
- `settled` unverified count (LOW-2): a claim with no recorded receipt (`receiptSeq` 0) holds only the customer deposit seed. It is read while budget remains, but when the budget is gone it is skipped and never counted unverified.

Evidence boundary: convex-test with `transactionLimits: true`, which emulates the 16 MiB ceiling; not proven against the Convex backend (SCRUM-762): that its metering matches the headroom figures and the cost of one `getTransactionMetrics` call per document. The shared budget means a heavy payables family can also withhold the receivable side of `outstanding`; that is a truthful incomplete section, not a total. Round 5 closed the two reads this paragraph used to list as not covered (next section).

### SCRUM-869 supplier statement queries: every read spends the shared budget, a cut is never an absence (PR #764 round 5)

Same matrix row, `SCRUM869-supplier-read-limits`; failing-first commit `c4fc1e88ec6ff2ac2c9ea8e98989e1840c669cd6`. Invariant: a read that happens inside a supplier statement query is either charged to the one shared transaction budget or is a single schema-bounded document; and a section the budget cut is reported as incomplete with a reason, never rendered as "none".

- Row enrichment (M1): `payableFacts` and `receivableFacts` read the linked sale and finance application with `db.get`. Those reads now go through `budgetedReader`, which checks `hasReadHeadroom` before each get and throws a private `ReadBudgetExhausted`; `readRowsWithinBudget` catches it per family. The family is then incomplete with `incompleteReason: "READ_BUDGET"` and its totals withheld.
- Queued postings (M2): `paymentsReconciliation` calls the new `pendingAffectingPostingState`, which reads each queued reversal's original event one at a time after a headroom check and answers NONE, AFFECTING or OVER_LIMIT. OVER_LIMIT is `UNAVAILABLE / OVER_LIMIT`, never "nothing pending". `hasPendingAffectingPosting` is unchanged: its two other callers are report queries outside this change (follow-up, not widened here).
- Reason (M3): `section` and `settledSection` carry `incompleteReason: "ROW_LIMIT" | "READ_BUDGET"` when `complete` is false. The screen shows the empty sentence only when the section is complete, and a budget cut has its own English and Arabic note.

Class table, every db read in the three readers:

| Reader | Read | Bound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `paymentsReconciliation` | in-flight outbox events | `readBoundedStream`, shared budget |
| | payables, seven statuses | `readBoundedStream` |
| | each payable's journal entries and lines | `readBoundedStream`, 1,000-entry budget |
| | queued reversals' original events | `pendingAffectingPostingState`, headroom before each get |
| | accounts payable control account | one `unique()` document, no free-text field |
| `outstanding` | payables, margin receivables, cost recoveries | `readBoundedStream` per family |
| | linked sale and finance application per row | `budgetedReader`, headroom before each get |
| `settled` | payables, claims, recoveries | `readSettledFamily` over `readBoundedStream` |
| | claim receipts and their entries | `readBoundedStream`, 1,000-entry budget |
| | receivable-from-suppliers control account | one `unique()` document, no free-text field |

Evidence boundary: convex-test with `transactionLimits: true`; the headroom call is not proven against the Convex backend's metering (SCRUM-762). Not changed: a generic reversal of `SUPPLIER_PAYMENT_SETTLED` leaves `amountPaid` as it was, so the payable history can still say "Settled in full" (LOW, SCRUM-879).

## SCRUM-908 ACCOUNTANT commission visibility

Matrix row `SCRUM908-accountant-commission-visibility` binds `convex/accountantCommissionsAccess.scrum908.test.ts` and the ACCOUNTANT/SENIOR_ACCOUNTANT template checks in `convex/utils/permissions.test.ts` to owner ruling SCRUM-908 c24538. A newly created organization seeds ACCOUNTANT with `view:commissions` and without `manage:commissions`. A member in that stored role may call `sales.listCommissions` for its own organization and is refused for a foreign organization. Owner-only role create and update accept commission viewing alongside finance, cost and disbursement permissions. An owner may still withhold commission visibility while retaining cost and sale-completion access. Regression records are under `regression/scenarios/permission/`. Original template/ruling-change records have no `bugRef`; round-2 defect records carry the committed failing-first revision.

`convex/accountantCommissionsBackfill.scrum908.test.ts` extends this row with the coordinator-selected exact 19-permission pre-change ACCOUNTANT set, including cost access. Order is irrelevant; extra, missing, same-length substituted or duplicate permissions, renamed, deleted and system-owner roles are unchanged. The grant and `accountant-commissions-2026-11-04` marker commit in one patch. Replays do nothing, including after an owner removes commissions from a marked role. Public role saves, existing backfills and template sync preserve provenance; new organizations stamp it with the default grant. Owner-run template sync explicitly restores current template permissions, including commission viewing; its test asserts marker retention. The raw admin editor refuses provenance changes while accepting unchanged round trips and normal permission edits. One-row tenant pages preserve ownership, and the 500-row upper clamp resumes through the remaining row. The frozen-set test compares it with a literal snapshot of the pre-change template.

Round-2 review remediation adds deployment-wide completion in `roleMigrationRuns`, atomic with the final page. New public-door tests recreate ACCOUNTANT with exactly 19 permissions and rename a 19-permission Bookkeeper to accountant after completion. Later migration calls must return zero updates and leave both roles without `view:commissions`. A multi-page control checks no premature completion, one final record, unchanged roles and completion history on replay, and a terminal page even with an unusable role cursor. These three records cite red revision `bc5c19cd0ffc34741d0e7c916ec5b82a904a2dfb`, which contains their named tests. Earlier custom-extra-permission recreation coverage remains separately bound.

`scripts/accountantCommissionsRollout.test.ts` retains commissions-before-cost wiring, shared deadline, bounded cursor walking, counts-only output and malformed evidence refusal. Cost order does not affect the supported already-cost-bearing cohort. No-cost rows do not match this commission phase until the separate cost repair changes their set. The backfill remains internal.

Round-2 defect checks have failing-first revision `a59e3afdf198c00c67aa1450a84cb37552bc65d0`, which contains the named tests. The coordinator's read-only observation counted one matching production ACCOUNTANT; this lane used synthetic fixtures and did not access production. See `docs/adr/2026-11-04-accountant-commissions-backfill-runs-in-the-deploy-rollout.md` for commands, mutation controls and provenance boundaries.

Evidence boundary: serial `convex-test`, template and helper checks. This row does not certify PR #772's management-net headline, authenticated browser behavior, platform concurrency, real-backend schema deployment or production rollout. Independent seats and release verification remain pending.

| Empty or whitespace code | `ACCOUNT_CODE_INVALID`; no account is created | `empty account codes are refused without creating accounts` |

| Empty or whitespace English name on create | `ACCOUNT_NAME_REQUIRED`; no account is created | `blank account names are refused on create without creating accounts` |
| Empty or whitespace English name on custom or system account update | `ACCOUNT_NAME_REQUIRED`; the entire account row stays unchanged | `custom and system accounts refuse blank names on update without changing the row` |
| Valid padded English name | Create stores `Partner Payables`; update stores `Service Provider Payables`, without surrounding spaces | `valid account names are stored trimmed on create and update` |

At `d7a759ad18851b53669220fdb040884cdfaf82ea`, the duplicate-code test fails
because the refusal has no code. The other five checks already pass. Their
scenario records are regressions, not new defects, and carry no invented
`failingFirst` SHA. No opening policy, posting path, or pilot switch changes.
The role table later becomes one concrete test registration so the regression
runner can select its exact name; each of the same three role cases still runs.
This is serial convex-test evidence, not cloud runtime or concurrency evidence.
The coordinator verifies rendered screens and obtains the independent review seats.

Round 3 reproduces invalid account identity at `cee22464d`: 15 failing cases and
four passing existing checks before the fix. The same cases are grouped under
concrete test titles for scenario-runner selection. The duplicate scenario now
includes canonical variants; the five identity scenarios cite that red commit.
Scenario inputs and expected names use `unicodeCodePoints` arrays for literal
strings that the library's space-free token rules cannot represent directly.
Removing only the normalization call caused six failures before restoration.
Repeating that mutation after grouping caused both selected tests to fail
(canonical storage and duplicate refusal); the source was restored byte-for-byte.
The component suite checks both coded refusals in English and Arabic, including
name refusal on update, without displaying technical error text.

Codes normalize rather than reject Arabic keyboard digits so identifiers have
one stored form. The mapping uses Unicode's [Arabic-Indic U+0660–U+0669 and
Eastern Arabic-Indic U+06F0–U+06F9 ranges](https://www.unicode.org/charts/PDF/U0600.pdf).
Expected refusals use [Convex application errors](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/error-handling/application-errors)
with structured `data.code`. Early returns replace nested conditional rendering,
as recommended by [Sonar's nested-ternary guidance](https://www.sonarsource.com/blog/stop-nesting-ternaries-javascript/).
Status messages use native [HTML output](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-elements.html#the-output-element)
and retain advisory [WAI-ARIA status semantics](https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#status),
following [Sonar S6819's preference for native elements](https://github.com/SonarSource/SonarJS/blob/master/packages/analysis/src/jsts/rules/README.md).

The implementation keeps authorization and validators at the existing backend
entry points, following [Convex authorization](https://docs.convex.dev/auth/overview)
and [validation guidance](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/validation). The UI
uses the repository's shared components and SupplierCreateDialog mounting pattern,
with labelled dialogs and native table semantics, following
[WAI-ARIA dialogs](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/),
[WAI-ARIA tables](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/table/), and
[shadcn Dialog](https://ui.shadcn.com/docs/components/radix/dialog).

### SCRUM-932 round 4: account code alphabet and optional Arabic names

Red commit: `c08c8f39959b92446313fa5bfcfe9e865bf2ef33`. It contains only
the management test additions. All ten new bug records below bind unchanged
test names registered in that commit. The four existing empty/canonical-code
and blank-English-name records are untouched in this round.

The repeated mistake class is validating a few spellings rather than defining
the identifier alphabet. Round 3 (`bebc7cb61`, followed by `b49071337`)
canonicalized Arabic digits and boundary spaces; round 4's red run reproduced
the same near-duplicate class through format controls and internal whitespace.
The structural correction is one admission rule in public `create`, immediately
after normalization: `^[A-Za-z0-9.-]+$`. Empty strings and every other character
refuse with the existing `ACCOUNT_CODE_INVALID` payload and existing EN/AR keys.
No new length cap is imposed: the compatibility inventory does not establish a
business limit. Convex's existing value/document limits still apply. Case remains
significant. Stored rows and other `by_org_code` readers are unchanged.

The decision follows [OWASP's allowlist validation guidance](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Input_Validation_Cheat_Sheet.html).
[Unicode's character database](https://www.unicode.org/Public/UCD/latest/ucd/UnicodeData.txt)
classifies U+200B through U+200F and U+061C as Cf, format controls.
Rejecting outside a defined alphabet covers this class without a character blacklist.
[Sonar's rule mapping](https://github.com/SonarSource/SonarJS/blob/master/packages/analysis/src/jsts/rules/README.md)
maps S7758 to [prefer-code-point](https://github.com/sindresorhus/eslint-plugin-unicorn/blob/main/docs/rules/prefer-code-point.md).
The digit replacement now uses indexOf on the two ten-digit strings, requiring
neither charCodeAt nor a non-null assertion. Expected refusals retain structured
[ConvexError application errors](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/error-handling/application-errors);
an uncaught error rolls back the mutation. Arabic names are trimmed at both
public write doors; blank input removes the optional field. Omitting nameAr
on update preserves the stored name.

#### Compatibility inventory

Scope: tracked TS/TSX/JS/MJS source at the supplied round-3 baseline, including
code properties in files referencing chartOfAccounts/defaultChart, every public
create call and direct chart insert, and manually resolved shorthand, spread,
rehearsal-helper and generated-code inputs. The literal inventory is a conservative
superset of account codes; error-code properties and keyboard/currency codes
were excluded. This is a repository compatibility check, not a production-data census.

The default chart is imported from `convex/utils/defaultChart.ts`; that file
has no imports and defines all 57 default rows plus the system-key identifiers.
System-key strings such as CASH_ON_HAND identify mappings, not stored account codes.
initialize, ensureSystemAccount, general-expense/supplier-payable creation and
e2eBootstrap seed from those definitions. Copy-based bank/cheque fixtures inherit
their existing default row codes.

### SCRUM-932 round 6: custom account normal balances

Owner ruling 2026-11-08, SCRUM-932 c24767/c24768: a custom account created
through public `chartOfAccounts.create` has its type's normal balance. The
eight-type mapping is shared with the dialog and keyed by the schema-derived
`Doc<"chartOfAccounts">["type"]`, so adding a schema type requires a mapping.
The backend checks it after tenant authorization and the accounting feature
check, before writes. Create derives the displayed balance; edit displays the
stored balance. Existing rows, update, initialization, repair and system
definitions retain their behavior, including the two contra defaults among
57 definitions: 1510 Accumulated Depreciation (ASSET/CREDIT) and 3300 Partner
Drawings (EQUITY/DEBIT).

| Matrix row | Scenario | Expected | Executable check |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SCRUM932-coa-normal-balance` | `coa-normal-balance-mismatch-refused`: create LIABILITY/DEBIT after a valid 2500 Partner Payables LIABILITY/CREDIT | `NORMAL_BALANCE_MISMATCH`; row count remains 1 and all rows are unchanged. The sibling ASSET/CREDIT case also refuses; the literal table of all eight correct pairs succeeds. | `convex/chartOfAccounts.management.test.ts`: `create LIABILITY with DEBIT is refused without writing accounts`; sibling `create ASSET with CREDIT is refused without writing accounts`; positive control `every account type creates with its normal balance` |

Initial backend red commit `dc9795d09953f9619f01ba0a6e23d414adf27e29`
ran against unchanged `e5b311c9822eb16a1abbde846d1798c5f23aef4b` backend
source: 2 failed, 21 passed; both mismatches resolved instead of rejecting.
The scenario runner requires concrete registrations. Test-only commit
`badb0817ee409c7a4de87b85b3688e7668f3db9b` retains the same assertions
under literal test names; the same baseline-source replay produced 2 failed,
21 passed, exit 1. The record binds its exact LIABILITY test name and that
red-replayed commit. Backend source was restored afterward.

Component red commit `b7957f8a7aa0a3fda60188eee645d8126011b959` ran
against the unchanged baseline dialog: 1 failed, 29 passed. Choosing LIABILITY
sent DEBIT instead of CREDIT; the EXPENSE/DEBIT control passed. The UI fix
also removes the old duplicate-code test's manual balance-selection steps;
every existing assertion is retained.

The type/balance decision follows the normal-balance table in
[OpenStax Principles of Accounting, section 3.1](https://openstax.org/books/principles-financial-accounting/pages/3-1-describe-principles-assumptions-and-concepts-of-accounting-and-their-relationship-to-financial-statements).
COGS and other expenses follow expense balances; other income follows revenue.
Contra creation is excluded by the owner ruling. Structured refusal follows
[Convex application errors](https://docs.convex.dev/functions/error-handling/application-errors),
using an uncaught `ConvexError` with a code and safe English message.
Deriving the create balance during render follows
[React's guidance on avoiding redundant state](https://react.dev/learn/choosing-the-state-structure#avoid-redundant-state).

The safety boundary is the public custom-create door. No journal amounts,
posting rules, cancellation/reversal paths, historical rows or audit conventions
change. The five predicted failures checked locally are reversed debit/credit
mapping, an omitted type, stale UI balance after a type change, reclassification
of a stored contra balance on edit, and a refusal that writes an account.
The all-types control, schema-derived exhaustive mapping, component interaction
checks, stored-contra edit check and before/after document comparison address
those respectively. Harness results do not establish cloud runtime behavior,
OCC, browser layout or production-data parity. Independent review and release
evidence remain the coordinator's responsibility.

Local round-6 checks: all 13 backend caller files plus the component file
passed (581 tests across completed runs, one Vitest worker). An earlier combined
run was interrupted without a verdict; the smaller completed groups cover the
same requested files. The six executable scenario-validator files passed all
374 tests across the initial run and the corrected-record rerun, including the
70-test live-library validator. The new record uses the existing
`unicodeCodePoints` name format; its initial prose name was rejected and fixed.
`refreshRulings.test.ts` remains unavailable: Vite/Rolldown rejects the imported

(`16f58b33880c3743fd6f244b6bbe05f328b41e9e`); native `node --check` passes.
No runner or gate was changed to hide that failure.

ESLint passed on all six touched TS/TSX files. `pnpm typecheck:convex` passed

## SCRUM-850-opening-audit-confidentiality

S850-R6-1 / S850-R5-1 / SCRUM-902 c24475: a finance-only audit reader must not
receive any monetary figure from a pending opening draft, including totals or
successive-edit deltas. ADR: `docs/adr/2026-11-08-opening-balance-edit-void-correct.md`, rounds 6–7.
Failing-first commit `b4250b0c7c822bc05ca99504984b107e050d2662` contains these
literal tests in `convex/openingBalanceMaintenance.scrum850.test.ts`; all five
fail there on disclosed amounts. Records are in the permission and money
scenario folders. This is serial public-function harness evidence, not cloud
or release certification. Historical audit rows are not repaired by this change.

| Scenario | Literal backend test |
| --- | --- |
| `opening-edit-audit-singleton-employee` | `SCRUM-850 R7 singleton employee advance edit audit withholds pending amounts` |
| `opening-edit-audit-singleton-vehicle` | `SCRUM-850 R7 singleton vehicle inventory edit audit withholds pending amounts` |
| `opening-edit-audit-sensitive-content` | `SCRUM-850 R7 mixed draft edit audit contains metadata without protected amounts or totals` |
| `opening-edit-audit-summary-controls` | `SCRUM-850 R7 amount-only successive edits reveal versions and counts without amount deltas` |
| `opening-edit-audit-unsafe-history` | `SCRUM-850 R7 correcting an unsafe retained draft omits historical and replacement audit amounts` |

Singleton controls prove dedicated stock/GL reader refusal, withheld draft
content, audit absence before editing, and one edit row afterwards through all
four audit-reader branches. Employee pendingDraftId is withheld; stock cost
permission is false in both cases. Each snapshot retains versions, counts,
kinds, date and currency. The after snapshot retains memoChanged. No journal posts.
The mixed case preserves the id and free-text exclusions and rejects aggregates.
Two edits changing only cash amounts require summaries differing only in version,
with no monetary key or amount delta. Unsafe retained history is still correctable,
without a null total field or replacement amount in the audit. Separate source
mutants reintroducing totalDebits in before and after must each fail these tests.


### SCRUM-936-audit-reader-confidentiality

Financial audit copies inherit the dedicated source reader's permission. Run
`convex/financialAudit.scrum936.test.ts`. Twelve literal scenario tests are
registered in `regression/scenarios/money/scrum936-*.scenario.json`, with
failing-first commit `e5813b68f46b63546c3b448900efec0efa0241e9` (13 failed,
including classification completeness). All four audit branches, both opening
posting doors, singleton vehicle and employee openings, retained full-draft and
total-only edits, posted manual journal history, commission add/remove/set,
retained migration COGS, and supplier cost receipt/reversal payloads are covered.
Each negative audit assertion has a source-reader refusal/withholding control
and a full-row owner control. Explicit non-owner grants also restore full rows.
Cost-only employee controls remain withheld under the opening payroll rule.
Pending manual-journal drafts preserve their existing manage-finance audience.
The stored rows remain unchanged. Classification is exhaustive over the schema.

This is serial convex-test evidence. It is not platform, cloud, concurrency,
production-data, independent-seat or pilot-release certification. The manual
posting pilot switch remains blocked. Historical supplier and migration rows
are inserted as fixtures, not emitted by executing their internal or disabled
writers. The complete writer census and final checks are recorded in
`docs/architecture/scrum936-audit-reader-evidence.md`.


SCRUM-936 supplemental controls at
`18992de610caea44ce5503f2362bfc27b36f5225` fail all 15 tests against that
checkpoint's production sources. Two additional records cover posted-event line
counts and unwind-actor remittance parity. The primary tests also cover protected
source ids, mutable-draft payroll history and the stricter maintenance payroll
predicate. Restricted resource types remain visible; source ids are `WITHHELD`.


The initial SCRUM-936 library contains fourteen records for fourteen behavioral
tests. The legacy-edit and direct-employee records include the historical-employee and
void-reason controls from the supplemental checkpoint. The initial eighteen source mutants produced failed tests
and were restored clean. Both typechecks and the source-freshness check passed at that checkpoint.
The unrelated refreshRulings parse failure and thermally interrupted broad
SCRUM-858 read-budget suite remain failed/incomplete verification, not waivers.

Three further records cover the application base-reader gate and real public
cheque writers. `c941eba60f4c1125e78775b329702a50d5cd57ef` fails the application
gate test (1 failed, 15 passed). `9956ef3e01b055416dd920bce89aa85210949c8f`
fails the literal cheque-face-reader and expected-payment-reader tests (2 failed,
16 passed). The final library has seventeen behavioral records and one separate
schema classification test. Cheque attestation retains the finance-only reader's
existing full visibility; expected-payment method/date follow view:sales while
cheque ids and the stored cancellation reason follow view:finance. No new policy
is inferred from the writer module. The final source checkpoint
`26fe73f57f621a571097d4c75c9cc526b4d0b677` passed 307 tests in seven focused files.
The final-head proof and repeated mutation results are in the evidence report.
All nineteen final-source mutants failed tests and were restored clean. The
restored four-file proof passed 137 tests, and the two required guards passed 32.

Round 2 adds five literal regression checks to the same backend suite and five
records, bringing the SCRUM-936 library to twenty-two scenarios plus the schema
classification check. The request-reuse case fails at
`e934f16d1` with 8,200 line-account gets for a 200-row synthetic page. It requires
zero such gets at that checkpoint, two org-scoped control lookups, at most two draft
gets, correct visibility for both drafts, and fresh decisions on a later request.
The four public supplier-writer cases fail at `0d219ab0b` because authorized
audit rows lack structured payment method, supplier snapshot name, and operation
amount. Each now checks restored authorized evidence, refused dedicated-reader
controls, all audit branches, amount-free descriptions, and unchanged stored rows.
Full failing-first SHAs are stored in the records. Earlier scenarios are retained.
The maintenance payroll rule and unwind actor predicate retain their permissions.
See the round 2 evidence appendix for checks and explicit execution limits.

Round 3 retains those twenty-two scenarios and adds ten literal checks: active
and inactive duplicates of both payroll control keys, owner and non-owner plan
lifecycles, the accounting-reader entitlement census, missing and foreign draft
evidence, and the sales-only SET_COMMISSION_AMOUNT positive control. The duplicate
regressions fail first at `c928ff01c`; the entitlement regressions fail first at
`896249060`. The three Seat 1 coverage controls change no production behavior.
Their corresponding source mutations fail the tests.

The request-reuse scenario now follows the actual referenced account ids rather
than assuming unique system keys. Its mixed 200-row page requires three account
gets, one per distinct account, zero control-index lookups, at most two draft
gets and one subscription lookup. A later request reads fresh state. The
duplicate cases reference the non-canonical account, preserve inactive controls,
withhold protected draft and journal copies, and keep ordinary/payroll-authorized
positive controls. The page-size and draft-read bounds, feature gates and grants
are unchanged. Account reads are now bounded by distinct referenced ids.

The plan cases keep event metadata and counts while withholding protected
accounting details for expired, cancelled, past-due, free and absent subscription
states, including for owners. Active and restored plans retain full rows. An
active subscription with an old period end follows stored status. Independent
opening, manual-draft, supplier, sales, commission, application, bank and sign-off
families remain readable under their existing grants. The action census tests
both owner and explicitly granted non-owner readers. This matrix row remains a
local backend permission oracle, not cloud or release certification.

| --- | --- | --- |
| Opening supplier identity | 100003 JOD minor units, MASTER_A, master name and version 3; approval after rename captures version 4 | `convex/openingSupplierIdentity.scrum868.test.ts`, direct master-snapshot cases and approval rename |
| Identity admission and provenance | Missing/name-only, foreign, deleted and inactive new use refuse without state changes; different and one-sided ids do not match | Same suite, 36 literal preparation/review refusals and both basis cases |
| Settlement and history | Id-less supplier openings refuse; ordinary creditor settles 100003; identified partial payment is 40001 and legacy partial 10001 refuses | Same suite, legacy, ordinary-creditor, inactive-retry and partial/dispute cases |
| Pending review | Unbound approval refuses, edit increments version, stale version 0 refuses and version 1 posts | Same suite, pending legacy draft case |

Run `convex/openingSupplierIdentity.scrum868.test.ts` through convex-test public
mutations and queries. The 52 backend scenarios are registered under
`regression/scenarios/money/opening-supplier-p5-*.scenario.json` and bind to the
actual registered case names. Supplier openings use 100003 JOD minor units;
the receipt and partial-payment inputs are 40001, and the refused legacy partial
input is 10001. These are synthetic independent literals, not business data.

Create, edit, approve and direct posting require a same-org active supplier for
SUPPLIER_PAYABLE and SUPPLIER_RECOVERABLE. Identified OTHER_PAYABLE records use
the same master snapshot; ordinary creditors remain id-less. Id/name/version
come from the master, never the supplied label/version. Different or one-sided
ids cannot satisfy opening provenance. Legacy reads preserve stored content;
new supplier settlement without an id refuses, while inactive identified
obligations remain serviceable and retries change no state. Pending edits keep
reviewed-version binding. SCRUM-850 void/re-entry and audit confidentiality
controls remain covered by their existing suites.

The pending-legacy-draft scenario cites failing-first commit
`16d16280cbe2c02cacc5605fc67077b03b6257de`, where the unbound approval resolves
instead of refusing. Other new records are preventive controls and have no
bugRef. Rulings: SCRUM-868 c23871/c23873, OQ-4, and SCRUM-850 c24749.
This is harness evidence, not real-backend, OCC, production or pilot certification.

### SCRUM868-P7-launch-evidence

| Cell | Literal expected result | Bound check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Missing identity | One missing row counts 1 for each of eleven tables; supplier expense missing payee and bearer counts 1 in each independent reason | `convex/supplierLaunchEvidence.test.ts`, eleven literal table cases |
| Complete identified data | All eleven tables report 0; full before/after document arrays equal | Same suite, fully identified tenant |
| Exemptions | Ordinary OPENING OTHER_PAYABLE and SHOWROOM vendor text count 0; supplier opening counts 1; metadata-only pending update inherits id | Same suite, ordinary creditor and partial-edit cases |
| Partial and unreadable evidence | Nine conversion rows require two pages, missing counts 7 then 1; native byte split, unreadable cursor and missing UPDATE source return UNAVAILABLE without counts | Same suite, paging, split, cursor and pending-edit cases |
| Release refusal | Any count 1 refuses with SUPPLIER_IDENTITY_UNRESOLVED; failed call, malformed response, deadline and page cap return SUPPLIER_EVIDENCE_UNAVAILABLE | `scripts/releaseGuard.supplierLaunch.test.ts`; actual rollout phase executed with failing CLI responses |
| Browser identity | EN and AR/RTL: opening 100003 JOD minor units selects MASTER_A, consigned vehicle cost 80 and selling price 100 selects the same party with zero journal change, payment 100.003 retains MASTER_A after reload | `playwright/tests/supplier-identity.spec.ts`, both literal journeys |

The eleven-table census adds openingPayablePayments to design section 4.1.
Recovery receipts and opening payments prove identity through their same-org
parents. OWNED ON_ACCOUNT creditor identity remains on the acquisition payable.
The query is internal and read-only; no amount, posting, settlement, opening or
correction behavior changes. Counts are complete or UNAVAILABLE. See
`docs/adr/2026-11-10-supplier-launch-evidence.md` for limits and exclusions.

P7 also registers thirteen previously unbound P2-P4b controls against the existing
matrix rows: P2 update-by-id, unchanged-name echo, role conflict and on-account
creditor; P3 concession basis; P4a inactive completion and receipt refusal; P4b
bearer spoofing, legacy notes-only edit, SHOWROOM free text, pending bearer
switch, inactive/stale parties and shared economic confirmation. P5's 52 literal
backend controls already have registrations. These are preventive controls
without bugRef. Scenario records use the repository fingerprint function.

Local harness and script results do not certify the browser rows. The journeys
require the disposable runner-local backend, block cloud Convex HTTP and
nonlocal WebSockets, and preserve existing authentication. Docker and QA auth
state were unavailable in the implementing worktree, so browser execution,
phone/desktop screen audit, explorer hunters, hosted Sonar, independent seats,
real-platform certification and production counts remain UNAVAILABLE here.
The trusted exact-SHA accounting release verdict remains a separate gate.
